## 文章 51. Wildfire Smoke Health Impacts

Wildfire smoke is increasingly affecting human health far from the fire sources, as climate change leads to larger, more intense fires that produce smoke that can travel thousands of kilometers. The fine particulate（微粒）matter in wildfire smoke, known as PM2.5, poses（造成）serious health risks that extend well beyond the immediate fire zones.

Research shows that wildfire smoke can affect air quality in cities hundreds or even thousands of kilometers from the fires. During the 2020 wildfire season in the western United States, smoke from California and Oregon fires reached the East Coast, affecting air quality in cities like New York and Boston. Similar events have occurred in Australia, where smoke from bushfires（丛林大火）traveled across the Pacific Ocean to South America. These long-range transport events expose（使暴露于）millions of people to unhealthy air quality who would not normally be affected by local fires.

The health effects of wildfire smoke are severe（严重的）and well-documented. Fine particles can penetrate（穿透）deep into the lungs and enter the bloodstream（血流）, causing respiratory（呼吸的）and cardiovascular（心血管的）problems. Studies have linked wildfire smoke exposure to increased hospitalizations（住院）for asthma（哮喘）, chronic（慢性的）obstructive（阻塞的）pulmonary（肺的）disease, heart attacks, and strokes（中风）. Vulnerable（易受伤的）populations including children, the elderly, pregnant women, and people with pre-existing conditions are at greatest risk. Recent research has also shown that wildfire smoke can affect mental health, increasing anxiety（焦虑）and depression（抑郁）, and can impair（损害）cognitive（认知的）function.

The economic costs are substantial（巨大的）. Healthcare costs increase due to additional hospitalizations and emergency room visits. Worker productivity（生产力）declines as people are unable to work due to health problems or must stay home to care for affected family members. Agricultural losses occur when smoke affects crop growth and harvest（收获）operations. Tourism and outdoor recreation（娱乐）suffer when air quality is poor. The research indicates that the total economic costs of wildfire smoke exposure in the United States alone exceed $10 billion annually（每年）.

Addressing this growing health threat requires action on multiple fronts. Reducing greenhouse gas emissions can slow the climate change that is making wildfires worse. Improving fire management through prescribed（规定的）burns and forest thinning（间伐）can reduce fuel loads and fire intensity（强度）. Public health systems need to be prepared for smoke events, with early warning systems, air quality monitoring, and guidance for vulnerable populations. Urban planning can incorporate（纳入）air filtration（过滤）systems in public buildings and schools. The research underscores（强调）that wildfire smoke is not just a local problem but a regional and even global health challenge that requires comprehensive（全面的）solutions.

**Questions:**

1. What is the passage mainly about?
   A) Wildfire smoke health impacts far from fire sources
   B) The history of fire fighting
   C) The benefits of wildfire smoke
   D) Methods for starting wildfires

2. How far can wildfire smoke travel?
   A) Hundreds or thousands of kilometers
   B) Only within the fire zone
   C) Only a few kilometers
   D) It cannot travel

3. What health problems can wildfire smoke cause?
   A) No health problems
   B) Only skin problems
   C) Respiratory and cardiovascular problems, and mental health effects
   D) Only eye irritation

4. What are the economic costs of wildfire smoke?
   A) Over $10 billion annually in the United States
   B) No economic costs
   C) Only a few million dollars
   D) Only affects fire fighters

5. What is required to address wildfire smoke?
   A) Only evacuating people
   B) Ignoring the problem
   C) Only building more hospitals
   D) Action on multiple fronts including reducing emissions and improving fire management

**Answers:**
1. A - 文章主要讲述野火烟雾对远离火源的健康影响
2. A - 数百或数千公里
3. C - 呼吸和心血管问题,以及心理健康影响
4. A - 仅在美国每年超过100亿美元
5. D - 多方面的行动,包括减少排放和改善火灾管理

### 翻译

**文章 51. 野火烟雾对健康的影响**

野火烟雾正日益影响着远离火源的人类健康,因为气候变化导致更大、更强烈的火灾产生的烟雾可以传播数千公里。野火烟雾中的细颗粒物(称为PM2.5)造成的健康风险远远超出 immediate 火区范围。

研究表明,野火烟雾会影响距离火灾数百甚至数千公里城市的空气质量。在2020年美国西部的野火季,加利福尼亚州和俄勒冈州火灾的烟雾到达了东海岸,影响了纽约和波士顿等城市的空气质量。类似事件也发生在澳大利亚,丛林大火的烟雾穿越太平洋到达南美洲。这些长距离传输事件使数百万人暴露在不健康的空气质量中,而这些人群通常不会受到当地火灾的影响。

野火烟雾对健康的影响是严重且有充分记录的。细颗粒可以深入肺部并进入血流,导致呼吸系统和心血管问题。研究已将野火烟雾暴露与哮喘、慢性阻塞性肺病、心脏病发作和中风的住院率增加联系起来。儿童、老年人、孕妇和患有既往疾病的人等易感人群面临最大风险。最近的研究还表明,野火烟雾会影响心理健康,增加焦虑和抑郁,并会损害认知功能。

经济损失是巨大的。由于额外的住院和急诊就诊,医疗成本增加。由于健康问题无法工作或必须留在家中照顾受影响的家庭成员,工人生产力下降。当烟雾影响作物生长和收获作业时,会发生农业损失。当空气质量差时,旅游业和户外娱乐活动受到影响。研究表明,仅在美国,野火烟雾暴露的总经济成本每年就超过100亿美元。

应对这一日益增长的健康威胁需要多方面的行动。减少温室气体排放可以减缓使野火恶化的气候变化。通过规定 burns 和森林间伐改善火灾管理可以减少燃料负荷和火灾强度。公共卫生系统需要为烟雾事件做好准备,建立预警系统、空气质量监测和为易感人群提供指导。城市规划可以将空气过滤系统纳入公共建筑和学校。研究强调,野火烟雾不仅仅是一个局部问题,而是一个需要全面解决方案的区域甚至全球健康挑战。

**题目翻译:**

1. 文章主要讲什么?
   A) 远离火源的野火烟雾健康影响
   B) 消防历史
   C) 野火烟雾的好处
   D) 引发野火的方法

2. 野火烟雾能传播多远?
   A) 数百或数千公里
   B) 仅在火区内
   C) 仅几公里
   D) 无法传播

3. 野火烟雾会导致什么健康问题?
   A) 没有健康问题
   B) 仅皮肤问题
   C) 呼吸和心血管问题,以及心理健康影响
   D) 仅眼睛刺激

4. 野火烟雾的经济成本是多少?
   A) 美国每年超过100亿美元
   B) 没有经济成本
   C) 仅几百万美元
   D) 仅影响消防员

5. 应对野火烟雾需要什么?
   A) 仅疏散人群
   B) 忽视问题
   C) 仅建造更多医院
   D) 多方面的行动,包括减少排放和改善火灾管理

## 文章 52. Soil Erosion Threatens Food Security

Soil erosion（土壤侵蚀）is threatening global food security at an alarming rate, with the world losing approximately 24 billion tons of fertile（肥沃的）soil annually due to wind and water erosion. This loss is equivalent to about 3% of the world's agricultural land being degraded（退化的）each year, undermining（破坏）the ability to feed a growing global population that is projected to reach 10 billion by 2050.

The causes of soil erosion are primarily related to unsustainable（不可持续的）agricultural practices. Deforestation（森林砍伐）removes vegetation（植被）that holds soil in place. Overgrazing（过度放牧）by livestock（牲畜）strips protective plant cover. Intensive（集约的）tillage（耕作）breaks up soil structure, making it vulnerable（易受伤害的）to erosion by wind and water. Monoculture（单一种植）farming depletes（耗尽）soil organic matter and reduces the root systems that stabilize（稳定）soil. Climate change exacerbates（加剧）the problem through more intense rainfall events and stronger winds, both of which increase erosion rates.

The consequences are severe and far-reaching（深远的）. As topsoil（表土）is lost, soil fertility（肥力）declines, requiring increasing amounts of fertilizer to maintain crop yields. This creates a vicious（恶性的）cycle where farmers apply more chemicals, which can further degrade soil health and pollute waterways（水道）. In severe cases, erosion leads to complete land degradation, rendering（使成为）areas unsuitable for agriculture. The research indicates that if current erosion rates continue, we may only have about 60 years of topsoil left globally, a terrifying（可怕的）prospect（前景）for food security.

The economic costs are substantial. Soil erosion reduces agricultural productivity, increasing food prices and threatening the livelihoods（生计）of the 2.5 billion people who depend directly on agriculture. The cost of replacing lost nutrients with fertilizers exceeds $40 billion annually worldwide. Infrastructure（基础设施）damage from sediment（沉积物）runoff（径流）, including silted（淤积的）reservoirs（水库）and clogged（堵塞的）drainage（排水）systems, adds billions more in costs.

Solutions exist to reduce soil erosion. Conservation（保护的）tillage, which minimizes soil disturbance（干扰）, can reduce erosion by up to 90%. Cover crops protect soil during fallow（休耕的）periods. Terracing（梯田）and contour farming（等高耕作）slow water flow on slopes. Agroforestry（农林复合）integrates trees with crops to stabilize soil. The research shows that implementing（实施）these practices can rebuild soil health and increase agricultural productivity while reducing environmental impacts. However, widespread（广泛的）adoption（采用）requires changes in farming practices, policy support, and investment in soil conservation. The research underscores that soil is a non-renewable resource on human timescales, and protecting it is essential for long-term food security.

**Questions:**

1. What is the central argument of the passage?
   A) Soil erosion is threatening global food security
   B) Soil erosion only affects urban areas
   C) Soil erosion is beneficial for agriculture
   D) Soil erosion has no impact on food security

2. How much fertile soil is lost annually?
   A) 1 trillion tons
   B) 1 billion tons
   C) 100 billion tons
   D) 24 billion tons

3. What are the main causes of soil erosion?
   A) Unsustainable agricultural practices including deforestation, overgrazing, and intensive tillage
   B) Only climate change
   C) Only natural disasters
   D) Only urbanization

4. What happens as topsoil is lost?
   A) Soil fertility increases
   B) No change in soil quality
   C) Soil fertility declines, requiring more fertilizer
   D) Only affects urban areas

5. What is required to protect soil?
   A) Ignoring the problem
   B) Only removing all vegetation
   C) Only increasing fertilizer use
   D) Changes in farming practices, policy support, and investment in soil conservation

**Answers:**
1. A - 文章的核心论点是土壤侵蚀威胁全球粮食安全
2. D - 每年损失约240亿吨肥沃土壤
3. A - 不可持续的农业实践,包括森林砍伐、过度放牧和集约化耕作
4. C - 土壤肥力下降,需要更多肥料
5. D - 改变农业实践、政策支持和投资土壤保护

### 翻译

**文章 52. 土壤侵蚀威胁粮食安全**

土壤侵蚀正以惊人的速度威胁全球粮食安全,世界每年因风蚀和水蚀损失约240亿吨肥沃土壤。这种损失相当于全球约3%的农业用地每年退化,破坏了养活预计到2050年将达到100亿的不断增长的全球人口的能力。

土壤侵蚀的原因主要与不可持续的农业实践有关。森林砍伐移除了固定土壤的植被。牲畜过度放牧剥去了保护性植物覆盖。集约化耕作破坏了土壤结构,使其容易受到风蚀和水蚀。单一种植农业耗尽了土壤有机质,减少了稳定土壤的根系。气候变化通过更强烈的降雨事件和更强的风加剧了这个问题,这两者都增加了侵蚀率。

后果是严重且深远的。随着表土的流失,土壤肥力下降,需要越来越多的肥料来维持作物产量。这创造了一个恶性循环,农民施用更多化学品,这可能进一步退化土壤健康并污染水道。在严重的情况下,侵蚀导致完全的土地退化,使地区不适合农业。研究表明,如果当前的侵蚀率持续下去,全球可能只剩下约60年的表土,这对粮食安全来说是一个可怕的前景。

经济损失是巨大的。土壤侵蚀降低了农业生产力,增加了食品价格,威胁着直接依赖农业的25亿人的生计。用肥料替代流失养分的成本每年全球超过400亿美元。沉积物径流造成的基础设施损坏,包括淤积的水库和堵塞的排水系统,增加了数十亿美元的成本。

存在减少土壤侵蚀的解决方案。最小化土壤干扰的保护性耕作可以将侵蚀减少高达90%。覆盖作物在休耕期保护土壤。梯田和等高耕作减缓了坡地上的水流。农林复合经营将树木与作物结合以稳定土壤。研究表明,实施这些做法可以重建土壤健康并提高农业生产力,同时减少环境影响。然而,广泛采用需要改变农业实践、政策支持和投资土壤保护。研究强调,土壤在人类时间尺度上是一种不可再生资源,保护它对长期粮食安全至关重要。

**题目翻译:**

1. 文章的核心论点是什么?
   A) 土壤侵蚀威胁全球粮食安全
   B) 土壤侵蚀仅影响城市地区
   C) 土壤侵蚀对农业有益
   D) 土壤侵蚀对粮食安全没有影响

2. 每年损失多少肥沃土壤?
   A) 1万亿吨
   B) 10亿吨
   C) 1000亿吨
   D) 240亿吨

3. 土壤侵蚀的主要原因是什么?
   A) 不可持续的农业实践,包括森林砍伐、过度放牧和集约化耕作
   B) 仅气候变化
   C) 仅自然灾害
   D) 仅城市化

4. 随着表土流失会发生什么?
   A) 土壤肥力增加
   B) 土壤质量没有变化
   C) 土壤肥力下降,需要更多肥料
   D) 仅影响城市地区

5. 保护土壤需要什么?
   A) 忽视问题
   B) 仅移除所有植被
   C) 仅增加肥料使用
   D) 改变农业实践、政策支持和投资土壤保护

## 文章 53. Arctic Warming Amplifies Climate Change

Arctic warming is amplifying（放大）global climate change through multiple feedback mechanisms（反馈机制）that accelerate the rate of temperature increase. The Arctic is warming two to three times faster than the global average, a phenomenon（现象）known as Arctic amplification, and this rapid warming has consequences that extend far beyond the polar（极地的）region.

The primary driver of Arctic amplification is the ice-albedo（冰-反照率）feedback. As sea ice and snow cover decline due to warming, they expose darker ocean and land surfaces that absorb more solar radiation（太阳辐射）rather than reflecting it back to space. This additional heat absorption causes further warming, which causes more ice to melt, creating a self-reinforcing（自我强化的）cycle. Research shows that this feedback alone accounts for approximately 40% of the amplified warming in the Arctic. Other feedbacks include the release of heat from the ocean as ice thins, changes in cloud cover, and increased water vapor（水蒸气）in the atmosphere, which is itself a greenhouse gas.

The consequences of Arctic warming extend globally through atmospheric（大气的）and oceanic circulation（环流）patterns. The jet stream（急流）, which is driven by the temperature difference between the poles and the equator（赤道）, becomes weaker and more meandering（蜿蜒的）as the Arctic warms. This leads to more persistent（持续的）weather patterns, with heat waves, cold spells（寒潮）, droughts（干旱）, and floods lasting longer than in the past. Research has linked Arctic warming to extreme weather events in mid-latitude（中纬度）regions including North America, Europe, and Asia.

Arctic warming also affects ocean circulation. The influx of fresh water from melting ice can slow down the Atlantic Meridional Overturning Circulation (AMOC), which distributes heat around the North Atlantic. A slowdown of this circulation could lead to cooler temperatures in Europe while warming continues elsewhere, creating complex climate impacts. The research indicates that AMOC is already at its weakest point in over 1,000 years, partly due to Arctic freshwater input.

The global implications（影响）are severe. Sea level rise from melting Greenland ice threatens coastal cities worldwide. Thawing（融化）permafrost（永久冻土）releases methane（甲烷）and carbon dioxide, accelerating global warming. Changes in Arctic ecosystems affect global biodiversity（生物多样性）and indigenous（土著的）communities. The research underscores that Arctic warming is not just a regional issue but a global amplifier（放大器）of climate change. Reducing greenhouse gas emissions is essential to slow Arctic warming and prevent the most severe consequences for the planet.

**Questions:**

1. What is the author's primary purpose in writing this passage?
   A) To explain how to increase Arctic ice
   B) To report on Arctic warming amplifying global climate change
   C) To argue against climate action
   D) To promote Arctic tourism

2. How much faster is the Arctic warming compared to the global average?
   A) Ten times faster
   B) Two to three times faster
   C) The same rate
   D) Slower

3. What is the primary driver of Arctic amplification?
   A) Ocean currents
   B) The ice-albedo feedback
   C) Increased solar radiation
   D) Volcanic activity

4. How does Arctic warming affect the jet stream?
   A) No effect
   B) It makes it weaker and more meandering
   C) It stops completely
   D) It makes it stronger

5. What is essential to slow Arctic warming?
   A) Reducing greenhouse gas emissions
   B) Removing all ice
   C) Increasing greenhouse gas emissions
   D) Ignoring the problem

**Answers:**
1. B - 作者的主要目的是报道北极变暖放大全球气候变化
2. B - 北极变暖速度是全球平均水平的两到三倍
3. B - 冰-反照率反馈
4. B - 使其更弱、更弯曲
5. A - 减少温室气体排放

### 翻译

**文章 53. 北极变暖放大气候变化**

北极变暖正通过多种加速温度上升速率的反馈机制放大全球气候变化。北极变暖的速度是全球平均水平的两到三倍,这种现象被称为北极放大效应,这种快速变暖的影响远远超出极地地区。

北极放大效应的主要驱动因素是冰-反照率反馈。随着变暖导致海冰和积雪覆盖减少,它们暴露出更暗的海洋和陆地表面,这些表面吸收更多太阳辐射而不是将其反射回太空。这种额外的热量吸收导致进一步变暖,进而导致更多冰融化,形成一个自我强化的循环。研究表明,仅这种反馈就占北极放大变暖的约40%。其他反馈包括冰变薄时海洋释放热量、云层覆盖变化以及大气中水蒸气增加(水蒸气本身就是一种温室气体)。

北极变暖的影响通过大气和海洋环流模式扩展到全球。由极地和赤道之间温差驱动的急流,随着北极变暖变得更弱、更弯曲。这导致更持久的天气模式,热浪、寒潮、干旱和洪水持续的时间比过去更长。研究已将北极变暖与包括北美、欧洲和亚洲在内的中纬度地区的极端天气事件联系起来。

北极变暖也影响海洋环流。融冰产生的淡水流入可以减缓大西洋经向翻转环流(AMOC),该环流在北大西洋周围分配热量。这种环流的减缓可能导致欧洲温度下降,而世界其他地区继续变暖,造成复杂的气候影响。研究表明,AMOC已处于1000多年来的最弱状态,部分原因是北极淡水输入。

全球影响是严重的。格陵兰冰盖融化导致的海平面上升威胁着全球沿海城市。永久冻土融化释放甲烷和二氧化碳,加速全球变暖。北极生态系统的变化影响全球生物多样性和土著社区。研究强调,北极变暖不仅仅是一个区域问题,而是气候变化的全球放大器。减少温室气体排放对于减缓北极变暖和防止对地球造成最严重后果至关重要。

**题目翻译:**

1. 作者写这篇文章的主要目的是什么?
   A) 解释如何增加北极冰层
   B) 报道北极变暖放大全球气候变化
   C) 反对气候行动
   D) 推广北极旅游

2. 北极变暖速度比全球平均水平快多少?
   A) 快十倍
   B) 快两到三倍
   C) 相同速度
   D) 更慢

3. 北极放大效应的主要驱动因素是什么?
   A) 洋流
   B) 冰-反照率反馈
   C) 太阳辐射增加
   D) 火山活动

4. 北极变暖如何影响急流?
   A) 没有影响
   B) 使其更弱、更弯曲
   C) 完全停止
   D) 使其更强

5. 减缓北极变暖需要什么?
   A) 减少温室气体排放
   B) 移除所有冰
   C) 增加温室气体排放
   D) 忽视问题

## 文章 54. Global Phosphorus Crisis

The world is facing a global phosphorus（磷）crisis that threatens food production and ecosystem health. Phosphorus is an essential nutrient for all life, critical for plant growth and food production, but it is a finite（有限的）resource that cannot be synthesized（合成）. The current system of phosphorus use is highly inefficient, with most of the nutrient lost to the environment after a single use, creating pollution while depleting（耗尽）reserves.

Research shows that approximately 80% of phosphorus used in agriculture is lost through runoff and erosion, entering waterways where it causes eutrophication and dead zones. At the same time, phosphorus rock reserves, the primary source of fertilizer, are concentrated in just a few countries, with Morocco controlling approximately 70% of global reserves. This creates geopolitical vulnerabilities and price volatility that can affect food security worldwide. Some estimates suggest that peak phosphorus production could occur within the next few decades, after which supplies will decline.

The consequences of this crisis are twofold（双重的）. In regions with phosphorus deficiency（缺乏）, particularly in sub-Saharan Africa, crop yields are limited by lack of this essential nutrient, contributing to hunger and malnutrition（营养不良）. In regions with phosphorus excess（过剩）, particularly in developed countries with intensive agriculture, the nutrient causes severe water pollution. The Gulf of Mexico dead zone, algal blooms（藻华）in Lake Erie, and eutrophication in the Baltic Sea are all consequences of phosphorus pollution.

Solutions require transforming the phosphorus cycle from a linear to a circular system. Precision（精确的）agriculture can optimize（优化）fertilizer application, reducing losses while maintaining crop yields. Recycling phosphorus from waste streams, including sewage sludge（污水污泥）, food waste, and animal manure（粪便）, can recover this valuable nutrient. Improved wastewater treatment can capture phosphorus before it reaches waterways. The research indicates that these measures could reduce demand for mined phosphorus by 50% or more while eliminating（消除）pollution.

However, implementing these solutions requires changes in agricultural practices, waste management systems, and policy frameworks（政策框架）. The research underscores that phosphorus is a critical resource for food security that is currently being mismanaged（管理不当）. Without action, the world faces the paradox（悖论）of simultaneous（同时的）phosphorus scarcity（稀缺）and pollution, threatening both food production and ecosystem health.

**Questions:**

1. What is the passage mainly about?
   A) The benefits of phosphorus pollution
   B) The global phosphorus crisis threatening food production
   C) Methods for increasing phosphorus mining
   D) The history of phosphorus discovery

2. What percentage of phosphorus used in agriculture is lost?
   A) 20%
   B) 95%
   C) 80%
   D) 50%

3. What are the twofold consequences of the phosphorus crisis?
   A) Only pollution
   B) Phosphorus deficiency in some regions and excess pollution in others
   C) No consequences
   D) Only scarcity

4. What solutions are required?
   A) Only reducing all phosphorus use
   B) Ignoring the problem
   C) Only increasing mining
   D) Transforming from a linear to a circular phosphorus system

5. What does the research underscore about phosphorus?
   A) It only affects agriculture
   B) It is a critical resource for food security that is currently being mismanaged
   C) It is not important
   D) It is unlimited

**Answers:**
1. B - 文章主要讲述威胁粮食生产的全球磷危机
2. C - 农业中使用的磷约80%损失
3. B - 一些地区磷缺乏,其他地区污染过剩
4. D - 从线性磷系统转变为循环系统
5. B - 它是粮食安全的关键资源,目前管理不善

### 翻译

**文章 54. 全球磷危机**

世界正面临威胁粮食生产和生态系统健康的全球磷危机。磷是所有生命必需的营养素,对植物生长和粮食生产至关重要,但它是一种无法合成的有限资源。当前的磷使用系统效率极低,大部分营养素在一次使用后就流失到环境中,在耗尽 reserves 的同时造成污染。

研究表明,农业中使用的磷约80%通过径流和侵蚀流失,进入水道导致富营养化和死亡区。同时,磷矿 reserves(肥料的主要来源)集中在少数几个国家,摩洛哥控制着全球约70%的 reserves。这造成了地缘政治脆弱性和价格波动,可能影响全球粮食安全。一些估计表明,磷产量峰值可能在未来几十年内出现,之后供应将下降。

这场危机的后果是双重的。在磷缺乏的地区,特别是撒哈拉以南非洲,作物产量受到这种必需营养素缺乏的限制,导致饥饿和营养不良。在磷过剩的地区,特别是集约化农业的发达国家,这种营养素造成严重的水污染。墨西哥湾死亡区、伊利湖的藻华和波罗的海的富营养化都是磷污染的后果。

解决方案需要将磷循环从线性系统转变为循环系统。精准农业可以优化肥料施用,在保持作物产量的同时减少损失。从废物流中回收磷,包括污水污泥、食物垃圾和动物粪便,可以回收这种宝贵的营养素。改进的废水处理可以在磷到达水道之前捕获它。研究表明,这些措施可以将对开采磷的需求减少50%或更多,同时消除污染。

然而,实施这些解决方案需要改变农业实践、废物管理系统和政策框架。研究强调,磷是粮食安全的关键资源,目前管理不当。如果不采取行动,世界将面临磷稀缺和污染同时存在的悖论,威胁粮食生产和生态系统健康。

**题目翻译:**

1. 文章主要讲什么?
   A) 磷污染的好处
   B) 威胁粮食生产的全球磷危机
   C) 增加磷矿开采的方法
   D) 磷发现的历史

2. 农业中使用的磷有多少比例流失?
   A) 20%
   B) 95%
   C) 80%
   D) 50%

3. 磷危机的双重后果是什么?
   A) 仅污染
   B) 一些地区磷缺乏,其他地区污染过剩
   C) 没有后果
   D) 仅稀缺

4. 需要什么解决方案?
   A) 仅减少所有磷使用
   B) 忽视问题
   C) 仅增加开采
   D) 从线性磷系统转变为循环系统

5. 研究关于磷强调了什么?
   A) 它仅影响农业
   B) 它是粮食安全的关键资源,目前管理不善
   C) 它不重要
   D) 它是无限的

## 文章 55. Ocean Circulation Changes

Ocean circulation（海洋环流）changes threaten climate stability worldwide, as the system that distributes heat around the planet shows signs of weakening due to climate change. The Atlantic Meridional Overturning Circulation (AMOC), often called the "global ocean conveyor belt（传送带）," is at its weakest point in over 1,000 years, with research indicating a 15% decline in strength since the mid-20th century.

The AMOC works by transporting warm, salty water from the tropics（热带）northward in the Atlantic Ocean. As this water reaches the North Atlantic, it cools, becomes denser（更密集的）, and sinks, driving a deep ocean current that flows southward. This circulation distributes heat globally, warming Europe by several degrees Celsius and influencing weather patterns worldwide. The circulation is driven by differences in water temperature and salinity（盐度）, which are both being altered by climate change.

Freshwater input from melting Greenland ice and increased precipitation（降水）in the North Atlantic is reducing the salinity of surface waters, making them less dense and less likely to sink. This weakens the overturning（翻转的）circulation. Research indicates that if the AMOC continues to weaken, it could reach a tipping point（临界点）where it collapses entirely, a scenario（情景）that would have catastrophic（灾难性的）consequences for global climate.

The impacts of AMOC weakening or collapse would be severe. Europe would experience significant cooling while the rest of the world continues to warm, creating complex climate patterns. Sea levels along the US East Coast would rise rapidly as the ocean adjusts to the changed circulation. Monsoon（季风）patterns in Africa and Asia would shift, affecting rainfall for billions of people. Marine ecosystems would be disrupted（扰乱）as nutrient transport changes. The research indicates that some of these changes are already underway, with observable（可观察的）shifts in ocean temperatures and circulation patterns.

Addressing this threat requires urgent action to reduce greenhouse gas emissions and slow the rate of climate change. The research underscores that ocean circulation is a critical component of the Earth's climate system that is being destabilized（不稳定化）by human activities. Without significant emission reductions, the risk of catastrophic circulation changes will continue to increase, with profound（深远的）consequences for climate stability worldwide.

**Questions:**

1. What is the central concern of the passage?
   A) Methods for increasing ocean temperatures
   B) Ocean circulation changes threatening climate stability
   C) The benefits of ocean circulation changes
   D) The history of ocean exploration

2. How much has AMOC strength declined since the mid-20th century?
   A) 5%
   B) 90%
   C) 50%
   D) 15%

3. What drives the AMOC?
   A) Only ocean depth
   B) Wind patterns only
   C) Only surface currents
   D) Differences in water temperature and salinity

4. What would happen if AMOC collapses?
   A) Europe would cool, sea levels would rise on US East Coast, and monsoons would shift
   B) The climate would improve
   C) Only Europe would be affected
   D) No significant effects

5. What is required to address this threat?
   A) Removing all ocean water
   B) Increasing greenhouse gas emissions
   C) Ignoring the problem
   D) Urgent action to reduce greenhouse gas emissions

**Answers:**
1. B - 文章的核心关注点是海洋环流变化威胁气候稳定
2. D - 自20世纪中叶以来,AMOC强度下降了15%
3. D - 水温和盐度的差异
4. A - 欧洲变冷,美国东海岸海平面上升,季风改变
5. D - 迫切需要采取行动减少温室气体排放

### 翻译

**文章 55. 海洋环流变化**

海洋环流变化威胁着全球气候稳定,因为在全球分配热量的系统显示出因气候变化而减弱的迹象。大西洋经向翻转环流(AMOC),通常被称为"全球海洋传送带",正处于1000多年来的最弱状态,研究表明自20世纪中叶以来强度下降了15%。

AMOC通过在北大西洋将温暖、咸的水从热带向北输送来工作。当这些水到达北大西洋时,它冷却、变得更密集并下沉,驱动向南流动的深海洋流。这种环流在全球分配热量,使欧洲变暖数摄氏度并影响全球天气模式。这种环流由水温和盐度的差异驱动,而这两者都正被气候变化改变。

格陵兰冰盖融化的淡水输入和北大西洋降水的增加正在降低表层水的盐度,使其密度降低、更不容易下沉。这削弱了翻转环流。研究表明,如果AMOC继续减弱,它可能达到完全崩溃的临界点,这种情况将对全球气候产生灾难性后果。

AMOC减弱或崩溃的影响将是严重的。欧洲将经历显著降温,而世界其他地区继续变暖,造成复杂的气候模式。随着海洋适应变化的环流,美国东海岸的海平面将迅速上升。非洲和亚洲的季风模式将改变,影响数十亿人的降雨。随着营养运输的变化,海洋生态系统将被扰乱。研究表明,其中一些变化已经在进行中,海洋温度和环流模式出现可观察到的转变。

应对这一威胁需要采取紧急行动减少温室气体排放并减缓气候变化速率。研究强调,海洋环流是地球气候系统的关键组成部分,正被人类活动 destabilized。如果不大幅减少排放,灾难性环流变化的风险将继续增加,对全球气候稳定产生深远影响。

**题目翻译:**

1. 文章的核心关注点是什么?
   A) 增加海洋温度的方法
   B) 威胁气候稳定的海洋环流变化
   C) 海洋环流变化的好处
   D) 海洋探索的历史

2. 自20世纪中叶以来AMOC强度下降了多少?

   B) 90%
   C) 50%
   D) 15%

3. 什么驱动AMOC?
   A) 仅海洋深度
   B) 仅风模式
   C) 仅表层洋流
   D) 水温和盐度的差异

4. 如果AMOC崩溃会发生什么?

   B) 气候会改善
   C) 仅欧洲会受影响
   D) 没有显著影响

5. 应对这一威胁需要什么?
   A) 移除所有海水
   B) 增加温室气体排放
   C) 忽视问题
   D) 采取紧急行动减少温室气体排放

## 文章 56. Biodiversity Hotspots Under Threat

Biodiversity hotspots, regions with exceptionally（异常地）high species richness（丰富度）and endemism（特有性）, are under severe threat from human activities, with many facing irreversible（不可逆转的）loss of unique species and ecosystems. Research shows that these hotspots, which cover only 2.4% of the Earth's land surface, support over 50% of all plant species and 42% of all vertebrate（脊椎动物）species, making their conservation critical for global biodiversity.

The concept of biodiversity hotspots was developed to prioritize（优先）conservation efforts in areas where the most species could be protected with the least area. There are 36 recognized hotspots, including the tropical Andes（安第斯山脉）, Madagascar, Sundaland (Southeast Asia), the Caribbean（加勒比海）, and the Mediterranean（地中海）Basin. These regions have high concentrations of endemic（特有的）species, meaning species found nowhere else on Earth. However, they have also experienced high levels of habitat loss, with most hotspots having lost over 70% of their original vegetation.

The threats are multiple and intensifying（加剧的）. Deforestation for agriculture, logging（伐木）, and urbanization is the primary driver, particularly in tropical hotspots. Climate change is altering habitats faster than many species can adapt or migrate. Invasive（入侵的）species outcompete（胜过）native organisms. Overexploitation（过度开发）through hunting, fishing, and collection directly reduces populations. Pollution contaminates（污染）habitats. The research indicates that these threats often interact, creating synergistic（协同的）effects that accelerate biodiversity loss.

The consequences of losing hotspots would be catastrophic（灾难性的）. Many species found only in hotspots provide unique ecosystem services, including medicinal plants that have not yet been studied. The loss of these species represents an irreversible loss of genetic（基因的）diversity and potential resources for humanity. Hotspots also provide essential services including water regulation, climate stabilization（稳定化）, and cultural value for local communities.

Conservation efforts in hotspots face significant challenges. Many hotspots are in developing countries with limited resources for conservation. Political instability（不稳定）, poverty, and competing development priorities make conservation difficult. However, research shows that protected areas, community-based conservation, sustainable livelihoods（生计）, and international funding can be effective when properly implemented. The research underscores that protecting hotspots is not just an environmental imperative（当务之急）but essential for human well-being and sustainable development.

**Questions:**

1. 1. What is the author's primary purpose in writing this passage?
   A) To report on biodiversity hotspots under threat
   B) To explain how to increase deforestation
   C) To promote habitat destruction
   D) To argue against conservation

2. 2. What percentage of Earth's land surface do hotspots cover?
   A) 50%
   B) 10%
   C) 25%
   D) 2.4%

3. 3. What percentage of original vegetation have most hotspots lost?
   A) 30%
   B) 10%
   C) 95%
   D) Over 70%

4. 4. What is the primary threat to hotspots?
   A) Only invasive species
   B) Only climate change
   C) Only natural disasters
   D) Deforestation for agriculture, logging, and urbanization

5. 5. What is required to protect hotspots?
   A) Only increasing deforestation
   B) Only removing all species
   C) Protected areas, community-based conservation, sustainable livelihoods, and international funding
   D) Ignoring the problem

**Answers:**
1. A - 作者的主要目的是报道受到威胁的生物多样性热点地区
2. D - 热点地区覆盖地球陆地表面的2.4%
3. D - 大多数热点地区失去了超过70%的原始植被
4. D - 农业、伐木和城市化的森林砍伐
5. C - 保护区、基于社区的保护、可持续生计和国际资金

### 翻译

**文章 56. 生物多样性热点地区受到威胁**

生物多样性热点地区,即具有异常高物种丰富度和特有性的区域,正面临人类活动的严重威胁,许多地区面临着独特物种和生态系统不可逆转的损失。研究表明,这些热点地区仅覆盖地球陆地表面的2.4%,却支撑着超过50%的所有植物物种和42%的所有脊椎动物物种,使其保护对全球生物多样性至关重要。

生物多样性热点地区的概念被开发出来,是为了在能够以最小面积保护最多物种的地区优先开展保护工作。目前有36个公认的热点地区,包括热带安第斯山脉、马达加斯加、巽他大陆(东南亚)、加勒比海和地中海盆地。这些区域拥有高浓度的特有物种,即地球上仅在本地发现的物种。然而,它们也经历了高水平的栖息地丧失,大多数热点地区已经失去了超过70%的原始植被。

威胁是多方面的且在加剧。为农业、伐木和城市化而进行的森林砍伐是主要驱动因素,特别是在热带热点地区。气候变化改变栖息地的速度超过了许多物种能够适应或迁移的速度。入侵物种在竞争中胜过本地生物。通过狩猎、捕捞和采集进行的过度开发直接减少了种群数量。污染污染了栖息地。研究表明,这些威胁往往相互作用,产生加速生物多样性丧失的协同效应。

失去热点地区的后果将是灾难性的。许多仅在热点地区发现的物种提供独特的生态系统服务,包括尚未被研究的药用植物。这些物种的损失代表着基因多样性和人类潜在资源的不可逆转丧失。热点地区还提供基本服务,包括水调节、气候稳定化以及对当地社区的文化价值。

热点地区的保护工作面临重大挑战。许多热点地区位于保护资源有限的发展中国家。政治不稳定、贫困和相互竞争的发展优先事项使保护工作困难重重。然而,研究表明,保护区、基于社区的保护、可持续生计和国际资金在得到妥善实施时可以发挥有效作用。研究强调,保护热点地区不仅是环境当务之急,而且对人类福祉和可持续发展至关重要。

**题目翻译:**

1. 作者写这篇文章的主要目的是什么?
   A) 报道受到威胁的生物多样性热点地区
   B) 解释如何增加森林砍伐
   C) 促进栖息地破坏
   D) 反对保护

2. 热点地区覆盖地球陆地表面的百分之多少?
   A) 50%
   B) 10%
   C) 25%
   D) 2.4%

3. 大多数热点地区失去了百分之多少的原始植被?
   A) 30%
   B) 10%
   C) 95%
   D) 超过70%

4. 热点地区面临的主要威胁是什么?
   A) 仅入侵物种
   B) 仅气候变化
   C) 仅自然灾害
   D) 为农业、伐木和城市化的森林砍伐

5. 保护热点地区需要什么?
   A) 仅增加森林砍伐
   B) 仅移除所有物种
   C) 保护区、基于社区的保护、可持续生计和国际资金
   D) 忽视问题

## 文章 57. Climate Change Alters Seasonal Timing

Climate change is altering seasonal timing worldwide, with spring events occurring earlier and autumn events occurring later, disrupting（扰乱）the synchronization（同步）of ecological processes that have evolved over millennia（千年）. This phenomenon（现象）, known as phenological（物候的）change, is affecting plants, animals, and the interactions between species, with cascading（级联的）effects on ecosystems and human societies.

Research based on long-term observations shows that spring events such as leaf emergence, flowering, bird migration, and insect emergence are advancing by an average of 2-3 days per decade. Autumn events such as leaf coloration and fall are delaying by a similar amount. These changes are most pronounced（显著的）in temperate（温带的）and polar regions, where warming has been greatest. The research indicates that the rate of change is accelerating, with some species shifting their timing by over a week in just the past 30 years.

The consequences of these shifts can be severe when species that interact with each other respond differently to climate change. For example, if plants flower earlier but their pollinators（传粉者）do not adjust their emergence timing, pollination may fail, reducing seed production. Similarly, if birds migrate based on day length but their insect prey（猎物）emerges earlier due to temperature, chicks may hatch after the peak of food availability, reducing survival rates. These mismatches（不匹配）can lead to population declines and even local extinctions（灭绝）.

Agriculture is also affected by phenological changes. Crops may flower earlier, exposing them to late spring frosts（霜冻）that can destroy yields. Pest insects may emerge earlier or have more generations per year, increasing crop damage. The timing of planting and harvest must be adjusted, requiring changes in farming practices. The research indicates that without adaptation（适应）, climate change could reduce global crop yields by 10-25% by 2050.

Human health and economies are also impacted. Earlier springs can extend the season for allergenic（致敏的）pollen（花粉）, worsening allergies and asthma. Changes in seasonal timing affect tourism, recreation, and cultural traditions tied to seasonal events. The research underscores that phenological change is a visible and measurable（可衡量的）indicator（指标）of climate change with widespread consequences. Monitoring these changes provides valuable data for understanding climate impacts and developing adaptation strategies. However, the most effective response is to reduce greenhouse gas emissions to slow the rate of climate change.

**Questions:**

1. What is the passage mainly about?
   A) Methods for increasing seasonal stability
   B) The benefits of phenological change
   C) Climate change altering seasonal timing worldwide
   D) The history of seasonal observation

2. How much are spring events advancing per decade?
   A) 30 days
   B) 0.5 days
   C) 10 days
   D) 2-3 days

3. What happens when species respond differently to climate change?
   A) Only affects plants
   B) Mismatches that can lead to population declines
   C) No consequences
   D) Only positive effects

4. How is agriculture affected?
   A) Only positive effects
   B) Crops may flower earlier, pests may emerge earlier, and yields could be reduced
   C) No effects
   D) Only benefits farmers

5. What is the most effective response to phenological change?
   A) Only adjusting farming practices
   B) Only monitoring changes
   C) Ignoring the problem
   D) Reducing greenhouse gas emissions to slow climate change

**Answers:**
1. C - 文章主要讲述气候变化改变全球季节时间
2. D - 春季事件每十年提前2-3天
3. B - 可能导致种群下降的不匹配
4. B - 作物可能更早开花,害虫可能更早出现,产量可能减少
5. D - 减少温室气体排放以减缓气候变化

---

### 翻译

**文章 57. 气候变化改变季节时间**

气候变化正在全球范围内改变季节时间,春季事件发生得更早,秋季事件发生得更晚,扰乱了数千年来进化形成的生态过程的同步性。这种现象被称为物候变化,正在影响植物、动物以及物种之间的相互作用,对生态系统和人类社会产生级联效应。

基于长期观测的研究表明,叶片萌发、开花、鸟类迁徙和昆虫出现等春季事件平均每十年提前2-3天。叶片变色和落叶等秋季事件也延迟了类似的幅度。这些变化在温带和极地地区最为显著,这些地区的变暖幅度最大。研究表明,变化速度正在加快,一些物种仅在过去30年中就将时间调整了超过一周。

当相互作用的物种对气候变化的响应不同时,这些转变的后果可能很严重。例如,如果植物开花更早但它们的传粉者不调整出现时间,授粉可能失败,导致种子产量减少。同样,如果鸟类根据日照长度迁徙但它们的昆虫猎物因温度而更早出现,雏鸟可能在食物供应高峰期过后才孵化,降低存活率。这些不匹配可能导致种群下降甚至局部灭绝。

农业也受到物候变化的影响。作物可能更早开花,使其暴露在可能摧毁产量的晚春霜冻中。害虫昆虫可能更早出现或每年有更多代,增加作物损害。种植和收获的时间必须调整,需要改变农业实践。研究表明,如果不进行适应,到2050年气候变化可能使全球作物产量减少10-25%。

人类健康和经济也受到影响。更早的春季可能延长致敏花粉的季节,加重过敏和哮喘。季节时间的变化影响与季节事件相关的旅游、娱乐和文化传统。研究表明,物候变化是气候变化的一个可见且可衡量的指标,具有广泛的影响。监测这些变化为理解气候影响和制定适应策略提供了宝贵的数据。然而,最有效的应对措施是减少温室气体排放以减缓气候变化速度。

**题目翻译:**

1. 文章主要讲什么?
   A) 增加季节稳定性的方法
   B) 物候变化的好处
   C) 气候变化改变全球季节时间
   D) 季节观测的历史

2. 春季事件每十年提前多少?
   A) 30天
   B) 0.5天
   C) 10天
   D) 2-3天

3. 当物种对气候变化的响应不同时会发生什么?
   A) 仅影响植物
   B) 可能导致种群下降的不匹配
   C) 没有后果
   D) 只有积极影响

4. 农业如何受到影响?
   A) 只有积极影响
   B) 作物可能更早开花,害虫可能更早出现,产量可能减少
   C) 没有影响
   D) 只对农民有利

5. 对物候变化最有效的应对措施是什么?
   A) 仅调整农业实践
   B) 仅监测变化
   C) 忽视问题
   D) 减少温室气体排放以减缓气候变化

---

## 文章 58. Global Dust Cycle Intensifying

The global dust cycle is intensifying（加剧）with climate change and land use change, with dust emissions increasing by approximately 15% since the 19th century. This increase in atmospheric dust has significant implications（影响）for climate, air quality, and ecosystem health worldwide.

Research shows that dust emissions are driven by wind erosion of dry, bare soils, and both climate change and human activities are increasing the areas of exposed soil. Droughts are becoming more frequent and severe in many regions, drying out soils and making them more susceptible（易受影响的）to wind erosion. Deforestation and overgrazing remove vegetation that protects soil surfaces. Agricultural expansion into marginal（边缘的）lands exposes soils that were previously stable. The research indicates that major dust sources include the Sahara Desert, the Arabian Peninsula（阿拉伯半岛）, Central Asia, and Australia, with dust from these regions traveling thousands of kilometers across oceans and continents.

The effects of increased dust are complex and far-reaching（深远的）. Dust particles in the atmosphere affect climate by scattering（散射）and absorbing solar radiation, with the net effect depending on particle size, composition（成分）, and altitude（海拔）. Dust that settles on snow and ice reduces surface albedo（反照率）, accelerating melting. Dust deposited in oceans provides essential nutrients like iron that fertilize（施肥）phytoplankton（浮游植物）growth, affecting marine productivity and carbon cycling. However, dust can also carry pathogens（病原体）, pesticides（农药）, and heavy metals that contaminate（污染）ecosystems and affect human health.

Air quality is significantly impacted by dust events. Fine dust particles (PM10 and PM2.5) can travel long distances and penetrate（穿透）deep into the lungs, causing respiratory（呼吸的）and cardiovascular（心血管的）problems. The World Health Organization estimates that air pollution, including dust, causes approximately 7 million premature（过早的）deaths annually. Dust storms can reduce visibility（能见度）, disrupting（扰乱）transportation and causing economic losses. The research indicates that dust-related air quality problems are increasing in many regions, particularly in North Africa, the Middle East, and Central Asia.

Addressing the intensifying dust cycle requires action on multiple fronts. Reducing greenhouse gas emissions can slow climate change and reduce drought frequency. Sustainable land management practices, including conservation agriculture, reforestation（重新造林）, and controlled grazing（放牧）, can protect soil surfaces. Dust monitoring and early warning systems can help protect public health during dust events. The research underscores that dust is not just a natural phenomenon but is being amplified by human activities, requiring comprehensive（全面的）management strategies.

**Questions:**

1. What is the central argument of the passage?
   A) The dust cycle is decreasing
   B) Dust only affects local areas
   C) The global dust cycle is intensifying with climate and land use change
   D) Dust has no effects on climate

2. How much have dust emissions increased since the 19th century?
   A) 15%
   B) 5%
   C) 50%
   D) 100%

3. What drives dust emissions?
   A) Only ocean currents
   B) Wind erosion of dry, bare soils
   C) Only human activities
   D) Only volcanic activity

4. What are the effects of increased dust?
   A) Only positive effects
   B) No effects
   C) Only affects deserts
   D) Complex effects on climate, air quality, and ecosystem health

5. What is required to address the dust cycle?
   A) Action on multiple fronts including reducing emissions and sustainable land management
   B) Only increasing dust emissions
   C) Ignoring the problem
   D) Only removing all vegetation

**Answers:**
1. C - 文章的核心论点是全球粉尘循环随着气候和土地利用变化而加剧
2. A - 自19世纪以来,粉尘排放增加了约15%
3. B - 干燥裸露土壤的风蚀
4. D - 对气候、空气质量和生态系统健康的复杂影响
5. A - 多方面的行动,包括减少排放和可持续土地管理

---

### 翻译

**文章 58. 全球粉尘循环加剧**

全球粉尘循环正随着气候变化和土地利用变化而加剧,自19世纪以来粉尘排放增加了约15%。大气粉尘的增加对全球气候、空气质量和生态系统健康具有重大影响。

研究表明,粉尘排放由干燥裸露土壤的风蚀驱动,气候变化和人类活动都在增加暴露土壤的面积。干旱在许多地区变得更加频繁和严重,使土壤干燥并更容易受到风蚀。森林砍伐和过度放牧移除了保护土壤表面的植被。向边缘土地的农业扩张暴露了以前稳定的土壤。研究表明,主要粉尘源包括撒哈拉沙漠、阿拉伯半岛、中亚和澳大利亚,来自这些地区的粉尘跨越数千公里穿越海洋和大陆。

粉尘增加的影响是复杂且深远的。大气中的粉尘颗粒通过散射和吸收太阳辐射来影响气候,净效应取决于颗粒大小、成分和海拔。沉降到雪和冰上的粉尘降低了表面反照率,加速融化。沉积在海洋中的粉尘提供铁等必需营养物质,促进浮游植物生长,影响海洋生产力和碳循环。然而,粉尘也可能携带病原体、农药和重金属,污染生态系统并影响人类健康。

空气质量受到粉尘事件的显著影响。细粉尘颗粒(PM10和PM2.5)可以长距离传播并深入肺部,导致呼吸系统和心血管问题。世界卫生组织估计,包括粉尘在内的空气污染每年导致约700万人过早死亡。沙尘暴能降低能见度,扰乱交通并造成经济损失。研究表明,与粉尘相关的空气质量问题在许多地区正在增加,特别是在北非、中东和中亚。

应对加剧的粉尘循环需要多方面的行动。减少温室气体排放可以减缓气候变化并降低干旱频率。可持续土地管理实践,包括保护性农业、重新造林和控制放牧,可以保护土壤表面。粉尘监测和预警系统可以帮助在粉尘事件期间保护公众健康。研究强调,粉尘不仅仅是一种自然现象,而是正在被人类活动放大,需要全面的管理策略。

**题目翻译:**

1. 文章的核心论点是什么?
   A) 粉尘循环在减少
   B) 粉尘仅影响局部地区
   C) 全球粉尘循环随着气候和土地利用变化而加剧
   D) 粉尘对气候没有影响

2. 自19世纪以来粉尘排放增加了多少?
   A) 15%
   B) 5%
   C) 50%
   D) 100%

3. 什么驱动粉尘排放?
   A) 仅洋流
   B) 干燥裸露土壤的风蚀
   C) 仅人类活动
   D) 仅火山活动

4. 粉尘增加的影响是什么?
   A) 只有积极影响
   B) 没有影响
   C) 仅影响沙漠
   D) 对气候、空气质量和生态系统健康的复杂影响

5. 应对粉尘循环需要什么?
   A) 多方面的行动,包括减少排放和可持续土地管理
   B) 仅增加粉尘排放
   C) 忽视问题
   D) 仅移除所有植被

## 文章 59. Freshwater Crisis Intensifies

The global freshwater crisis is intensifying as demand outstrips（超过）supply in many regions, threatening food security, human health, and economic development. Research shows that approximately 2.2 billion people currently lack access to safely managed drinking water, and 4.2 billion lack safely managed sanitation（卫生）services. These numbers are projected to worsen as population growth, climate change, and pollution increase pressure on freshwater resources.

The causes of the freshwater crisis are multiple and interconnected. Population growth increases demand for water for drinking, agriculture, and industry. Climate change alters precipitation（降水）patterns, making some regions drier and others wetter, while increasing the frequency of droughts and floods. Pollution from agriculture, industry, and urban areas contaminates（污染）water sources, reducing the amount of usable water. Over-extraction of groundwater for irrigation（灌溉）is depleting（耗尽）aquifers（含水层）faster than they can recharge（补充）. The research indicates that by 2025, half of the world's population could be living in water-stressed areas.

The consequences are severe and far-reaching. Agriculture, which accounts for 70% of global freshwater use, faces increasing competition from urban and industrial users. Crop yields decline when water is scarce, threatening food security for billions of people. Waterborne（水传播的）diseases kill approximately 1.5 million people annually, mostly children under five. Ecosystems that depend on freshwater, including wetlands, rivers, and lakes, are degraded, losing biodiversity and the services they provide.

The economic costs are substantial. Water scarcity（稀缺）reduces agricultural and industrial productivity, constraining（限制）economic growth. The cost of water treatment and infrastructure increases as sources become more polluted or distant. Health costs rise due to waterborne diseases. The research indicates that water scarcity could reduce GDP in some regions by up to 6% by 2050.

Solutions require integrated（综合的）water resource management that balances human needs with ecosystem requirements. Improving water use efficiency in agriculture through drip irrigation（滴灌）and drought-resistant（抗旱的）crops can reduce demand. Wastewater treatment and reuse can expand water supplies. Protecting watersheds（流域）and wetlands maintains natural water filtration（过滤）and storage. Policy reforms can ensure equitable（公平的）access and sustainable use. The research underscores that water is a finite resource that requires careful management. Without action, the freshwater crisis will continue to intensify, with devastating（毁灭性的）consequences for human well-being and sustainable development.

**Questions:**

1. What is the author's primary purpose in writing this passage?
   A) To argue against water conservation
   B) To promote water pollution
   C) To report on the intensifying global freshwater crisis
   D) To explain how to increase water demand

2. How many people lack access to safely managed drinking water?
   A) 500 million
   B) 2.2 billion
   C) 10 billion
   D) 5 billion

3. What are the main causes of the freshwater crisis?
   A) Only climate change
   B) Population growth, climate change, pollution, and over-extraction
   C) Only population growth
   D) Only natural disasters

4. What are the consequences of the freshwater crisis?
   A) No consequences
   B) Only positive effects
   C) Declining crop yields, waterborne diseases, and ecosystem degradation
   D) Only affects urban areas

5. What is required to address the crisis?
   A) Only removing all water users
   B) Only increasing water extraction
   C) Ignoring the problem
   D) Integrated water resource management balancing human and ecosystem needs

**Answers:**
1. C - 作者的主要目的是报道加剧的全球淡水危机
2. B - 22亿人无法获得安全管理的饮用水
3. B - 人口增长、气候变化、污染和过度开采
4. C - 作物产量下降、水传播疾病和生态系统退化
5. D - 平衡人类和生态系统需求的综合水资源管理

---

### 翻译

**文章 59. 淡水危机加剧**

全球淡水危机正在加剧,因为在许多地区需求超过供应,威胁粮食安全、人类健康和经济发展。研究表明,目前约有22亿人无法获得安全管理的饮用水,42亿人缺乏安全管理的卫生服务。随着人口增长、气候变化和污染增加对淡水资源的压力,这些数字预计将恶化。

淡水危机的原因是多重且相互关联的。人口增长增加了对饮用水、农业和工业用水的需求。气候变化改变了降水模式,使一些地区更干燥,另一些地区更湿润,同时增加了干旱和洪水的频率。来自农业、工业和城市地区的污染污染了水源,减少了可用水量。过度抽取地下水进行灌溉正在以超过补充速度的速度耗尽含水层。研究表明,到2025年,世界一半的人口可能生活在水资源紧张的地区。

后果是严重且深远的。占全球淡水使用量70%的农业面临来自城市和工业用户日益增加的竞争。当水资源稀缺时,作物产量下降,威胁数十亿人的粮食安全。水传播疾病每年导致约150万人死亡,大多是五岁以下儿童。依赖淡水的生态系统,包括湿地、河流和湖泊,正在退化,失去生物多样性及其提供的服务。

经济损失是巨大的。水资源稀缺降低了农业和工业生产力,制约了经济增长。随着水源变得更加污染或遥远,水处理和基础设施的成本增加。由于水传播疾病,健康成本上升。研究表明,到2050年,水资源稀缺可能使某些地区的GDP减少高达6%。

解决方案需要平衡人类需求与生态系统需求的综合水资源管理。通过滴灌和抗旱作物提高农业用水效率可以减少需求。废水处理和回用可以扩大供水。保护流域和湿地维持自然的过滤和储存功能。政策改革可以确保公平获取和可持续使用。研究强调,水是一种有限资源,需要精心管理。如果不采取行动,淡水危机将继续加剧,对人类福祉和可持续发展造成毁灭性后果。

**题目翻译:**

1. 作者写这篇文章的主要目的是什么?
   A) 反对水资源保护
   B) 促进水污染
   C) 报道加剧的全球淡水危机
   D) 解释如何增加水需求

2. 多少人无法获得安全管理的饮用水?
   A) 5亿
   B) 22亿
   C) 100亿
   D) 50亿

3. 淡水危机的主要原因是什么?
   A) 仅气候变化
   B) 人口增长、气候变化、污染和过度开采
   C) 仅人口增长
   D) 仅自然灾害

4. 淡水危机的后果是什么?
   A) 没有后果
   B) 只有积极影响
   C) 作物产量下降、水传播疾病和生态系统退化
   D) 仅影响城市地区

5. 应对危机需要什么?
   A) 仅移除所有用水者
   B) 仅增加水抽取
   C) 忽视问题
   D) 平衡人类和生态系统需求的综合水资源管理

## 文章 60. Invasive Species Costs Exceed $423 Billion

The economic costs of invasive（入侵的）species exceed $423 billion annually worldwide, making them one of the most expensive environmental problems facing humanity. These non-native（非本地的）species, introduced intentionally（故意地）or accidentally（意外地）to new environments, cause damage to agriculture, infrastructure（基础设施）, ecosystems, and human health on a global scale.

Research shows that invasive species are responsible for significant losses in multiple sectors. In agriculture, invasive pests（害虫）and diseases reduce crop yields and increase control costs. The fall armyworm（行军虫）, which has spread across Africa and Asia, causes billions in crop losses annually. In forestry, invasive insects like the emerald（翡翠）ash borer（钻孔虫）kill millions of trees, requiring costly removal and replacement. Infrastructure damage occurs when invasive species like zebra mussels（斑马贻贝）clog（堵塞）water intake pipes, or when invasive plants destabilize（使不稳定）roads and levees（堤坝）. Health costs arise from invasive species that transmit diseases, such as mosquitoes that carry malaria（疟疾）, dengue（登革热）, and Zika virus.

The ecological impacts are equally severe. Invasive species are the second leading cause of biodiversity loss after habitat destruction. They outcompete（胜过）native species for resources, prey on native organisms, alter habitats, and introduce diseases. The brown tree snake, introduced to Guam after World War II, caused the extinction of 10 native forest bird species. Zebra mussels in the Great Lakes have fundamentally altered the ecosystem, affecting entire food webs. The research indicates that once established, invasive species are extremely difficult and expensive to control or eradicate（根除）.

The costs are not evenly distributed. Island nations and isolated ecosystems are particularly vulnerable（脆弱的）because native species evolved without competitors or predators. Developing countries often lack resources for prevention and control, making them more susceptible（易感的）to invasions. However, even wealthy nations spend billions annually on invasive species management. The research indicates that prevention is far more cost-effective than control, with every dollar spent on prevention saving approximately ten dollars in future control costs.

Addressing the invasive species crisis requires comprehensive strategies. Strengthening biosecurity（生物安全）measures at borders can prevent new introductions（引入）. Early detection and rapid response can eradicate invasions before they become established. Public education can reduce accidental introductions through trade and travel. International cooperation is essential because invasive species do not respect political boundaries. The research underscores that invasive species are a growing threat that requires urgent attention and increased investment in prevention and management.

**Questions:**

1. What is the passage mainly about?
   A) The economic costs of invasive species exceeding $423 billion annually
   B) The benefits of invasive species
   C) The history of invasive species discovery
   D) Methods for introducing more species

2. What are the annual economic costs of invasive species?
   A) $100 million
   B) $10 billion
   C) $423 billion
   D) $1 trillion

3. What is the second leading cause of biodiversity loss?
   A) Natural disasters
   B) Pollution
   C) Climate change
   D) Invasive species

4. Why are island nations particularly vulnerable?
   A) Native species evolved without competitors or predators
   B) They have no ecosystems
   C) They have more resources
   D) They are larger

5. What is more cost-effective than control?
   A) Prevention
   B) Ignoring the problem
   C) Removing all native species
   D) Introducing more species

**Answers:**
1. A - 文章主要讲述入侵物种的经济成本超过每年4230亿美元
2. C - 4230亿美元
3. D - 入侵物种
4. A - 本地物种在没有竞争者或捕食者的情况下进化
5. A - 预防

---

### 翻译

**文章 60. 入侵物种成本超过4230亿美元**

入侵物种的经济成本每年全球超过4230亿美元,使其成为人类面临的最昂贵的环境问题之一。这些非本地物种被有意或意外引入新环境,在全球范围内对农业、基础设施、生态系统和人类健康造成损害。

研究表明,入侵物种在多个部门造成了重大损失。在农业中,入侵性害虫和疾病降低作物产量并增加控制成本。已蔓延到非洲和亚洲的秋粘虫每年造成数十亿美元的作物损失。在林业中,翡翠灰螟等入侵性昆虫杀死数百万棵树,需要昂贵的移除和替换。当斑马贻贝等入侵物种堵塞取水管道,或入侵植物使道路和堤坝不稳定时,会发生基础设施损坏。入侵物种传播疾病(如携带疟疾、登革热和寨卡病毒的蚊子)会产生健康成本。

生态影响同样严重。入侵物种是栖息地破坏之后生物多样性丧失的第二大原因。它们在资源方面胜过本地物种,捕食本地生物,改变栖息地,并引入疾病。二战后引入关岛的棕树蛇导致了10种本地森林鸟类的灭绝。大湖区的斑马贻贝从根本上改变了生态系统,影响了整个食物网。研究表明,一旦建立,入侵物种极难控制或根除,且费用高昂。

成本分布不均。岛国和孤立生态系统特别脆弱,因为本地物种在没有竞争者或捕食者的情况下进化。发展中国家往往缺乏预防和控制的资源,使其更容易受到入侵。然而,即使是富裕国家每年也花费数十亿美元用于入侵物种管理。研究表明,预防比控制更具成本效益,每花1美元预防可节省约10美元的未来控制成本。

应对入侵物种危机需要综合策略。加强边境生物安全措施可以防止新的引入。早期发现和快速响应可以在入侵物种建立之前将其根除。公众教育可以减少通过贸易和旅行的意外引入。国际合作至关重要,因为入侵物种不尊重政治边界。研究强调,入侵物种是一个日益增长的威胁,需要紧急关注并增加对预防和管理的投资。

**题目翻译:**

1. 文章主要讲什么?
   A) 入侵物种的经济成本每年超过4230亿美元
   B) 入侵物种的好处
   C) 入侵物种发现的历史
   D) 引入更多物种的方法

2. 入侵物种的年度经济成本是多少?
   A) 1亿美元
   B) 100亿美元
   C) 4230亿美元
   D) 1万亿美元

3. 生物多样性丧失的第二大原因是什么?
   A) 自然灾害
   B) 污染
   C) 气候变化
   D) 入侵物种

4. 为什么岛国特别脆弱?
   A) 本地物种在没有竞争者或捕食者的情况下进化
   B) 它们没有生态系统
   C) 它们有更多资源
   D) 它们更大

5. 什么比控制更具成本效益?
   A) 预防
   B) 忽视问题
   C) 移除所有本地物种
   D) 引入更多物种

## 文章 61. Carbon Cycle Feedbacks Amplify Climate

Carbon cycle feedbacks are amplifying（放大）climate change, with natural systems that once absorbed carbon now releasing it as temperatures rise. Research shows that the land and ocean carbon sinks（碳汇）, which currently absorb approximately half of human emissions, are becoming less effective, and in some cases are reversing to become carbon sources.

The mechanisms are complex and interconnected. Warming soils increase microbial（微生物的）decomposition（分解）of organic matter, releasing carbon dioxide. Thawing（融化）permafrost（永久冻土）in the Arctic releases ancient carbon stored for thousands of years. Drought and heat stress reduce forest growth and increase tree mortality（死亡率）, particularly in tropical forests. Wildfires, which are becoming more frequent and intense, release large amounts of carbon and destroy forests that would otherwise continue absorbing it. In the oceans, warming reduces the solubility（溶解度）of carbon dioxide and slows circulation that transports carbon to deep waters. The research indicates that these feedbacks are already occurring and will accelerate as warming continues.

The consequences are severe. As natural carbon sinks weaken, a larger fraction of human emissions remains in the atmosphere, accelerating warming. This creates a positive feedback loop where warming causes more carbon release, which causes more warming. Current climate models do not fully account for these feedbacks, meaning that projections（预测）of future warming may be underestimating（低估）actual changes. The research indicates that if these feedbacks become strong enough, it could be impossible to stabilize climate even with drastic（激烈的）emission reductions.

The research on carbon cycle feedbacks has important implications（影响）for climate policy. The Paris Agreement targets of limiting warming to 1.5 or 2 degrees Celsius are based on models that may not fully incorporate（纳入）these feedbacks. If the carbon sinks continue to weaken, achieving these targets will require even deeper and faster emission reductions than currently planned. The research underscores that protecting and restoring natural carbon sinks, including forests, wetlands, and soils, is essential for climate mitigation（缓解）.

Addressing this challenge requires urgent action on multiple fronts. Reducing greenhouse gas emissions is the primary solution to prevent dangerous warming. Protecting existing carbon sinks, particularly tropical forests and peatlands（泥炭地）, prevents additional carbon release. Restoring degraded ecosystems can rebuild carbon storage capacity. Developing technologies for carbon dioxide removal may be necessary to compensate（补偿）for weakening natural sinks. The research underscores that the carbon cycle is not a static（静态的）system but is being fundamentally altered by climate change, requiring comprehensive strategies to maintain its stabilizing role.

**Questions:**

1. What is the central concern of the passage?
   A) Methods for increasing carbon emissions
   B) Carbon cycle feedbacks amplifying climate change
   C) The benefits of carbon cycle feedbacks
   D) The history of carbon cycle discovery

2. What percentage of human emissions do land and ocean sinks currently absorb?
   A) 25%
   B) 90%
   C) 10%
   D) Approximately 50%

3. What happens as natural carbon sinks weaken?
   A) More carbon is absorbed
   B) No change
   C) A larger fraction of human emissions remains in the atmosphere
   D) Only affects oceans

4. What do current climate models not fully account for?
   A) Only temperature changes
   B) Only precipitation changes
   C) Carbon cycle feedbacks
   D) Only sea level rise

5. What is essential for climate mitigation?
   A) Only removing all forests
   B) Protecting and restoring natural carbon sinks
   C) Ignoring carbon sinks
   D) Only increasing emissions

**Answers:**
1. B - 文章的核心关注点是碳循环反馈放大气候变化
2. D - 陆地和海洋汇目前吸收约50%的人类排放
3. C - 更大比例的人类排放留在大气中
4. C - 碳循环反馈
5. B - 保护和恢复自然碳汇

---

### 翻译

**文章 61. 碳循环反馈放大气候**

碳循环反馈正在放大气候变化,曾经吸收碳的自然系统随着温度升高正在释放碳。研究表明,陆地和海洋碳汇目前吸收约一半的人类排放,但其效率正在降低,在某些情况下正在逆转为碳源。

这些机制是复杂且相互关联的。变暖的土壤增加微生物对有机物的分解,释放二氧化碳。北极永久冻土的融化释放了储存数千年的古老碳。干旱和热胁迫减少森林生长并增加树木死亡率,特别是在热带森林中。野火变得更加频繁和强烈,释放大量碳并摧毁本可以继续吸收碳的森林。在海洋中,变暖降低了二氧化碳的溶解度并减缓了将碳输送到深水的环流。研究表明,这些反馈已经在发生,并将随着变暖继续而加速。

后果是严重的。随着自然碳汇减弱,更大比例的人类排放留在大气中,加速变暖。这创造了一个正反馈循环,变暖导致更多碳释放,进而导致更多变暖。当前的气候模型没有完全考虑这些反馈,这意味着对未来变暖的预测可能低估了实际变化。研究表明,如果这些反馈变得足够强烈,即使大幅减排也可能无法稳定气候。

碳循环反馈的研究对气候政策有重要影响。巴黎协定将变暖限制在1.5或2摄氏度的目标是基于可能没有完全纳入这些反馈的模型。如果碳汇继续减弱,实现这些目标将需要比目前计划更深入、更快速的减排。研究强调,保护和恢复自然碳汇,包括森林、湿地和土壤,对气候缓解至关重要。

应对这一挑战需要多方面的紧急行动。减少温室气体排放是防止危险变暖的主要解决方案。保护现有碳汇,特别是热带森林和泥炭地,防止额外的碳释放。恢复退化的生态系统可以重建碳储存能力。开发二氧化碳移除技术可能是补偿自然碳汇减弱所必需的。研究强调,碳循环不是一个静态系统,而是正在被气候变化根本性地改变,需要综合策略来维持其稳定作用。

**题目翻译:**

1. 文章的核心关注点是什么?
   A) 增加碳排放的方法
   B) 碳循环反馈放大气候变化
   C) 碳循环反馈的好处
   D) 碳循环发现的历史

2. 陆地和海洋汇目前吸收人类排放的多少?
   A) 25%
   B) 90%
   C) 10%
   D) 约50%

3. 随着自然碳汇减弱会发生什么?
   A) 更多碳被吸收
   B) 没有变化
   C) 更大比例的人类排放留在大气中
   D) 仅影响海洋

4. 当前气候模型没有完全考虑什么?
   A) 仅温度变化
   B) 仅降水变化
   C) 碳循环反馈
   D) 仅海平面上升

5. 什么对气候缓解至关重要?
   A) 仅移除所有森林
   B) 保护和恢复自然碳汇
   C) 忽视碳汇
   D) 仅增加排放

## 文章 62. Sea Level Rise Already Unavoidable

Sea level rise is already unavoidable for centuries to come due to the thermal（热的）inertia（惯性）of the climate system, even if greenhouse gas emissions were stopped immediately. Research shows that the warming already in the system will continue to cause ice sheet melting and thermal expansion of oceans for hundreds to thousands of years, committing（使承担）future generations to rising seas regardless of current emission reduction efforts.

The mechanisms（机制）driving long-term sea level rise are well understood. The ocean has absorbed over 90% of the excess（过量的）heat trapped by greenhouse gases, and this heat continues to cause thermal expansion of seawater. Ice sheets, particularly in Greenland and West Antarctica, are responding to warming by melting at accelerating（加速的）rates. Even if temperatures were stabilized, ice sheets would continue to lose mass for centuries due to the lag（滞后）between atmospheric warming and ice sheet response. The research indicates that current commitments will likely result in 2-3 meters of sea level rise over the next 2,000 years, even under low emission scenarios（情景）.

The consequences for coastal communities are severe and long-lasting. Over 600 million people currently live in coastal areas that will be below annual flood levels by 2100 under mid-range sea level rise scenarios. This number could increase to over 1 billion by 2150. Coastal cities including Miami, Shanghai, Mumbai, Jakarta, and New York face existential（生存的）threats. Infrastructure worth trillions of dollars is at risk, including ports, airports, roads, and wastewater treatment facilities. Saltwater intrusion（侵入）will contaminate（污染）freshwater aquifers（含水层）, affecting drinking water and agriculture for hundreds of millions of people.

The economic costs are staggering（惊人的）. Protecting coastal cities through seawalls, pumps, and other infrastructure could cost tens of trillions of dollars globally. In some cases, managed retreat（有计划的撤退）—relocating communities and infrastructure inland—may be the only viable（可行的）option. The research indicates that the costs of inaction（不作为）far exceed the costs of adaptation, but even with significant investment, some losses are unavoidable. Insurance markets are already struggling with coastal risks, and some properties are becoming uninsurable（不可投保的）.

Addressing this unavoidable reality requires both mitigation（缓解）and adaptation. Reducing emissions can limit the ultimate amount of sea level rise, giving communities more time and space to adapt. However, even with aggressive mitigation, significant sea level rise is locked in. Adaptation measures including coastal protection, land use planning, and managed retreat must be implemented（实施）now to reduce future risks. The research underscores（强调）that sea level rise is a long-term challenge that requires sustained commitment across generations.

**Questions:**

1. What is the author's primary purpose in writing this passage?
   A) To explain how to stop all sea level rise
   B) To argue against climate action
   C) To promote coastal development
   D) To report on unavoidable sea level rise for centuries

2. What percentage of excess heat has the ocean absorbed?
   A) 10%
   B) 50%
   C) Over 90%
   D) 100%

3. How many people live in coastal areas at risk?
   A) 100 million
   B) Over 600 million currently, potentially over 1 billion by 2150
   C) 50 million
   D) 10 million

4. What are the economic costs of protecting coastal cities?
   A) Only a few million dollars
   B) Only affects small towns
   C) No costs
   D) Tens of trillions of dollars globally

5. What is required to address sea level rise?
   A) Both mitigation and adaptation
   B) Only adaptation
   C) Only mitigation
   D) Ignoring the problem

**Answers:**
1. D - 作者的主要目的是报道几个世纪内不可避免的海平面上升
2. C - 海洋吸收了超过90%的多余热量
3. B - 目前超过6亿人,到2150年可能超过10亿人
4. D - 全球数万亿美元
5. A - 缓解和适应都需要

---

### 翻译

**文章 62. 海平面上升已不可避免**

由于气候系统的热惯性,即使立即停止温室气体排放,海平面上升在未来几个世纪已经不可避免。研究表明,系统中已有的变暖将继续导致冰盖融化和海洋热膨胀,持续数百年到数千年,使未来几代人无论当前的减排努力如何都将面临海平面上升。

驱动长期海平面上升的机制已被充分理解。海洋吸收了温室气体捕获的超过90%的多余热量,这些热量继续导致海水热膨胀。冰盖,特别是格陵兰岛和西南极洲的冰盖,正在以加速的速率响应变暖而融化。即使温度稳定,由于大气变暖与冰盖响应之间的滞后,冰盖也将继续损失质量数百年。研究表明,当前的承诺可能在低排放情景下也会导致未来2000年内海平面上升2-3米。

对沿海社区的后果是严重且持久的。目前超过6亿人生活在中范围海平面上升情景下到2100年将低于年度洪水水平的沿海地区。到2150年,这一数字可能增加到超过10亿。包括迈阿密、上海、孟买、雅加达和纽约在内的沿海城市面临生存威胁。价值数万亿美元的基础设施面临风险,包括港口、机场、道路和废水处理设施。盐水入侵将污染淡水含水层,影响数亿人的饮用水和农业。

经济损失是惊人的。通过海堤、泵站和其他基础设施保护沿海城市全球可能花费数十万亿美元。在某些情况下,有计划的撤退——将社区和基础设施向内陆搬迁——可能是唯一可行的选择。研究表明,不作为的成本远远超过适应的成本,但即使有大量投资,一些损失也是不可避免的。保险市场已经在应对沿海风险方面面临困难,一些房产正在变得不可投保。

应对这一不可避免的现实需要缓解和适应。减排可以限制海平面上升的最终幅度,为社区提供更多的时间和空间来适应。然而,即使采取积极的缓解措施,显著的海平面上升已经锁定。包括沿海保护、土地利用规划和有计划的撤退在内的适应措施必须现在就开始实施,以减少未来风险。研究强调,海平面上升是一个长期挑战,需要跨代人的持续承诺。

**题目翻译:**

1. 作者写这篇文章的主要目的是什么?
   A) 解释如何阻止所有海平面上升
   B) 反对气候行动
   C) 促进沿海开发
   D) 报道几个世纪内不可避免的海平面上升

2. 海洋吸收了多少比例的多余热量?
   A) 10%
   B) 50%
   C) 超过90%
   D) 100%

3. 多少人生活在有风险的沿海地区?
   A) 1亿
   B) 目前超过6亿,到2150年可能超过10亿
   C) 5000万
   D) 1000万

4. 保护沿海城市的经济成本是多少?
   A) 仅几百万美元
   B) 仅影响小城镇
   C) 没有成本
   D) 全球数万亿美元

5. 应对海平面上升需要什么?
   A) 缓解和适应都需要
   B) 仅适应
   C) 仅缓解
   D) 忽视问题

## 文章 63. Ecosystem Services Valued at $125 Trillion

Ecosystem services, the benefits that humans receive from natural ecosystems, are valued at approximately $125 trillion annually, exceeding global GDP by more than 50%. Despite their enormous economic value, these services are often taken for granted and not accounted for in decision-making, leading to their degradation（退化）and loss.

Research has identified multiple categories of ecosystem services. Provisioning（供给）services include food, water, timber（木材）, and fiber. Regulating services include climate regulation, flood control, water purification, and pollination. Supporting services include nutrient cycling, soil formation, and photosynthesis（光合作用）. Cultural services include recreational, aesthetic（美学的）, and spiritual benefits. The research indicates that pollination alone is worth $235-577 billion annually, while coastal wetlands provide storm protection valued at $23 billion per year. Forests regulate water cycles, prevent erosion, and store carbon worth billions.

Despite their value, ecosystem services are declining globally due to habitat destruction, pollution, climate change, and overexploitation（过度开发）. The research shows that approximately 60% of ecosystem services are being degraded or used unsustainably（不可持续地）. This decline has direct economic consequences. When wetlands are destroyed, flood damage increases. When pollinator populations decline, crop yields decrease. When forests are cleared, water regulation fails and erosion increases. The costs of replacing these services with artificial alternatives are often prohibitive（高得令人望而却步的）.

The economic case for conservation is compelling（令人信服的）. Research shows that every dollar invested in ecosystem restoration generates $7-30 in economic benefits through improved ecosystem services. Protecting mangroves（红树林）is far cheaper than building seawalls for coastal protection. Maintaining forested watersheds（流域）is less expensive than building water treatment facilities. The research indicates that incorporating ecosystem service values into decision-making can lead to more efficient and sustainable outcomes.

However, current economic systems fail to account for these values. Markets do not reflect the true cost of ecosystem degradation, leading to overexploitation. Policy reforms are needed to internalize（内部化）these externalities（外部性）through payments for ecosystem services, natural capital accounting, and regulations that protect critical ecosystems. The research underscores that ecosystem services are not just environmental amenities（便利设施）but fundamental economic assets that support human well-being and prosperity. Protecting them is essential for sustainable development.

**Questions:**

1. What is the passage mainly about?
   A) The history of ecosystem discovery
   B) The costs of ecosystem destruction
   C) Ecosystem services valued at $125 trillion annually
   D) Methods for increasing pollution

2. What is the annual value of ecosystem services?
   A) $10 trillion
   B) $125 trillion
   C) $1 trillion
   D) $500 trillion

3. What percentage of ecosystem services are being degraded?
   A) Approximately 60%
   B) 30%
   C) 90%
   D) 10%

4. What is the economic return on ecosystem restoration investment?
   A) $7-30 for every dollar invested
   B) Only $1 return
   C) Negative returns
   D) No return

5. What is needed to protect ecosystem services?
   A) Policy reforms to internalize ecosystem service values
   B) Ignoring their value
   C) Only removing all ecosystems
   D) Only increasing exploitation

**Answers:**
1. C - 文章主要讲述每年价值125万亿美元的生态系统服务
2. B - 125万亿美元
3. A - 约60%
4. A - 每投资1美元产生7-30美元
5. A - 将生态系统服务价值内部化的政策改革

---

### 翻译

**文章 63. 生态系统服务价值125万亿美元**

生态系统服务,即人类从自然生态系统获得的好处,每年价值约125万亿美元,超过全球GDP的50%以上。尽管具有巨大的经济价值,这些服务往往被视为理所当然,在决策中未被计入,导致其退化和丧失。

研究已经确定了生态系统服务的多个类别。供给服务包括食物、水、木材和纤维。调节服务包括气候调节、洪水控制、水净化和授粉。支持服务包括营养循环、土壤形成和光合作用。文化服务包括娱乐、美学和精神效益。研究表明,仅授粉每年就价值2350-5770亿美元,而沿海湿地提供每年价值230亿美元的风暴保护。森林调节水循环,防止侵蚀,并储存价值数十亿美元的碳。

尽管具有价值,生态系统服务正在全球范围内下降,原因是栖息地破坏、污染、气候变化和过度开发。研究表明,约60%的生态系统服务正在退化或被不可持续地使用。这种下降具有直接的经济后果。当湿地被破坏时,洪水损害增加。当传粉者种群下降时,作物产量减少。当森林被清除时,水调节失败,侵蚀增加。用人工替代品替代这些服务的成本往往高得令人望而却步。

保护的经济学理由是令人信服的。研究表明,每投资1美元用于生态系统恢复,通过改善的生态系统服务可产生7-30美元的经济效益。保护红树林比建造海堤进行沿海保护要便宜得多。维持森林流域比建造水处理设施成本更低。研究表明,将生态系统服务价值纳入决策可以带来更高效和可持续的结果。

然而,当前的经济系统未能考虑这些价值。市场没有反映生态系统退化的真实成本,导致过度开发。需要政策改革来通过生态系统服务付费、自然资本核算和保护关键生态系统的法规来内部化这些外部性。研究强调,生态系统服务不仅仅是环境便利设施,而是支持人类福祉和繁荣的基本经济资产。保护它们对可持续发展至关重要。

**题目翻译:**

1. 文章主要讲什么?
   A) 生态系统发现的历史
   B) 生态系统破坏的成本
   C) 每年价值125万亿美元的生态系统服务
   D) 增加污染的方法

2. 生态系统服务的年价值是多少?
   A) 10万亿美元
   B) 125万亿美元
   C) 1万亿美元
   D) 500万亿美元

3. 多少比例的生态系统服务正在退化?
   A) 约60%
   B) 30%
   C) 90%
   D) 10%

4. 生态系统恢复投资的回报率是多少?
   A) 每投资1美元产生7-30美元
   B) 仅1美元回报
   C) 负回报
   D) 没有回报

5. 保护生态系统服务需要什么?
   A) 将生态系统服务价值内部化的政策改革
   B) 忽视其价值
   C) 仅移除所有生态系统
   D) 仅增加开发

## 文章 64. Climate Migration Could Displace 200 Million

Climate migration could displace（使流离失所）200 million people by 2050, creating one of the largest humanitarian（人道主义的）challenges in human history. Research shows that rising sea levels, increasing droughts, more intense storms, and other climate impacts are making some regions uninhabitable（不适宜居住的）, forcing people to move in search of safety and livelihoods（生计）.

The drivers of climate migration are multiple and interconnected. Sea level rise threatens coastal communities and small island nations, with some Pacific islands already planning relocation（搬迁）. Drought and desertification（荒漠化）in regions like the Sahel（萨赫勒地区）, Central America, and South Asia reduce agricultural productivity, making it impossible for farmers to sustain their families. Increasing frequency and intensity of extreme weather events, including hurricanes（飓风）, floods, and wildfires, destroy homes and infrastructure. The research indicates that climate migration is already occurring, with millions displaced annually by climate-related disasters.

The consequences are severe for both origin and destination areas. Communities left behind lose population, economic activity, and cultural heritage. Destination areas face pressure on housing, infrastructure, services, and job markets. Social tensions can arise when large numbers of migrants arrive, particularly if resources are scarce. The research indicates that climate migration disproportionately（不成比例地）affects vulnerable（脆弱的）populations, including the poor, women, children, and indigenous（土著的）communities, exacerbating（加剧）existing inequalities.

The economic costs are substantial. Relocating communities requires massive investment in new infrastructure, housing, and services. Lost agricultural productivity and property values in origin areas reduce economic output. Destination areas must expand capacity to absorb newcomers. The research indicates that the total costs of climate migration could reach trillions of dollars by 2050 if not addressed proactively（主动地）.

Addressing this challenge requires comprehensive strategies. Reducing greenhouse gas emissions can limit the scale of future migration. Adaptation measures in vulnerable areas, including coastal protection, drought-resistant agriculture, and early warning systems, can help people remain in place. For those who must move, planned relocation with adequate support can reduce suffering and costs. International cooperation is essential because climate migration crosses borders and requires coordinated responses. The research underscores that climate migration is not just an environmental issue but a human rights challenge that requires urgent attention and action.

**Questions:**

1. What is the central argument of the passage?
   A) Climate migration is not occurring
   B) Climate migration has no impact
   C) Climate migration only benefits destination areas
   D) Climate migration could displace 200 million people by 2050

2. How many people could be displaced by 2050?
   A) 1 billion
   B) 200 million
   C) 10 million
   D) 50 million

3. What are the main drivers of climate migration?
   A) Only economic opportunities
   B) Only sea level rise
   C) Only political conflicts
   D) Sea level rise, drought, desertification, and extreme weather

4. What are the consequences for destination areas?
   A) Only positive effects
   B) Pressure on housing, infrastructure, services, and job markets
   C) Only benefits
   D) No consequences

5. What is required to address climate migration?
   A) Only removing all vulnerable populations
   B) Comprehensive strategies including emission reduction, adaptation, and planned relocation
   C) Only increasing migration
   D) Ignoring the problem

**Answers:**
1. D - 文章的核心论点是到2050年,气候迁移可能使2亿人流离失所
2. B - 2亿人
3. D - 海平面上升、干旱、荒漠化和极端天气
4. B - 住房、基础设施、服务和就业市场的压力
5. B - 包括减排、适应和计划搬迁的综合策略

---

### 翻译

**文章 64. 气候迁移可能使2亿人流离失所**

到2050年,气候迁移可能使2亿人流离失所,造成人类历史上最大的人道主义挑战之一。研究表明,海平面上升、干旱加剧、风暴更加强烈以及其他气候影响正使一些地区不适宜居住,迫使人们迁移以寻求安全和生计。

气候迁移的驱动因素是多重且相互关联的。海平面上升威胁沿海社区和小岛屿国家,一些太平洋岛屿已经在计划搬迁。萨赫勒地区、中美洲和南亚等地区的干旱和荒漠化降低了农业生产力,使农民无法维持家庭生计。极端天气事件(包括飓风、洪水和野火)的频率和强度增加,摧毁了家园和基础设施。研究表明,气候迁移已经在发生,每年有数百万人因气候相关灾害而流离失所。

对来源地和目的地地区的后果都是严重的。留下的社区失去人口、经济活动和文化遗产。目的地地区面临住房、基础设施、服务和就业市场的压力。当大量移民到达时,特别是如果资源稀缺,可能出现社会紧张。研究表明,气候迁移不成比例地影响脆弱人群,包括穷人、妇女、儿童和土著社区,加剧现有的不平等。

经济损失是巨大的。重新安置社区需要大量投资用于新基础设施、住房和服务。来源地区农业生产力丧失和财产价值下降减少了经济产出。目的地地区必须扩大容量以吸收新来者。研究表明,如果不主动应对,到2050年气候迁移的总成本可能达到数万亿美元。

应对这一挑战需要综合策略。减少温室气体排放可以限制未来迁移的规模。脆弱地区的适应措施,包括沿海保护、抗旱农业和预警系统,可以帮助人们留在原地。对于必须迁移的人,有充分支持的计划搬迁可以减少痛苦和成本。国际合作至关重要,因为气候迁移跨越边界,需要协调一致的应对措施。研究强调,气候迁移不仅仅是一个环境问题,而是一个需要紧急关注和行动的人权挑战。

**题目翻译:**

1. 文章的核心论点是什么?
   A) 气候迁移没有发生
   B) 气候迁移没有影响
   C) 气候迁移只对目的地有利
   D) 到2050年,气候迁移可能使2亿人流离失所

2. 到2050年可能有多少人流离失所?
   A) 10亿
   B) 2亿
   C) 1000万
   D) 5000万

3. 气候迁移的主要驱动因素是什么?
   A) 仅经济机会
   B) 仅海平面上升
   C) 仅政治冲突
   D) 海平面上升、干旱、荒漠化和极端天气

4. 对目的地地区的后果是什么?
   A) 只有积极影响
   B) 住房、基础设施、服务和就业市场的压力
   C) 只有好处
   D) 没有后果

5. 应对气候迁移需要什么?
   A) 仅移除所有脆弱人群
   B) 包括减排、适应和计划搬迁的综合策略
   C) 仅增加迁移
   D) 忽视问题

## 文章 65. Ocean Acoustic Pollution Threatens Marine Life

Ocean acoustic（声学的）pollution, the increasing noise from human activities in marine environments, is threatening marine life that depends on sound for communication, navigation, and survival. Research shows that noise levels in the ocean have increased dramatically over the past century, with shipping, seismic（地震的）surveys, military sonar（声纳）, and construction activities creating a cacophony（刺耳的噪音）that interferes with marine species.

Marine animals rely on sound in ways that humans cannot fully imagine. Whales communicate across entire ocean basins using low-frequency calls that travel thousands of kilometers. Dolphins and porpoises（鼠海豚）use echolocation（回声定位）to navigate and hunt in dark waters. Many fish species use sound to find mates, defend territories（领地）, and avoid predators. The research indicates that excessive noise can mask（掩盖）these critical acoustic signals, reducing communication range, disrupting behaviors, and causing stress. In severe cases, loud noises can cause physical damage to hearing organs and even death.

The consequences are severe for many species. Research has linked naval sonar to mass strandings（搁浅）of beaked whales（喙鲸）, which panic and surface too quickly when exposed to mid-frequency active sonar. Shipping noise has been shown to reduce communication range for right whales by over 80%, making it harder for them to find mates. Seismic airguns（气枪）used in oil and gas exploration can disturb fish behavior and reduce catch rates for commercial fisheries. The research indicates that chronic（慢性的）noise exposure can cause long-term stress, reduced reproduction, and population declines.

The problem is growing as ocean activities expand. Shipping traffic is increasing with global trade. Oil and gas exploration is moving into deeper waters. Offshore wind farms, while beneficial for climate, create noise during construction. The research indicates that without mitigation（缓解措施）, ocean noise could double or triple by 2050 in some regions, with devastating consequences for marine ecosystems.

Solutions exist to reduce ocean acoustic pollution. Slower shipping speeds can reduce noise significantly. Quieter ship designs and propellers（螺旋桨）can minimize sound generation. Seasonal restrictions on noisy activities can protect sensitive periods like breeding and migration. Marine protected areas can provide quiet refuges. The research shows that these measures can be effective without significantly impacting economic activities. However, implementation requires international cooperation and regulatory（监管的）frameworks. The research underscores that ocean sound is a critical but often overlooked component of marine ecosystems that requires protection.

**Questions:**

1. What is the author's primary purpose in writing this passage?
   A) To report on ocean acoustic pollution threatening marine life
   B) To explain how to increase shipping noise
   C) To promote ocean noise
   D) To argue against marine conservation

2. How do marine animals use sound?
   A) For communication, navigation, hunting, and finding mates
   B) Only for warning predators
   C) They don't use sound
   D) Only for entertainment

3. What has research linked naval sonar to?
   A) Mass strandings of beaked whales
   B) Better navigation
   C) Increased fish populations
   D) Improved whale communication

4. What solutions exist to reduce ocean noise?
   A) Only removing all marine life
   B) No solutions
   C) Slower shipping speeds, quieter ship designs, seasonal restrictions, and marine protected areas
   D) Only increasing noise

5. What does the research underscore about ocean sound?
   A) It is a critical but often overlooked component of marine ecosystems
   B) It is not important
   C) It only affects humans
   D) It has no effect on marine life

**Answers:**
1. A - 作者的主要目的是报道威胁海洋生物的海洋声学污染
2. A - 用于交流、导航、狩猎和寻找配偶
3. A - 喙鲸的大规模搁浅
4. C - 降低航运速度、更安静的船舶设计、季节性限制和海洋保护区
5. A - 它是海洋生态系统的关键但常被忽视的组成部分

---

### 翻译

**文章 65. 海洋声学污染威胁海洋生物**

海洋声学污染,即海洋环境中人类活动产生的日益增加的噪音,正威胁着依赖声音进行交流、导航和生存海洋生物。研究表明,在过去一个世纪里,海洋中的噪音水平急剧增加,航运、地震勘探、军事声纳和建筑活动产生的刺耳噪音干扰了海洋物种。

海洋动物以人类无法完全想象的方式依赖声音。鲸鱼使用低频叫声在整个海洋盆地进行交流,叫声可以传播数千公里。海豚和鼠海豚使用回声定位在黑暗的水域中导航和捕猎。许多鱼类物种使用声音寻找配偶、保卫领地和避免捕食者。研究表明,过大的噪音可以掩盖这些关键的声学信号,减少交流范围,扰乱行为,并造成压力。在严重的情况下,巨大的噪音可以对听觉器官造成物理损伤甚至导致死亡。

对许多物种来说,后果是严重的。研究已将海军声纳与喙鲸的大规模搁浅联系起来,这些鲸鱼在暴露于中频主动声纳时会恐慌并过快浮出水面。航运噪音已被证明使右鲸的交流范围减少超过80%,使它们更难找到配偶。用于石油和天然气勘探的地震气枪可以扰乱鱼类行为并降低商业渔业的捕获率。研究表明,长期噪音暴露会导致长期压力、繁殖减少和种群下降。

随着海洋活动的扩大,问题正在加剧。随着全球贸易的增长,航运交通正在增加。石油和天然气勘探正在向更深的水域移动。海上风电场虽然对气候有益,但在建设期间会产生噪音。研究表明,如果不采取缓解措施,到2050年某些地区的海洋噪音可能翻倍或三倍,对海洋生态系统造成毁灭性后果。

存在减少海洋声学污染的解决方案。降低航运速度可以显著减少噪音。更安静的船舶设计和螺旋桨可以最小化声音产生。对嘈杂活动的季节性限制可以保护繁殖和迁徙等敏感时期。海洋保护区可以提供安静的避难所。研究表明,这些措施可以在不显著影响经济活动的情况下有效。然而,实施需要国际合作和监管框架。研究强调,海洋声音是海洋生态系统的一个关键但常被忽视的组成部分,需要保护。

**题目翻译:**

1. 作者写这篇文章的主要目的是什么?
   A) 报道威胁海洋生物的海洋声学污染
   B) 解释如何增加航运噪音
   C) 促进海洋噪音
   D) 反对海洋保护

2. 海洋动物如何使用声音?
   A) 用于交流、导航、捕猎和寻找配偶
   B) 仅用于警告捕食者
   C) 它们不使用声音
   D) 仅用于娱乐

3. 研究已将海军声纳与什么联系起来?
   A) 喙鲸的大规模搁浅
   B) 更好的导航
   C) 鱼类种群增加
   D) 改善的鲸鱼交流

4. 存在哪些减少海洋噪音的解决方案?
   A) 仅移除所有海洋生物
   B) 没有解决方案
   C) 降低航运速度、更安静的船舶设计、季节性限制和海洋保护区
   D) 仅增加噪音

5. 研究关于海洋声音强调了什么?
   A) 它是海洋生态系统的关键但常被忽视的组成部分
   B) 它不重要
   C) 它仅影响人类
   D) 它对海洋生物没有影响

## 文章 66. Tipping Points in Ice Sheets

Tipping points in ice sheets could trigger rapid sea level rise that would be difficult or impossible to reverse, posing existential（生存威胁的）threats to coastal communities worldwide. Research shows that both the Greenland and West Antarctic ice sheets contain thresholds（阈值）beyond which melting becomes self-sustaining and continues even if global warming is stopped.

The mechanisms driving ice sheet tipping points are well understood. For Greenland, surface melting creates pools of water on the ice surface that drain through cracks, lubricating（润滑）the base and accelerating ice flow to the ocean. As melting increases, the ice sheet surface lowers into warmer air, creating a positive feedback loop. Research indicates that Greenland could pass its tipping point with 1.5-2 degrees of warming, which we may have already committed to. For West Antarctica, warming ocean waters melt ice shelves from below, removing the buttresses（支撑物）that hold back glaciers. Once these shelves collapse, glaciers flow faster into the ocean in a process that becomes self-sustaining.

The consequences of crossing these thresholds would be severe. Complete melting of Greenland would raise sea levels by 7.4 meters over centuries. West Antarctic collapse would add 3.3 meters. Even partial melting would have devastating effects on coastal cities. Miami, Shanghai, Mumbai, Bangkok, and hundreds of other cities would face permanent inundation（淹没）. Over 600 million people live in areas that would be below annual flood levels. The economic costs would be staggering, with trillions of dollars in infrastructure at risk.

The research indicates that we may be approaching these tipping points sooner than previously thought. Current observations show that both ice sheets are losing mass at accelerating rates, with some glaciers in West Antarctica already showing signs of irreversible retreat. Climate models that incorporate these feedbacks suggest that tipping points could be crossed within decades under current emission scenarios.

Avoiding these catastrophic outcomes requires immediate and drastic reduction in greenhouse gas emissions. The Paris Agreement targets were designed to stay below most ice sheet tipping points, but current policies put the world on track for 2.5-3 degrees of warming. The research underscores that ice sheet stability is not guaranteed and that urgent action is needed to preserve coastal communities and the civilizations they support.

**Questions:**

1. What is the passage mainly about?
   A) Tipping points in ice sheets that could trigger rapid sea level rise
   B) The benefits of ice sheet melting
   C) The history of ice sheet discovery
   D) Methods for increasing ice sheet size

2. What could happen if Greenland passes its tipping point?
   A) Sea level drop
   B) Only local effects
   C) Sea level rise of 7.4 meters over centuries
   D) No significant effects

3. What drives West Antarctic ice sheet instability?
   A) Only snow accumulation
   B) Only wind
   C) Warming ocean waters melting ice shelves from below
   D) Only atmospheric warming

4. How many people live in areas at risk?
   A) Over 600 million
   B) 10 million
   C) 1 billion
   D) 100 million

5. What is required to avoid catastrophic outcomes?
   A) Increasing greenhouse gas emissions
   B) Ignoring the problem
   C) Removing all ice sheets
   D) Immediate and drastic reduction in greenhouse gas emissions

**Answers:**
1. A - 文章主要讲述可能引发快速海平面上升的冰盖临界点
2. C - 几个世纪内海平面上升7.4米
3. C - 变暖的海水从下方融化冰架
4. A - 超过6亿人
5. D - 立即大幅减少温室气体排放

---

### 翻译

**文章 66. 冰盖临界点**

冰盖临界点可能引发难以或不可能逆转的快速海平面上升,对全球沿海社区构成生存威胁。研究表明,格陵兰岛和西南极洲冰盖都包含阈值,超过这些阈值后,融化变得自我维持,即使停止全球变暖也会继续。

驱动冰盖临界点的机制已被充分理解。对于格陵兰岛,表面融化在冰面上形成水池,水池通过裂缝排出,润滑底部并加速冰流向海洋。随着融化增加,冰盖表面降低到更温暖的空气中,形成正反馈循环。研究表明,格陵兰岛可能在1.5-2度变暖时通过其临界点,而我们可能已经承诺了这一点。对于西南极洲,变暖的海水从下方融化冰架,移除了支撑冰川的支墩。一旦这些冰架崩塌,冰川以自我维持的过程更快地流入海洋。

通过这些阈值的后果将是严重的。格陵兰岛的完全融化将在几个世纪内使海平面上升7.4米。西南极洲崩塌将增加3.3米。即使是部分融化也会对沿海城市造成毁灭性影响。迈阿密、上海、孟买、曼谷和数百个其他城市将面临永久淹没。超过6亿人生活在低于年度洪水水平的地区。经济成本将是惊人的,数万亿美元的基础设施面临风险。

研究表明,我们可能比之前认为的更快接近这些临界点。当前观测表明,两个冰盖都在以加速的速率损失质量,西南极洲的一些冰川已经显示出不可逆转退缩的迹象。纳入这些反馈的气候模型表明,在当前排放情景下,临界点可能在几十年内被跨越。

避免这些灾难性后果需要立即和大幅度减少温室气体排放。巴黎协定目标旨在保持在大多数冰盖临界点以下,但当前政策使世界走上2.5-3度变暖的道路。研究强调,冰盖稳定性不是有保证的,需要紧急行动来保护沿海社区及其支持的文明。

**题目翻译:**

1. 文章主要讲什么?
   A) 可能引发快速海平面上升的冰盖临界点
   B) 冰盖融化的好处
   C) 冰盖发现的历史
   D) 增加冰盖大小的方法

2. 如果格陵兰岛通过其临界点会发生什么?
   A) 海平面下降
   B) 仅局部影响
   C) 几个世纪内海平面上升7.4米
   D) 没有显著影响

3. 什么驱动西南极洲冰盖不稳定?
   A) 仅积雪
   B) 仅风
   C) 变暖的海水从下方融化冰架
   D) 仅大气变暖

4. 多少人生活在有风险的地区?
   A) 超过6亿
   B) 1000万
   C) 10亿
   D) 1亿

5. 避免灾难性后果需要什么?
   A) 增加温室气体排放
   B) 忽视问题
   C) 移除所有冰盖
   D) 立即大幅减少温室气体排放
