## 文章 83. Deforestation Accelerates in Tropical Regions

Tropical deforestation is accelerating despite decades of international commitments (承诺) to halt (停止) forest loss, with the tropics losing 4.1 million hectares (公顷) of primary forest in 2022—an area larger than Switzerland. Primary forests, which have grown undisturbed (未受干扰的) for centuries, store enormous amounts of carbon and harbor (孕育) the majority of the world's terrestrial (陆地的) biodiversity (生物多样性). Their destruction releases approximately 2.6 billion tons of carbon dioxide annually, equivalent to the emissions from 600 million cars, making tropical deforestation one of the largest sources of global greenhouse gas emissions.

The drivers (驱动因素) are primarily economic and agricultural. Cattle ranching (牧场经营) accounts for the largest share of deforestation in South America, particularly in the Amazon basin (流域) where Brazil has lost approximately 17% of its forest cover. In Southeast Asia, palm oil plantations (种植园) drive forest clearance (清理) across Indonesia and Malaysia. Africa's Congo Basin faces increasing pressure from subsistence (自给自足的) agriculture, charcoal (木炭) production, and mining. Infrastructure projects including roads, dams (水坝), and mines open previously inaccessible (难以到达的) areas to further exploitation (开发). The research shows that deforestation rates increase during periods of weak governance (治理) and economic pressure, suggesting that political commitment is essential for forest protection.

The consequences extend far beyond carbon emissions. Tropical forests are home to approximately 80% of terrestrial biodiversity, and their destruction drives species extinctions at unprecedented (前所未有的) rates. Indigenous (土著的) communities who have managed these forests for thousands of years lose their livelihoods (生计) and cultural heritage (遗产). Forests also regulate (调节) regional rainfall patterns, and their loss can lead to reduced agricultural productivity and water scarcity in surrounding areas. The research indicates that continued deforestation could push some tropical forests past tipping points where they transition to savanna-like ecosystems (生态系统).

Solutions require addressing root causes. Strengthening land tenure (保有权) rights for indigenous communities has proven effective, as these groups manage forests more sustainably than other land users. Payment for ecosystem services programs compensate (补偿) landowners for preserving forests. Satellite monitoring enables rapid detection of illegal clearing. Supply chain regulations that prohibit (禁止) imports of commodities (商品) produced on deforested land can reduce market incentives. The research underscores (强调) that protecting tropical forests is one of the most cost-effective climate solutions available, requiring coordinated action from governments, businesses, and consumers to preserve these critical ecosystems for future generations.

**Questions:**

1. What is the passage mainly concerned with?
   A) How deforestation is accelerating in tropical regions
   B) Methods for increasing forest loss
   C) The history of forest conservation
   D) The benefits of tropical deforestation

2. How much primary forest was lost in 2022?
   A) 1 million hectares
   B) 10 million hectares
   C) 2.5 million hectares
   D) 4.1 million hectares

3. What is the largest driver of deforestation in South America?
   A) Mining
   B) Palm oil plantations
   C) Cattle ranching
   D) Urban development

4. What percentage of terrestrial biodiversity do tropical forests harbor?
   A) 95%
   B) 50%
   C) 30%
   D) 80%

5. What has proven effective for forest protection?
   A) Strengthening land tenure rights for indigenous communities
   B) Ignoring illegal clearing
   C) Removing indigenous communities
   D) Increasing deforestation

**Answers:**
1. A - 文章主要关注热带地区的森林砍伐如何加速
2. D - 410万公顷
3. C - 养牛业
4. D - 80%
5. A - 加强原住民社区的土地权

---

### 翻译

**文章 83. 热带地区森林砍伐加速**

尽管国际社会数十年来承诺停止森林丧失,热带森林砍伐仍在加速,2022年热带地区损失了410万公顷原始森林——比瑞士面积还大。原始森林数百年来未受干扰地生长,储存大量碳并孕育了世界大部分陆地生物多样性。它们的破坏每年释放约26亿吨二氧化碳,相当于6亿辆汽车的排放,使热带森林砍伐成为全球温室气体排放的最大来源之一。

驱动因素主要是经济和农业性的。养牛业占南美洲森林砍伐的最大份额,特别是在亚马逊流域,巴西已损失约17%的森林覆盖。在东南亚,棕榈油种植园推动印度尼西亚和马来西亚的森林清理。非洲刚果盆地面临来自自给农业、木炭生产和采矿的日益增加的压力。包括道路、水坝和矿山在内的基础设施项目使以前难以到达的地区面临进一步开发。研究表明,在治理薄弱和经济压力时期,森林砍伐率增加,表明政治承诺对森林保护至关重要。

后果远远超出碳排放。热带森林是约80%陆地生物多样性的家园,它们的破坏以前所未有的速率推动物种灭绝。数千年来管理这些森林的土著社区失去了他们的生计和文化遗产。森林还调节区域降雨模式,它们的丧失可能导致周边地区农业生产力下降和水资源短缺。研究表明,持续的森林砍伐可能将一些热带森林推过临界点,使其过渡到类似稀树草原的生态系统。

解决方案需要解决根本原因。加强土著社区的土地权已被证明有效,因为这些群体比其他土地使用者更可持续地管理森林。生态系统服务付费计划补偿土地所有者保护森林。卫星监测使非法清理能够快速检测。禁止在砍伐土地上生产的商品进口的供应链法规可以减少市场激励。研究强调,保护热带森林是现有的最具成本效益的气候解决方案之一,需要政府、企业和消费者的协调行动,为子孙后代保护这些关键生态系统。

**题目翻译:**

1. 文章主要关注什么?
   A) 热带地区的森林砍伐如何加速
   B) 增加森林丧失的方法
   C) 森林保护的历史
   D) 热带森林砍伐的好处

2. 2022年损失了多少原始森林?
   A) 100万公顷
   B) 1000万公顷
   C) 250万公顷
   D) 410万公顷

3. 南美洲森林砍伐的最大驱动因素是什么?
   A) 采矿
   B) 棕榈油种植园
   C) 养牛业
   D) 城市发展

4. 热带森林孕育了多少比例的陆地生物多样性?
   A) 95%
   B) 50%
   C) 30%
   D) 80%

5. 什么对森林保护被证明有效?
   A) 加强原住民社区的土地权
   B) 忽视非法清理
   C) 移除原住民社区
   D) 增加森林砍伐

## 文章 84. Water Scarcity Affects Billions Worldwide

Water scarcity (短缺) is becoming one of the most pressing (紧迫的) challenges of the 21st century, with approximately 2.2 billion people currently lacking access to safely managed drinking water services. The United Nations projects that by 2030, global water demand will exceed supply by 40%, creating severe shortages for agriculture, industry, and human consumption (消耗). The crisis is not limited to arid (干旱的) regions—water-stressed areas now span every continent, affecting both developing and developed nations as population growth, urbanization (城市化), and climate change intensify (加剧) pressure on freshwater resources.

The causes are multifaceted (多方面的). Agriculture consumes approximately 70% of global freshwater withdrawals (取水量), and inefficient irrigation (灌溉) practices waste enormous quantities. Industrial processes and energy production require vast amounts of water for cooling and manufacturing. Population growth increases demand in regions where water resources are already limited. Climate change alters precipitation (降水) patterns, reducing snowpack (积雪) that feeds rivers during dry seasons and increasing the frequency and severity (严重程度) of droughts (干旱). Pollution from agricultural runoff (径流), industrial discharge (排放), and untreated sewage (污水) contaminates (污染) available water supplies, making them unsafe for human use.

The consequences are devastating (毁灭性的) and far-reaching (深远的). Water-borne diseases including cholera (霍乱), typhoid (伤寒), and dysentery (痢疾) kill approximately 485,000 people annually, mostly children under five. Women and girls in developing countries spend an estimated 200 million hours daily collecting water, limiting their opportunities for education and economic participation. Agriculture suffers when irrigation water is unavailable, threatening food security for millions. Ecosystems dependent on freshwater—wetlands, rivers, and lakes—degrade (退化), reducing biodiversity and the services they provide.

Addressing water scarcity requires integrated (综合的) approaches. Improving irrigation efficiency through drip systems can reduce agricultural water use by 30-60%. Wastewater treatment and recycling (回收利用) provide alternative water sources. Rainwater harvesting (收集) captures precipitation for local use. Desalination (海水淡化) technology is becoming more energy-efficient and affordable for coastal communities. Policy reforms including water pricing, allocation (分配) systems, and conservation incentives can reduce waste. The research shows that with proper management and investment, water scarcity can be mitigated (缓解), but requires immediate action to ensure sustainable water resources for the growing global population.

**Questions:**

1. What does the passage mainly discuss?
   A) How water scarcity affects billions of people worldwide
   B) The history of water treatment
   C) Methods for wasting water resources
   D) The abundance of global water resources

2. How many people lack access to safely managed drinking water?
   A) 5 billion
   B) 1 billion
   C) 500 million
   D) 2.2 billion

3. What percentage of global freshwater does agriculture consume?
   A) 90%
   B) 30%
   C) 50%
   D) 70%

4. How many people die annually from water-borne diseases?
   A) 485,000
   B) 1 million
   C) 100,000
   D) 250,000

5. What can reduce agricultural water use?
   A) Flood irrigation
   B) Removing all irrigation
   C) Only increasing water supply
   D) Drip irrigation systems

**Answers:**
1. A - 文章主要讨论水资源短缺如何影响全球数十亿人
2. D - 22亿
3. D - 70%
4. A - 48.5万
5. D - 滴灌系统

---

### 翻译

**文章 84. 水资源短缺影响全球数十亿人**

水资源短缺正在成为21世纪最紧迫的挑战之一,目前约22亿人无法获得安全管理的饮用水服务。联合国预测,到2030年,全球水需求将超过供应40%,为农业、工业和人类消费造成严重短缺。危机不仅限于干旱地区——水资源紧张的地区现在跨越每个大陆,随着人口增长、城市化和气候变化加剧对淡水资源的压力,影响发展中国家和发达国家。

原因是多方面的。农业消耗约70%的全球淡水取水量,低效的灌溉实践浪费大量水。工业过程和能源生产需要大量水用于冷却和制造。人口增长在水资源已经有限的地区增加需求。气候变化改变降水模式,减少在旱季补给河流的积雪,增加干旱的频率和严重程度。来自农业径流、工业排放和未处理污水的污染污染可用的水供应,使其不安全供人类使用。

后果是毁灭性且深远的。包括霍乱、伤寒和痢疾在内的水传播疾病每年导致约48.5万人死亡,大多是五岁以下儿童。发展中国家的妇女和女孩每天估计花费2亿小时收集水,限制了她们的教育和经济参与机会。当灌溉水不可用时,农业受到影响,威胁数百万人的粮食安全。依赖淡水的生态系统——湿地、河流和湖泊——退化,减少生物多样性及其提供的服务。

解决水资源短缺需要综合方法。通过滴灌系统提高灌溉效率可以减少30-60%的农业用水。废水处理和回收提供替代水源。雨水收集捕获降水供当地使用。海水淡化技术对沿海社区变得更加节能和负担得起。包括水定价、分配系统和保护激励在内的政策改革可以减少浪费。研究表明,通过适当的管理和投资,水资源短缺可以得到缓解,但需要立即行动以确保不断增长的世界人口的可持续水资源。

**题目翻译:**

1. 文章主要讨论什么?
   A) 水资源短缺如何影响全球数十亿人
   B) 水处理的历叐
   C) 浪费水资源的方法
   D) 全球水资源的丰富

2. 多少人无法获得安全管理的饮用水?
   A) 50亿
   B) 10亿
   C) 5亿
   D) 22亿

3. 农业消耗全球淡水的多少比例?
   A) 90%
   B) 30%
   C) 50%
   D) 70%

4. 水传播疾病每年导致多少人死亡?
   A) 48.5万
   B) 100万
   C) 10万
   D) 25万

5. 什么可以减少农业用水?
   A) 洪水灌溉
   B) 移除所有灌溉
   C) 仅增加供水
   D) 滴灌系统

## 文章 85. Artificial Intelligence Transforms Healthcare

Artificial intelligence is revolutionizing (彻底改变) healthcare by improving diagnosis (诊断) accuracy, accelerating drug discovery, and enabling personalized (个性化的) treatment plans. Machine learning algorithms (算法) can now analyze medical images—X-rays, MRIs, and CT scans—with accuracy equaling or exceeding that of experienced radiologists (放射科医生). In some applications, AI systems detect early-stage cancers, diabetic retinopathy (视网膜病变), and heart abnormalities that human doctors might miss. A study published in Nature demonstrated that an AI system could identify breast cancer from mammograms (乳房X光片) with 11.5% fewer false positives and 9.4% fewer false negatives than radiologists.

Drug development is being transformed by AI's ability to analyze vast datasets and identify promising compounds (化合物). Traditional drug discovery takes 10-15 years and costs over $2 billion per approved medication. AI can screen millions of molecular structures in days, predicting which compounds are most likely to be effective and safe. During the COVID-19 pandemic, AI accelerated the identification of existing drugs that could be repurposed (重新利用) to treat the virus. Pharmaceutical (制药的) companies are now using AI throughout the development pipeline, from target identification to clinical trial (临床试验) design, potentially reducing development time by several years.

Personalized medicine represents another frontier. By analyzing patients' genetic information, lifestyle factors, and medical history, AI can recommend treatments tailored to individual characteristics. This approach is particularly promising in oncology (肿瘤学), where tumors' genetic profiles guide the selection of targeted therapies. AI-powered wearable devices continuously monitor vital signs, alerting patients and doctors to potential health problems before symptoms appear. The technology can predict heart attacks, strokes, and diabetic episodes hours before they occur, enabling preventive intervention (干预).

Challenges must be addressed to realize AI's full potential. Data privacy concerns require robust (稳健的) security measures and clear regulations. Algorithmic bias (算法偏见) can perpetuate (使永久化) health disparities (差距) if training data does not represent diverse populations. Integration with existing healthcare systems and workflows needs careful planning. Doctors require training to interpret AI recommendations and maintain appropriate oversight (监督). The research indicates that AI will not replace physicians but rather augment (增强) their capabilities, creating a more efficient, accurate, and accessible healthcare system that can better serve patients worldwide.

**Questions:**

1. What is the passage mainly about?
   A) How artificial intelligence is transforming healthcare
   B) Methods for increasing drug development costs
   C) The history of medical imaging
   D) The limitations of AI in healthcare

2. How does AI compare to radiologists in analyzing medical images?
   A) AI accuracy equals or exceeds that of experienced radiologists
   B) AI is significantly worse
   C) Radiologists are always more accurate
   D) AI cannot analyze images

3. How long does traditional drug development take?
   A) 10-15 years
   B) 5-7 years
   C) 20-25 years
   D) 1-2 years

4. What can AI-powered wearable devices do?
   A) Only count steps
   B) Replace doctors entirely
   C) Only measure blood pressure
   D) Predict heart attacks, strokes, and diabetic episodes before they occur

5. What challenges must be addressed for AI in healthcare?
   A) Data privacy, algorithmic bias, system integration, and doctor training
   B) No challenges exist
   C) Only high costs
   D) Only patient acceptance

**Answers:**
1. A - 文章主要讲述人工智能如何改变医疗保健
2. A - AI准确性等于或超过经验丰富的放射科医生
3. A - 10到15年
4. D - 在心脏病发作、中风和糖尿病发作前预测
5. A - 数据隐私、算法偏见、系统集成和医生培训

---

### 翻译

**文章 85. 人工智能改变医疗保健**

人工智能正在通过提高诊断准确性、加速药物发现和实现个性化治疗计划来彻底改变医疗保健。机器学习算法现在可以分析医学图像——X光、MRI和CT扫描——准确性等于或超过经验丰富的放射科医生。在一些应用中,AI系统检测人类医生可能遗漏的早期癌症、糖尿病视网膜病变和心脏异常。发表在《自然》上的一项研究表明,AI系统可以从乳房X光片中识别乳腺癌,假阳性比放射科医生少11.5%,假阴性少9.4%。

药物开发正在被AI分析大量数据集和识别有前途的化合物的能力所改变。传统药物发现需要10-15年,每种批准的药物成本超过20亿美元。AI可以在几天内筛选数百万种分子结构,预测哪些化合物最可能有效和安全。在COVID-19大流行期间,AI加速了识别可以重新利用来治疗病毒的现有药物。制药公司现在在整个开发管线中使用AI,从靶点识别到临床试验设计,可能将开发时间缩短数年。

个性化医学代表了另一个前沿。通过分析患者的遗传信息、生活方式因素和病史,AI可以推荐针对个体特征定制的治疗。这种方法在肿瘤学中特别有前途,肿瘤的基因谱指导靶向治疗的选择。AI驱动的可穿戴设备持续监测生命体征,在症状出现之前向患者和医生提醒潜在健康问题。该技术可以在心脏病发作、中风和糖尿病发作前数小时预测,实现预防性干预。

必须解决挑战才能实现AI的全部潜力。数据隐私问题需要稳健的安全措施和明确的法规。如果训练数据不代表多样化的人群,算法偏见可能使健康差距永久化。与现有医疗系统和工作流程的整合需要仔细规划。医生需要培训来解读AI建议并维持适当的监督。研究表明,AI不会取代医生,而是增强他们的能力,创造一个更高效、准确和可及的医疗系统,更好地服务全球患者。

**题目翻译:**

1. 文章主要讲什么?
   A) 人工智能如何改变医疗保健
   B) 增加药物开发成本的方法
   C) 医学成像的历史
   D) AI在医疗保健中的局限性

2. AI在分析医学图像方面与放射科医生相比如何?
   A) AI准确性等于或超过经验丰富的放射科医生
   B) AI明显更差
   C) 放射科医生总是更准确
   D) AI不能分析图像

3. 传统药物开发需要多长时间?
   A) 10到15年
   B) 5到7年
   C) 20到25年
   D) 1到2年

4. AI驱动的可穿戴设备能做什么?
   A) 仅计步
   B) 完全取代医生
   C) 仅测量血压
   D) 在心脏病发作、中风和糖尿病发作前预测

5. AI在医疗保健中必须解决什么挑战?
   A) 数据隐私、算法偏见、系统集成和医生培训
   B) 没有挑战
   C) 仅高成本
   D) 仅患者接受度

---

## 文章 86. Plastic Pollution Chokes Ocean Ecosystems

Every year, approximately 8 million metric tons of plastic waste enter the world's oceans, equivalent to dumping a garbage truck of plastic every minute. This relentless（无情的）influx（涌入）has created massive accumulation（积聚）zones, the largest being the Great Pacific Garbage Patch, which now spans an area three times the size of France. But the visible debris（碎片）represents only a fraction of the problem—microplastics, fragments smaller than five millimeters, have permeated（渗透）every level of marine ecosystems, from surface waters to the deepest ocean trenches（海沟）.

Marine life bears the brunt of this contamination（污染）. Over 700 species, including sea turtles, seabirds, whales, and fish, are known to ingest（摄入）or become entangled（缠绕）in plastic debris. Sea turtles mistake plastic bags for jellyfish, their primary food source. Seabirds feed plastic fragments to their chicks, filling their stomachs until they starve. Whales have been found dead with dozens of kilograms of plastic in their digestive systems. Beyond physical harm, plastics absorb toxic chemicals from seawater, and when ingested, these poisons accumulate in animal tissues, eventually entering the human food chain through seafood consumption.

The sources of ocean plastic are diverse but identifiable. Rivers carry approximately 80% of land-based plastic waste to the sea, with just twenty rivers—mostly in Asia and Africa—accounting for two-thirds of the total. Single-use packaging, disposable bottles, and synthetic（合成的）textiles（纺织品）released during washing contribute heavily. Once in the ocean, plastics do not biodegrade（生物降解）; they merely break into smaller pieces that persist for centuries.

Solutions demand action at multiple points along the chain. Upstream（上游）, reducing plastic production and designing products for reuse rather than disposal addresses the root cause. Midstream（中游）, improved waste collection and recycling infrastructure in developing nations can intercept（拦截）waste before it reaches waterways. Downstream（下游）, cleanup technologies like floating barriers and river interceptors（拦截器）remove existing debris. International agreements, including the UN Global Plastics Treaty currently under negotiation, aim to establish binding（有约束力的）targets for reduction. Research consistently shows that without systemic（系统性的）change in how we produce and manage plastic, ocean contamination will triple by 2040, with catastrophic（灾难性的）consequences for marine biodiversity（生物多样性）and human health.

**Questions:**

1. What is the passage primarily concerned with?
   A) How plastic pollution devastates ocean ecosystems
   B) Methods for increasing ocean waste
   C) The history of garbage collection
   D) The benefits of plastic production

2. How much plastic waste enters oceans annually?
   A) 1 million metric tons
   B) 100 million metric tons
   C) 8 million metric tons
   D) 50 million metric tons

3. What percentage of land-based plastic waste is carried to the sea by rivers?
   A) 95%
   B) 50%
   C) 20%
   D) 80%

4. How many marine species are affected by plastic debris?
   A) Over 1,000
   B) Over 300
   C) Over 100
   D) Over 700

5. What is identified as the root solution to ocean plastic pollution?
   A) Only cleanup technologies
   B) Reducing plastic production and designing products for reuse
   C) Ignoring the problem
   D) Only international agreements

**Answers:**
1. A - 文章主要关注塑料污染如何破坏海洋生态系统
2. C - 800万公吨
3. D - 80%
4. D - 超过700种
5. B - 减少塑料生产并设计可重复使用的产品

---

### 翻译

**文章 86. 塑料污染窒息海洋生态系统**

每年约800万公吨的塑料废物进入世界海洋,相当于每分钟倾倒一卡车塑料。这种无情的涌入创造了巨大的积聚区,最大的是大太平洋垃圾带,现在覆盖的面积是法国的三倍。但可见碎片仅代表问题的一小部分——微塑料,小于五毫米的碎片,已渗透到海洋生态系统的各个层面,从表层水到最深的海沟。

海洋生物承受了这种污染的沉重负担。已知超过700种物种,包括海龟、海鸟、鲸鱼和鱼类,摄入或被塑料碎片缠绕。海龟将塑料袋误认为它们的主要食物来源水母。海鸟将塑料碎片喂给雏鸟,填满它们的胃直到它们饿死。鲸鱼被发现死亡时消化系统中有数十公斤塑料。除了物理伤害外,塑料从海水中吸收有毒化学品,当被摄入时,这些毒素在动物组织中积累,最终通过海鲜消费进入人类食物链。

海洋塑料的来源是多样但可识别的。河流将约80%的陆地塑料废物带入海洋,仅二十条河流——主要在亚洲和非洲——占总量的三分之二。一次性包装、一次性瓶子和洗涤时释放的合成纺织品贡献巨大。一旦进入海洋,塑料不会生物降解;它们只是分解成持续数百年的更小碎片。

解决方案需要在链条的多个点采取行动。在上游,减少塑料生产和设计可重复使用而非丢弃的产品解决了根本原因。在中游,发展中国家改善的废物收集和回收基础设施可以在废物到达水道之前拦截它。在下游,浮动屏障和河流拦截器等清理技术清除现有碎片。包括目前正在谈判的联合国全球塑料条约在内的国际协议旨在建立有约束力的减少目标。研究一致表明,如果不在塑料生产和管理方式上进行系统性改变,到2040年海洋污染将三倍,对海洋生物多样性和人类健康造成灾难性后果。

**题目翻译:**

1. 文章主要关注什么?
   A) 塑料污染如何破坏海洋生态系统
   B) 增加海洋废物的方法
   C) 垃圾收集的历史
   D) 塑料生产的好处

2. 每年有多少塑料废物进入海洋?
   A) 100万公吨
   B) 1亿公吨
   C) 800万公吨
   D) 5000万公吨

3. 河流将多少比例的陆地塑料废物带入海洋?
   A) 95%
   B) 50%
   C) 20%
   D) 80%

4. 多少海洋物种受塑料碎片影响?
   A) 超过1000种
   B) 超过300种
   C) 超过100种
   D) 超过700种

5. 什么是海洋塑料污染的根本解决方案?
   A) 仅清理技术
   B) 减少塑料生产并设计可重复使用的产品
   C) 忽视问题
   D) 仅国际协议

## 文章 87. Mental Health Crisis Among Young Adults

Mental health disorders among young adults have reached alarming levels, with anxiety, depression, and stress-related conditions affecting an estimated one in three people aged 18 to 25. Recent surveys indicate that rates of psychological distress（精神困扰）have doubled since the early 2000s, a trend accelerated by the COVID-19 pandemic, economic uncertainty, and the pervasive（普遍的）influence of social media. Unlike previous generations, today's young adults face a unique combination of pressures: mounting student debt, competitive job markets, housing unaffordability, and constant digital comparison with curated（精心策划的）online personas（人设）.

Social media plays a particularly complex role. While it offers connection and community, excessive use correlates with increased loneliness, poor sleep, and negative self-image. Studies show that young adults who spend more than three hours daily on social platforms are twice as likely to develop depression. The constant exposure to idealized lifestyles fosters feelings of inadequacy（不足感）. Cyberbullying（网络欺凌）adds another layer of harm, with online harassment（骚扰）following victims into spaces that once offered refuge（庇护）. Yet social media also provides access to mental health resources and peer support networks that were previously unavailable.

The consequences of untreated mental health issues extend far beyond emotional suffering. Depression and anxiety impair（损害）academic performance, reduce workforce productivity, and increase the risk of substance abuse（药物滥用）and suicidal behavior. Suicide remains the second leading cause of death among young adults globally. The economic burden is substantial—untreated mental health conditions cost the global economy an estimated $1 trillion annually in lost productivity.

Addressing this crisis requires multifaceted approaches. Schools and universities are integrating mental health education and counseling services into their programs. Digital therapeutics, including app-based cognitive behavioral therapy, expand access to treatment. Employers increasingly offer mental health benefits and flexible work arrangements. Policy reforms mandating insurance coverage for mental health care reduce financial barriers. The research underscores that young adult mental health is not merely an individual concern but a societal imperative, demanding coordinated investment in prevention, early intervention, and accessible treatment to safeguard the wellbeing of future generations.

**Questions:**

1. What does the passage mainly discuss?
   A) The history of mental health treatment
   B) Methods for increasing academic pressure
   C) The benefits of social media for young adults
   D) The mental health crisis affecting young adults

2. What proportion of young adults aged 18 to 25 are affected by mental health disorders?
   A) One in ten
   B) One in two
   C) One in five
   D) One in three

3. How does excessive social media use affect young adults?
   A) It improves self-image
   B) It correlates with increased loneliness, poor sleep, and depression
   C) It only affects older adults
   D) It has no impact on mental health

4. What is the second leading cause of death among young adults globally?
   A) Cancer
   B) Suicide
   C) Accidents
   D) Heart disease

5. What approaches can address the mental health crisis?
   A) Only increasing social media use
   B) Ignoring the problem
   C) Only reducing academic pressure
   D) School counseling, digital therapeutics, employer benefits, and policy reforms

**Answers:**
1. D - 文章主要讨论影响年轻人的心理健康危机
2. D - 三分之一
3. B - 与孤独感增加、睡眠差和抑郁相关
4. B - 自杀
5. D - 学校咨询、数字疗法、雇主福利和政策改革

---

### 翻译

**文章 87. 年轻人心理健康危机**

年轻人中的心理健康障碍已达到令人担忧的水平,焦虑、抑郁和压力相关疾病影响估计三分之一18至25岁的人。最近的调查显示,自2000年代初以来,心理困扰率翻了一番,这一趋势因COVID-19大流行、经济不确定性和社交媒体的普遍影响而加速。与前几代人不同,今天的年轻人面临独特的压力组合:不断增加的学生债务、竞争激烈的就业市场、住房负担不起,以及与精心策划的在线人设的持续数字比较。

社交媒体扮演着特别复杂的角色。虽然它提供联系和社区,但过度使用与孤独感增加、睡眠差和负面自我形象相关。研究表明,每天在社交平台花费超过三小时的年轻人患抑郁症的可能性是其他人的两倍。持续暴露于理想化的生活方式助长不足感。网络欺凌增加了另一层伤害,在线骚扰跟随受害者进入曾经提供庇护的空间。然而,社交媒体也提供了以前无法获得的心理健康资源和同伴支持网络。

未治疗的心理健康问题的后果远远超出情感痛苦。抑郁和焦虑损害学业表现,降低劳动力生产力,增加药物滥用和自杀行为的风险。自杀仍然是全球年轻人第二大死亡原因。经济负担是巨大的——未治疗的心理健康状况估计每年使全球经济在生产力丧失方面损失1万亿美元。

应对这一危机需要多方面的方法。学校和大学正在将心理健康教育和咨询服务纳入其项目。数字疗法,包括基于应用程序的认知行为疗法,扩大了治疗的可及性。雇主越来越多地提供心理健康福利和灵活的工作安排。强制要求心理健康护理保险覆盖的政策改革减少了经济障碍。研究强调,年轻人心理健康不仅仅是个人关切,而是社会当务之急,需要协调投资于预防、早期干预和可及的治疗,以保护未来几代人的福祉。

**题目翻译:**

1. 文章主要讨论什么?
   A) 心理健康治疗的历史
   B) 增加学业压力的方法
   C) 社交媒体对年轻人的好处
   D) 影响年轻人的心理健康危机

2. 18至25岁的年轻人中有多少比例受心理健康障碍影响?
   A) 十分之一
   B) 二分之一
   C) 五分之一
   D) 三分之一

3. 过度使用社交媒体如何影响年轻人?
   A) 改善自我形象
   B) 与孤独感增加、睡眠差和抑郁相关
   C) 仅影响老年人
   D) 对心理健康没有影响

4. 全球年轻人第二大死亡原因是什么?
   A) 癌症
   B) 自杀
   C) 事故
   D) 心脏病

5. 什么方法可以应对心理健康危机?
   A) 仅增加社交媒体使用
   B) 忽视问题
   C) 仅减少学业压力
   D) 学校咨询、数字疗法、雇主福利和政策改革

---

## 文章 88. Biodiversity Loss Threatens Food Security

The decline in global biodiversity poses a direct and growing threat to food security, as the variety of species that sustain agricultural systems diminishes at an unprecedented rate. Approximately 75% of the world's food supply depends on just 12 crop species and five animal species, creating a dangerous concentration that leaves food systems vulnerable to pests, diseases, and climate change. Meanwhile, thousands of traditional crop varieties and wild relatives that once provided genetic diversity are disappearing, eroding（侵蚀）the biological foundation upon which agriculture depends.

Pollinators illustrate the crisis vividly. Bees, butterflies, bats, and birds that fertilize approximately 75% of flowering plants are declining due to pesticide exposure, habitat loss, and climate change. Without pollination, yields of fruits, vegetables, nuts, and oilseeds would plummet（暴跌）by up to 90%, affecting nutrition and livelihoods（生计）for billions. Soil organisms—earthworms, fungi（真菌）, bacteria—that maintain fertility and structure are similarly threatened by intensive farming practices. When these supporting species vanish, agricultural productivity declines, requiring ever more chemical inputs that further degrade ecosystems.

Genetic diversity within crops is equally critical. The Irish Potato Famine of the 1840s, caused by a single pathogen devastating a genetically uniform crop, remains a cautionary tale. Modern monocultures face similar risks—banana plantations worldwide now confront a fungal disease called Panama TR4 that threatens the dominant Cavendish variety. Wild relatives of cultivated plants harbor genes resistant to such threats, but as these species disappear through habitat destruction, options for breeding resilient crops narrow.

Conservation strategies offer pathways to reverse this trend. Seed banks preserve genetic material from thousands of crop varieties. Agroecological（农业生态的）practices—intercropping（间作）, crop rotation, maintaining hedgerows（树篱）and field margins—support both production and biodiversity. Payments to farmers for maintaining habitats that benefit pollinators and soil organisms create economic incentives for stewardship（管理）. The research indicates that integrating biodiversity into agricultural policy is not optional but essential, as food security for a growing global population depends fundamentally on the living systems that sustain it.

**Questions:**

1. What is the passage mainly about?
   A) The history of agricultural chemicals
   B) The benefits of monoculture farming
   C) How biodiversity loss threatens global food security
   D) Methods for reducing crop varieties

2. How many crop species provide 75% of the world's food supply?
   A) 100
   B) 50
   C) 5
   D) 12

3. What percentage of flowering plants depend on animal pollinators?
   A) 90%
   B) 50%
   C) 75%
   D) 25%

4. What historical event illustrates the danger of genetically uniform crops?
   A) World War II
   B) The Industrial Revolution
   C) The Great Depression
   D) The Irish Potato Famine

5. What strategy helps preserve crop genetic diversity?
   A) Only using chemical fertilizers
   B) Removing all wild relatives
   C) Seed banks and agroecological practices
   D) Only planting monocultures

**Answers:**
1. C - 文章主要讲述生物多样性丧失如何威胁全球粮食安全
2. D - 12种
3. C - 75%
4. D - 爱尔兰马铃薯饥荒
5. C - 种子库和农业生态实践

---

### 翻译

**文章 88. 生物多样性丧失威胁粮食安全**

全球生物多样性的下降对粮食安全构成直接且日益增长的威胁,因为维持农业系统的物种多样性正以前所未有的速率减少。世界约75%的食物供应仅依赖12种作物和5种动物物种,创造了一种危险的集中,使食物系统容易受到害虫、疾病和气候变化的影响。同时,数千种曾经提供遗传多样性的传统作物品种和野生近缘种正在消失,侵蚀农业依赖的生物学基础。

传粉者生动地说明了这一危机。为约75%的开花植物授粉的蜜蜂、蝴蝶、蝙蝠和鸟类因农药暴露、栖息地丧失和气候变化而下降。没有授粉,水果、蔬菜、坚果和油料作物的产量将暴跌高达90%,影响数十亿人的营养和生计。维持肥力和结构的土壤生物——蚯蚓、真菌、细菌——同样受到集约化农业实践的威胁。当这些支持性物种消失时,农业生产力下降,需要越来越多的化学投入,进一步退化生态系统。

作物内的遗传多样性同样关键。1840年代的爱尔兰马铃薯饥荒,由单一病原体摧毁遗传一致的作物引起,仍然是一个警示故事。现代单一栽培面临类似风险——全球香蕉种植园现在面临一种称为巴拿马TR4的真菌疾病,威胁着占主导地位的卡文迪什品种。栽培植物的野生近缘种含有抗此类威胁的基因,但随着这些物种因栖息地破坏而消失,培育有恢复力作物的选择变窄。

保护策略提供了逆转这一趋势的途径。种子库保存来自数千种作物品种的遗传物质。农业生态实践——间作、轮作、维护树篱和田间边缘——同时支持生产和生物多样性。向农民支付维护有利于传粉者和土壤生物的栖息地创造了管理的经济激励。研究表明,将生物多样性纳入农业政策不是可选的,而是必要的,因为不断增长的全球人口的粮食安全根本上依赖维持它的生命系统。

**题目翻译:**

1. 文章主要讲什么?
   A) 农业化学品的历史
   B) 单一栽培农业的好处
   C) 生物多样性丧失如何威胁全球粮食安全
   D) 减少作物品种的方法

## 文章 89. Space Tourism Becomes Reality

Space tourism has transitioned from science fiction to commercial reality, with multiple companies now offering suborbital（亚轨道的）and orbital flights to private citizens. Blue Origin and Virgin Galactic pioneered suborbital tourism, providing passengers with brief weightlessness（失重）experiences and views of Earth's curvature（曲率）from altitudes above 80 kilometers. SpaceX has taken orbital tourism further, sending civilian crews to the International Space Station and planning lunar flyby（绕月飞行）missions. The industry projects annual revenues exceeding $3 billion by 2030, driven by technological advances and growing demand from wealthy adventurers（冒险家）.

The economic implications（影响）extend beyond ticket sales. Space tourism stimulates development of reusable launch vehicles, advanced materials, and life support systems that have terrestrial（陆地的）applications. It creates high-skilled jobs in engineering, manufacturing, and operations. Training facilities and spaceports（航天港）generate economic activity in host communities. Proponents（支持者）argue that democratizing access to space inspires public interest in science and technology, potentially cultivating the next generation of explorers and innovators.

Environmental concerns accompany this growth. Each rocket launch emits carbon dioxide, black carbon, and alumina（氧化铝）particles into the upper atmosphere, where their warming effects are amplified. A single suborbital flight can produce as much carbon as a commercial passenger makes over a lifetime of flying. As launch frequency increases, cumulative（累积的）atmospheric impacts could undermine climate goals. Noise pollution and debris from launch sites affect local ecosystems and communities. The industry acknowledges these challenges but maintains that space tourism represents a tiny fraction of global aviation emissions.

Safety and regulatory frameworks are evolving alongside the technology. Spaceflight carries inherent（固有的）risks—acceleration forces, microgravity（微重力）effects, and reentry hazards require rigorous（严格的）passenger screening and training. Regulatory agencies like the FAA in the United States have established licensing processes but face criticism for insufficient oversight of passenger safety. International coordination is needed as space tourism crosses national boundaries and jurisdictional（管辖的）lines. The research suggests that while space tourism will remain a niche market for the foreseeable future, its technological spillovers（溢出效应）and cultural impact will shape humanity's relationship with space for decades to come.

**Questions:**

1. What does the passage mainly discuss?
   A) The emergence of commercial space tourism
   B) Methods for reducing space travel costs
   C) The history of government space programs
   D) The dangers of orbital mechanics

2. What experience do suborbital flights provide to passengers?
   A) Extended stays in orbit
   B) Visits to other planets
   C) Trips to the Moon
   D) Brief weightlessness and views of Earth's curvature

3. What environmental concern accompanies space tourism?
   A) Only water pollution
   B) Only noise pollution
   C) No environmental impact
   D) Rocket emissions including carbon dioxide and black carbon in the upper atmosphere

4. What is projected annual revenue for space tourism by 2030?
   A) $500 million
   B) Over $10 billion
   C) Over $3 billion
   D) $100 million

5. What challenge does the space tourism industry face regarding safety?
   A) Only ticket pricing
   B) No safety concerns exist
   C) Inherent risks requiring rigorous passenger screening and regulatory oversight
   D) Only passenger demand

**Answers:**
1. A - 文章主要讨论商业太空旅游的出现
2. D - 短暂的失重体验和地球曲率景观
3. D - 火箭排放包括二氧化碳和黑碳进入高层大气
4. C - 超过30亿美元
5. C - 需要严格乘客筛选和监管监督的固有风险

### 翻译

**文章 89. 太空旅游成为现实**

太空旅游已从科幻小说转变为商业现实,多家公司现在向私人公民提供亚轨道和轨道飞行。蓝色起源和维珍银河开创了亚轨道旅游,为乘客提供短暂的失重体验和从80公里以上高度观看地球曲率的景观。SpaceX将轨道旅游推进得更远,将平民机组送往国际空间站,并计划月球飞越任务。该行业预计到2030年年收入超过30亿美元,由技术进步和富裕冒险家不断增长的需求驱动。

经济影响超越门票销售。太空旅游刺激了可重复使用运载火箭、先进材料和支持系统的开发,这些系统在陆地有应用。它在工程、制造和运营中创造高技能就业。训练设施和航天港在主办社区产生经济活动。支持者认为,民主化太空访问激发了公众对科学和技术的兴趣,可能培养下一代探险家和创新者。

环境担忧伴随着这一增长。每次火箭发射将二氧化碳、黑碳和氧化铝颗粒排放到高层大气中,在那里它们的变暖效应被放大。一次亚轨道飞行产生的碳可能相当于一个商业乘客一生的飞行量。随着发射频率增加,累积的大气影响可能破坏气候目标。噪音污染和发射场的碎片影响当地生态系统和社区。该行业承认这些挑战,但坚持认为太空旅游仅占全球航空排放的一小部分。

安全和监管框架与技术同步发展。太空飞行具有固有风险——加速力、微重力效应和再入危险需要严格的乘客筛选和训练。美国联邦航空管理局等监管机构已建立许可程序,但面临对乘客安全监管不足的批评。随着太空旅游跨越国界和管辖界线,需要国际协调。研究表明,虽然太空旅游在可预见的未来仍将是一个小众市场,但其技术溢出效应和文化影响将在未来几十年塑造人类与太空的关系。

**题目翻译:**

   A) 商业太空旅游的出现
   B) 降低太空旅行成本的方法
   C) 政府太空项目的历史
   D) 轨道力学的危险

   C) 月球之旅
   D) 短暂的失重体验和地球曲率景观

   B) 仅噪音污染

   D) 火箭排放包括二氧化碳和黑碳进入高层大气

## 文章 90. Quantum Computing Advances Rapidly

Quantum computing is progressing from theoretical concept to practical technology at an accelerating pace, with major breakthroughs occurring in hardware, error correction, and algorithm development. Unlike classical computers that process information as binary bits of 0 or 1, quantum computers use qubits（量子比特）that can exist in superposition（叠加态）—representing both states simultaneously. This fundamental difference enables quantum machines to solve certain problems exponentially（指数级地）faster than even the most powerful supercomputers.

Recent milestones demonstrate tangible（有形的）progress. Google's Sycamore processor achieved quantum supremacy（量子霸权）by performing a specific calculation in 200 seconds that would take a classical supercomputer 10,000 years. IBM has developed processors with over 1,000 qubits and roadmap targets exceeding 100,000 qubits within the decade. China's Jiuzhang photonic（光子的）quantum computer solved a Gaussian boson（高斯玻色子）sampling problem billions of times faster than classical alternatives. These achievements validate that quantum systems can operate at scales where classical simulation becomes impossible.

Practical applications are emerging across multiple domains. In cryptography（密码学）, quantum computers threaten to break current encryption methods, driving development of quantum-resistant algorithms. In pharmaceuticals（制药业）, quantum simulation of molecular interactions accelerates drug discovery. In materials science, quantum modeling enables design of superconductors（超导体）, catalysts（催化剂）, and batteries with properties impossible to achieve through classical trial-and-error. Financial institutions explore quantum algorithms for portfolio optimization and risk analysis. Machine learning combined with quantum computing—quantum machine learning—promises to unlock patterns in data that classical systems cannot detect.

Significant challenges remain before widespread deployment. Qubits are extremely fragile, losing their quantum properties through decoherence（退相干）caused by environmental noise, temperature fluctuations, and electromagnetic interference. Error rates must be reduced through sophisticated error correction codes that require many physical qubits to encode a single logical qubit. Scaling up from hundreds to millions of qubits while maintaining coherence（相干性）and connectivity demands advances in materials, cooling systems, and control electronics. The research indicates that fault-tolerant（容错的）, large-scale quantum computers are likely a decade or more away, but incremental（渐进的）quantum advantage—where quantum systems outperform classical ones on specific useful tasks—may emerge within five years, gradually transforming industries that depend on complex computation.

**Questions:**

1. What is the passage mainly about?
   A) The limitations of classical computers
   B) The history of binary computing
   C) Methods for reducing computational power
   D) The rapid advancement of quantum computing technology

2. What fundamental difference distinguishes quantum computers from classical ones?
   A) They are slower at all tasks
   B) They use qubits that can exist in superposition
   C) They use binary bits only
   D) They cannot solve any problems

3. What practical application does quantum computing enable in pharmaceuticals?
   A) Only text processing
   B) Only data storage
   C) Simulation of molecular interactions to accelerate drug discovery
   D) Slower drug discovery

4. What challenge must be overcome for widespread quantum computing deployment?
   A) Qubit fragility, error rates, and scaling up while maintaining coherence
   B) Only high demand
   C) No challenges exist
   D) Only software development

5. When might fault-tolerant, large-scale quantum computers become available?
   A) Likely a decade or more away
   B) Already available
   C) Within one year
   D) Never

**Answers:**
1. D - 文章主要讲述量子计算技术的快速进步
2. B - 使用可以处于叠加态的量子比特
3. C - 模拟分子相互作用以加速药物发现
4. A - 量子比特脆弱性、错误率和在保持相干性的同时扩大规模
5. A - 可能还需要十年或更长时间

---

### 翻译

**文章 90. 量子计算快速进步**

量子计算正以加速的步伐从理论概念发展为实用技术,在硬件、纠错和算法开发方面取得重大突破。与将信息处理为0或1的二进制位的经典计算机不同,量子计算机使用可以处于叠加态的量子比特——同时表示两种状态。这一根本差异使量子机器能够以比最强大的超级计算机快指数级的速度解决某些问题。

最近的里程碑展示了有形的进展。谷歌的Sycamore处理器通过在200秒内执行经典超级计算机需要10000年的特定计算,实现了量子霸权。IBM已开发了超过1000个量子比特的处理器,十年内的路线图目标超过100000个量子比特。中国的九章光量子计算机解决了高斯玻色子采样问题,比经典替代方案快数十亿倍。这些成就验证了量子系统可以在经典模拟变得不可能的规模上运行。

实际应用正在多个领域出现。在密码学中,量子计算机威胁到破解当前的加密方法,推动抗量子算法的开发。在制药业,分子相互作用的量子模拟加速药物发现。在材料科学中,量子建模使设计超导体、催化剂和具有通过经典试错法无法实现的特性的电池成为可能。金融机构探索用于投资组合优化和风险分析的量子算法。与量子计算结合的机器学习——量子机器学习——承诺解锁经典系统无法检测的数据模式。

在广泛部署之前仍存在重大挑战。量子比特极其脆弱,通过环境噪音、温度波动和电磁干扰引起的退相干失去其量子特性。必须通过复杂的纠错码降低错误率,这需要许多物理量子比特来编码单个逻辑量子比特。从数百个量子比特扩展到数百万个,同时保持相干性和连接性,需要材料、冷却系统和控制电子学的进步。研究表明,容错的大规模量子计算机可能还需要十年或更长时间,但增量量子优势——量子系统在特定有用任务上超越经典系统——可能在五年内出现,逐渐改变依赖复杂计算的产业。

**题目翻译:**

1. 文章主要讲什么?
   A) 经典计算机的局限性
   B) 二进制计算的历史
   C) 降低计算能力的方法
   D) 量子计算技术的快速进步

2. 什么根本差异将量子计算机与经典计算机区分开来?
   A) 它们在所有任务上都更慢
   B) 它们使用可以处于叠加态的量子比特
   C) 它们仅使用二进制位
   D) 它们不能解决任何问题

3. 量子计算在制药业中实现什么实际应用?
   A) 仅文本处理
   B) 仅数据存储
   C) 模拟分子相互作用以加速药物发现
   D) 更慢的药物发现

4. 广泛部署量子计算必须克服什么挑战?
   A) 量子比特脆弱性、错误率和在保持相干性的同时扩大规模
   B) 仅高需求
   C) 没有挑战
   D) 仅软件开发

5. 容错的大规模量子计算机何时可能可用?

   B) 已经可用
   C) 一年内
   D) 永远不会

## 文章 91. Food Waste Crisis Demands Action

Approximately one-third of all food produced globally—about 1.3 billion tons annually—is wasted or lost, representing a crisis that simultaneously threatens food security, economic stability, and environmental sustainability. The problem manifests（显现）differently across regions: in developing countries, losses occur primarily during production and storage due to inadequate（不足的）infrastructure（基础设施）, while in wealthy nations, waste concentrates at retail and consumer stages through over-purchasing, confusion over expiration（到期）labels, and discarding of edible food.

The environmental consequences are staggering（惊人的）. If food waste were a country, it would rank third in greenhouse gas emissions after China and the United States. Decomposing food in landfills（垃圾填埋场）produces methane（甲烷）, a potent（强效的）greenhouse gas with 80 times the warming potential of carbon dioxide over two decades. The water used to grow wasted food equals the annual flow of the Volga River, Europe's largest. Land area devoted to food that is never consumed exceeds the size of China. This represents not merely inefficiency but a fundamental misallocation（错配）of planetary resources at a time when nearly 800 million people face chronic（慢性的）hunger.

Economic losses are equally substantial. Global food waste costs approximately $1 trillion annually, affecting farmers who lose income, retailers who discard unsold products, and households that throw away purchased food. In the United States, the average family of four wastes $1,500 worth of food each year. Restaurants and supermarkets face rising disposal costs as municipalities（市政当局）implement organic waste bans. The research indicates that reducing food waste by just 15% could provide enough food to feed 25 million Americans daily.

Solutions require coordinated action across the supply chain. Technology innovations including smart packaging that accurately indicates freshness, apps that connect retailers with food banks, and improved cold chain logistics（物流）can prevent losses. Policy interventions（干预）such as standardized date labeling, tax incentives（激励）for food donation, and mandatory（强制的）composting（堆肥）programs create structural change. Consumer education campaigns teaching meal planning, proper storage, and creative use of leftovers reduce household waste. The research demonstrates that food waste reduction is one of the most effective climate solutions available, requiring commitment from governments, businesses, and individuals to transform how we value and manage food resources.

**Questions:**

1. What is the passage mainly concerned with?
   A) Methods for increasing food consumption
   B) The history of agricultural development
   C) The benefits of food production
   D) The global food waste crisis and its solutions

2. What percentage of global food production is wasted annually?
   A) One-tenth
   B) One-half
   C) One-quarter
   D) One-third

3. If food waste were a country, where would it rank in greenhouse gas emissions?
   A) Second
   B) First
   C) Third
   D) Tenth

4. How much money does the average American family of four waste on food annually?
   A) $500
   B) $1,000
   C) $3,000
   D) $1,500

5. What solution can prevent food losses through technology?
   A) Smart packaging, food bank apps, and improved cold chain logistics
   B) Removing all packaging
   C) Only government bans
   D) Only consumer education

**Answers:**
1. D - 文章主要关注全球食物浪费危机及其解决方案
2. D - 三分之一
3. C - 第三
4. D - 1500美元
5. A - 智能包装、食物银行应用和改进的冷链物流

---

### 翻译

**文章 91. 食物浪费危机需要行动**

全球生产的所有食物中约三分之一——约13亿吨每年——被浪费或损失,代表了一个同时威胁粮食安全、经济稳定性和环境可持续性的危机。问题在不同地区表现不同:在发展中国家,损失主要发生在生产和储存阶段,原因是基础设施不足,而在富裕国家,浪费集中在零售和消费阶段,通过过度购买、对到期标签的混淆和丢弃可食用食物。

环境后果是惊人的。如果食物浪费是一个国家,它将在温室气体排放中排名第三,仅次于中国和美国。垃圾填埋场中分解的食物产生甲烷,一种在二十年内温室效应是二氧化碳80倍的强效温室气体。用于种植被浪费食物的水等于欧洲最大的伏尔加河的年流量。用于从未被消费的食物土地面积超过中国的大小。这不仅代表低效,而且在近8亿人面临慢性饥饿的时候,代表行星资源的根本错配。

经济损失同样巨大。全球食物浪费每年成本约1万亿美元,影响失去收入的农民、丢弃未售产品的零售商和扔掉购买食物的家庭。在美国,平均四口之家每年浪费价值1500美元的食物。随着市政当局实施有机废物禁令,餐馆和超市面临越来越高的处置成本。研究表明,仅减少15%的食物浪费就可以提供足够的食物每天喂养2500万美国人。

解决方案需要供应链的协调行动。技术创新,包括准确指示新鲜度的智能包装、将零售商与食物银行连接起来的应用程序和改进的冷链物流,可以防止损失。政策干预,如标准化日期标签、食物捐赠的税收激励和强制堆肥计划,创造结构性变革。消费者教育活动教授膳食计划、适当储存和剩余食物的创意使用,减少家庭浪费。研究表明,减少食物浪费是最有效的气候解决方案之一,需要政府、企业和个人的承诺来改变我们重视和管理食物资源的方式。

**题目翻译:**

1. 文章主要关注什么?
   A) 增加食物消费的方法
   B) 农业发展的历史
   C) 食物生产的好处
   D) 全球食物浪费危机及其解决方案

2. 全球食物生产中每年浪费多少比例?
   A) 十分之一
   B) 二分之一
   C) 四分之一
   D) 三分之一

3. 如果食物浪费是一个国家,温室气体排放排名如何?
   A) 第二
   B) 第一
   C) 第三
   D) 第十

4. 美国平均四口之家每年浪费多少食物?
   A) 500美元
   B) 1000美元
   C) 3000美元
   D) 1500美元

5. 什么解决方案可以通过技术防止食物损失?
   A) 智能包装、食物银行应用和改进的冷链物流
   B) 移除所有包装
   C) 仅政府禁令
   D) 仅消费者教育

## 文章 92. Aging Population Reshapes Society

The world is experiencing an unprecedented demographic shift as populations age at rates never before seen in human history. By 2050, the number of people aged 60 and older will double to 2.1 billion, representing nearly a quarter of the global population. This transformation results from two converging（趋同的）trends: declining fertility（生育率）rates as families choose to have fewer children, and increasing life expectancy due to medical advances, improved nutrition, and better living conditions. While longer, healthier lives represent a triumph of development, rapid aging creates profound challenges for economies, social systems, and family structures.

Economic implications are far-reaching. As the proportion of working-age adults shrinks relative to retirees, pension systems face sustainability crises. Healthcare costs rise as older populations require more medical care, particularly for chronic conditions like diabetes, heart disease, and dementia（痴呆症）. Labor shortages emerge in sectors dependent on younger workers, from manufacturing to caregiving. Governments must choose between raising retirement ages, increasing taxes, reducing benefits, or some combination—all politically difficult decisions. Japan, where nearly 30% of the population is over 65, illustrates these pressures, with rural areas depopulating（人口减少）as young people migrate to cities and elderly residents remain behind.

Social structures adapt in response. Multi-generational households, once declining, are experiencing renewed interest as families seek to share caregiving responsibilities and costs. Communities are redesigning public spaces to accommodate mobility limitations and promote social connection among older adults. Technology offers solutions—telemedicine（远程医疗）expands healthcare access, assistive devices enable independent living, and robotics address caregiver shortages. However, these innovations raise questions about equity（公平）, as not all older adults can afford or access them.

The research suggests that successful aging societies require rethinking fundamental assumptions about work, retirement, and intergenerational（代际的）relationships. Policies promoting lifelong learning, flexible work arrangements, and age-friendly urban design can extend productive participation. Investing in preventive healthcare reduces burden on acute（急性的）care systems. Encouraging immigration can temporarily offset labor shortages but does not address root causes. The challenge is not merely managing decline but creating societies where people of all ages can contribute meaningfully and live with dignity（尊严）.

**Questions:**

1. What does the passage mainly discuss?
   A) The history of retirement systems
   B) How aging populations are reshaping global society
   C) The benefits of population decline
   D) Methods for increasing birth rates

2. By 2050, how many people aged 60 and older are projected worldwide?
   A) 1.5 billion
   B) 1 billion
   C) 2.1 billion
   D) 3 billion

3. What percentage of Japan's population is over 65?
   A) 40%
   B) 20%
   C) Nearly 30%
   D) 15%

4. What social adaptation is experiencing renewed interest?
   A) Single-person households
   B) Multi-generational households
   C) Urban migration
   D) Early retirement

5. What approach can extend productive participation in aging societies?
   A) Only reducing healthcare
   B) Only increasing immigration
   C) Removing all retirement policies
   D) Lifelong learning, flexible work arrangements, and age-friendly urban design

**Answers:**
1. B - 文章主要讨论人口老龄化如何重塑全球社会
2. C - 21亿
3. C - 近30%
4. B - 多代同堂的家庭
5. D - 终身学习、灵活的工作安排和适老化的城市设计

---

### 翻译

**文章 92. 人口老龄化重塑社会**

世界正在经历前所未有的人口结构转变,人口以人类历史上前所未有的速度老龄化。到2050年,60岁及以上的人口将翻倍至21亿,占全球人口的近四分之一。这种转变源于两个趋同趋势:随着家庭选择生育更少的孩子,生育率下降,以及由于医学进步、营养改善和生活条件更好,预期寿命增加。虽然更长寿、更健康的生活代表发展的胜利,但快速老龄化对经济、社会系统和家庭结构造成深刻挑战。

经济影响是深远的。随着工作年龄成年人相对于退休人员的比例缩小,养老金系统面临可持续性危机。随着老年人口需要更多医疗护理,特别是糖尿病、心脏病和痴呆症等慢性病,医疗成本上升。依赖年轻工人的部门出现劳动力短缺,从制造业到护理。政府必须在提高退休年龄、增加税收、减少福利或某种组合之间做出选择——所有都是政治上困难的决定。日本近30%的人口超过65岁,说明了这些压力,农村地区随着年轻人迁移到城市而人口减少,老年居民留下。

社会结构正在适应响应。曾经下降的多代同堂家庭正在经历新的兴趣,因为家庭寻求分担护理责任和成本。社区正在重新设计公共空间,以适应行动限制并促进老年人之间的社会联系。技术提供解决方案——远程医疗扩大医疗保健可及性,辅助设备使独立生活成为可能,机器人技术解决护理者短缺。然而,这些创新引发了关于公平的问题,因为并非所有老年人都能负担得起或获得它们。

研究表明,成功的老龄化社会需要重新思考关于工作、退休和代际关系的基本假设。促进终身学习、灵活工作安排和适老化城市设计的政策可以延长生产性参与。投资于预防性医疗保健减少对急性护理系统的负担。鼓励移民可以暂时抵消劳动力短缺,但不能解决根本原因。挑战不仅仅是管理衰退,而是创造一个所有年龄的人都能有意义地贡献并有尊严地生活的社会。

**题目翻译:**

1. 文章主要讨论什么?
   A) 退休系统的历史
   B) 人口老龄化如何重塑全球社会
   C) 人口下降的好处
   D) 增加出生率的方法

2. 到2050年,全球预计有多少60岁及以上的人?
   A) 15亿
   B) 10亿
   C) 21亿
   D) 30亿

3. 日本65岁以上人口占多少比例?
   A) 40%
   B) 20%
   C) 近30%
   D) 15%

4. 什么社会适应正在经历新的兴趣?
   A) 单人家庭
   B) 多代同堂的家庭
   C) 城市迁移
   D) 提前退休

5. 什么方法可以在老龄化社会延长生产性参与?
   A) 仅减少医疗保健
   B) 仅增加移民
   C) 移除所有退休政策
   D) 终身学习、灵活的工作安排和适老化的城市设计

## 文章 93. Invasive Species Disrupt Ecosystems

Invasive species—organisms introduced outside their native range that spread aggressively—represent one of the leading causes of biodiversity loss worldwide, costing global economies over $400 billion annually in damages and control efforts. Unlike native species that co-evolved with their ecosystems, invaders often lack natural predators, diseases, or competitors in their new environments, allowing populations to explode and displace indigenous（本土的）flora（植物群）and fauna（动物群）. Globalization accelerates the problem, as international trade, travel, and climate change create pathways for species to cross natural barriers that once contained them.

The impacts are devastating and multifaceted. The brown tree snake, accidentally introduced to Guam after World War II, has eliminated 10 of 12 native forest bird species. Zebra mussels in the Great Lakes clog water intake pipes, costing billions in infrastructure damage while outcompeting native filter feeders. Kudzu vine in the American Southeast smothers forests, blocking sunlight and killing trees. Cane toads in Australia poison predators that attempt to eat them, disrupting food chains. Invasive plants alter fire regimes（火灾模式）, soil chemistry, and hydrology（水文学）, fundamentally transforming ecosystems. Once established, eradication（根除）is rarely possible, making prevention the most cost-effective strategy.

Economic consequences extend beyond environmental damage. Invasive species reduce agricultural yields, damage fisheries, degrade timber resources, and increase healthcare costs when they transmit diseases. The emerald ash borer（翡翠灰螟）, native to Asia, has killed hundreds of millions of ash trees in North America, eliminating urban shade, reducing property values, and increasing energy costs for cooling. Asian carp threaten the Great Lakes fishing industry and could devastate ecosystems if they establish populations. The research indicates that climate change will enable further range expansions, as warming temperatures allow tropical and subtropical species to colonize previously inhospitable（不宜居的）regions.

Management requires integrated approaches combining prevention, early detection, and control. Border inspections and quarantine（检疫）regulations reduce introduction risk. Public education campaigns discourage release of exotic pets and plants. Rapid response teams target new infestations（侵扰）before they spread. Biological control using natural enemies from the species' native range can suppress populations, though this approach requires careful evaluation to avoid unintended consequences. The research underscores that invasive species management is not merely an environmental concern but an economic imperative（当务之急）, demanding sustained investment and international cooperation to protect ecosystems and livelihoods from biological invaders.

**Questions:**

1. What is the passage mainly about?
   A) How invasive species disrupt ecosystems and economies
   B) Methods for increasing biodiversity
   C) The history of global trade
   D) The benefits of species introduction

2. How much do invasive species cost global economies annually?
   A) $10 billion
   B) Over $400 billion
   C) $100 billion
   D) $1 trillion

3. What happened to native forest birds on Guam after brown tree snake introduction?
   A) They increased in number
   B) They adapted successfully
   C) 10 of 12 species were eliminated
   D) No change occurred

4. Why are invasive species often successful in new environments?
   A) They face many predators
   B) They lack natural predators, diseases, or competitors
   C) They cannot reproduce
   D) They require special conditions

5. What is identified as the most cost-effective strategy for managing invasive species?
   A) Eradication after establishment
   B) Only biological control
   C) Prevention through border inspections and quarantine
   D) Ignoring new infestations

**Answers:**
1. A - 文章主要讲述入侵物种如何破坏生态系统和经济
2. B - 超过4000亿美元
3. C - 12种中有10种被消灭
4. B - 它们缺乏天敌、疾病或竞争者
5. C - 通过边境检查和检疫进行预防

---

### 翻译

**文章 93. 入侵物种破坏生态系统**

入侵物种——引入其原生范围之外并迅速扩散的生物——代表全球生物多样性丧失的主要原因之一,每年给全球经济造成超过4000亿美元的损害和控制成本。与与其生态系统共同进化的本地物种不同,入侵者在新环境中往往缺乏天敌、疾病或竞争者,使种群爆炸并取代本土动植物。全球化加速了这个问题,因为国际贸易、旅行和气候变化为物种跨越曾经限制它们的自然屏障创造了途径。

影响是毁灭性且多方面的。二战后意外引入关岛的棕树蛇已消灭了12种本地森林鸟类中的10种。大湖区的斑马贻贝堵塞取水管道,造成数十亿美元的基础设施损害,同时胜过本地滤食者。美国东南部的葛藤窒息森林,阻挡阳光并杀死树木。澳大利亚的甘蔗蟾蜍毒害试图吃它们的捕食者,破坏食物链。入侵植物改变火灾模式、土壤化学和水文学,从根本上改变生态系统。一旦建立,根除几乎不可能,使预防成为最具成本效益的策略。

经济后果超越环境损害。入侵物种降低农业产量,损害渔业,退化木材资源,并在传播疾病时增加医疗成本。原产亚洲的翡翠灰螟已杀死北美数亿棵白蜡树,消除城市阴凉,降低财产价值,并增加冷却能源成本。亚洲鲤鱼威胁大湖区渔业,如果建立种群可能破坏生态系统。研究表明,气候变化将使进一步的分布扩张成为可能,因为变暖的温度允许热带和亚热带物种殖民以前不宜居的地区。

管理需要结合预防、早期发现和控制的综合方法。边境检查和检疫法规降低引入风险。公众教育活动劝阻释放外来宠物和植物。快速响应团队在新侵扰扩散之前针对它们。使用物种原生范围的天敌进行生物控制可以抑制种群,尽管这种方法需要仔细评估以避免意外后果。研究强调,入侵物种管理不仅仅是环境问题,而是经济当务之急,需要持续投资和国际合作来保护生态系统和生计免受生物入侵者的侵害。

**题目翻译:**

1. 文章主要讲什么?
   A) 入侵物种如何破坏生态系统和经济
   B) 增加生物多样性的方法
   C) 全球贸易的历史
   D) 物种引入的好处

2. 入侵物种每年给全球经济造成多少成本?
   A) 100亿美元
   B) 超过4000亿美元
   C) 1000亿美元
   D) 1万亿美元

3. 棕树蛇引入后关岛的本地森林鸟类发生了什么?
   A) 数量增加
   B) 成功适应
   C) 12种中有10种被消灭
   D) 没有变化

4. 为什么入侵物种在新环境中往往成功?
   A) 它们面临许多捕食者
   B) 它们缺乏天敌、疾病或竞争者
   C) 它们不能繁殖
   D) 它们需要特殊条件

5. 什么是管理入侵物种最具成本效益的策略?
   A) 建立后根除
   B) 仅生物控制
   C) 通过边境检查和检疫进行预防
   D) 忽视新侵扰

## 文章 94. Digital Currency Revolution Transforms Finance

Digital currencies are fundamentally reshaping the global financial landscape, with central bank digital currencies (CBDCs), cryptocurrencies（加密货币）, and stablecoins（稳定币）challenging traditional banking models and payment systems. Over 100 countries, representing 95% of global GDP, are exploring or piloting CBDCs, seeking to modernize payment infrastructure, enhance financial inclusion, and maintain monetary sovereignty（货币主权）in an increasingly digital economy. China's digital yuan has processed over $250 billion in transactions since its 2020 launch, making it the world's largest CBDC experiment.

The potential benefits are substantial. Digital currencies can reduce transaction costs by eliminating intermediaries（中介机构）, enabling instant cross-border payments that currently take days and cost 6-7% in fees. They can expand financial access to the 1.7 billion unbanked（无银行账户的）adults worldwide who lack traditional bank accounts but possess mobile phones. Programmable money allows automatic execution of conditions—tax payments, welfare distributions, or smart contracts—reducing administrative overhead and fraud. Central banks gain enhanced ability to implement monetary policy, track money flows, and combat illegal activities like money laundering（洗钱）and tax evasion（逃税）.

Risks and challenges accompany this transformation. Privacy concerns arise as digital currencies enable unprecedented surveillance of financial transactions, raising questions about government oversight versus individual freedom. Cybersecurity threats multiply as hackers target digital wallets and payment infrastructure. Financial stability could be disrupted if citizens rapidly shift deposits from commercial banks to central bank digital currencies during crises, triggering bank runs（银行挤兑）. The energy consumption of proof-of-work（工作量证明）cryptocurrencies like Bitcoin rivals that of entire nations, creating environmental concerns. International coordination is needed to establish interoperability（互操作性）standards and prevent fragmentation into competing digital currency blocs（集团）.

The research suggests that digital currencies will not eliminate traditional banking but rather force its evolution. Banks must adapt by offering superior services, competitive interest rates, and innovative products to retain customers. Regulatory frameworks need updating to address new risks while fostering innovation. Financial literacy education becomes essential as citizens navigate unfamiliar digital tools. The transition is irreversible（不可逆转的）—societies that embrace digital currency innovation while managing its risks will gain competitive advantages in the emerging global economy, while those that resist risk falling behind in the digital financial revolution.

**Questions:**

1. What does the passage mainly discuss?
   A) The decline of digital payment systems
   B) The history of traditional banking
   C) How digital currencies are transforming global finance
   D) Methods for increasing transaction costs

2. How many countries are exploring or piloting CBDCs?
   A) Over 100
   B) 50
   C) 75
   D) 200

3. What percentage of global GDP do these countries represent?
   A) 50%
   B) 75%
   C) 95%
   D) 100%

4. How many unbanked adults worldwide could benefit from digital currencies?
   A) 500 million
   B) 3 billion
   C) 1 billion
   D) 1.7 billion

5. What risk accompanies digital currency adoption?
   A) Privacy concerns, cybersecurity threats, financial stability risks, and energy consumption
   B) Only high costs
   C) No risks exist
   D) Only user resistance

**Answers:**
1. C - 文章主要讨论数字货币如何改变全球金融
2. A - 超过100个
3. C - 95%
4. D - 17亿
5. A - 隐私问题、网络安全威胁、金融稳定性风险和能源消耗

---

### 翻译

**文章 94. 数字货币革命改变金融**

数字货币正在从根本上重塑全球金融格局,央行数字货币(CBDC)、加密货币和稳定币正在挑战传统银行模式和支付系统。超过100个国家,占全球GDP的95%,正在探索或试点CBDC,寻求在日益数字化的经济中现代化支付基础设施、增强金融包容性并维持货币主权。中国的数字人民币自2020年推出以来已处理超过2500亿美元的交易,使其成为世界上最大的CBDC实验。

潜在好处是巨大的。数字货币可以通过消除中介机构来降低交易成本,实现目前需要数天且费用为6-7%的即时跨境支付。它们可以将金融服务扩展到全球17亿缺乏传统银行账户但拥有手机的无银行账户成年人。可编程货币允许自动执行条件——税款支付、福利分配或智能合约——减少管理开销和欺诈。中央银行获得增强实施货币政策、跟踪资金流动和打击洗钱和逃税等非法活动的能力。

风险和挑战伴随着这一转型。随着数字货币实现对金融交易前所未有的监控,引发关于政府监督与个人自由之间权衡的问题,隐私问题出现。随着黑客瞄准数字钱包和支付基础设施,网络安全威胁成倍增加。如果公民在危机期间迅速将存款从商业银行转移到央行数字货币,可能引发银行挤兑,金融稳定性可能受到破坏。比特币等工作量证明加密货币的能源消耗可与整个国家匹敌,造成环境问题。需要国际协调来建立互操作性标准并防止分裂成竞争的数字货币集团。

研究表明,数字货币不会消除传统银行,而是迫使它进化。银行必须通过提供优质服务、有竞争力的利率和创新产品来适应以留住客户。监管框架需要更新以解决新风险,同时促进创新。随着公民驾驭不熟悉的数字工具,金融素养教育变得至关重要。转型是不可逆转的——在管理风险的同时拥抱数字货币创新的社会将在新兴全球经济中获得竞争优势,而抵制那些社会面临在数字金融革命中落后的风险。

**题目翻译:**

1. 文章主要讨论什么?
   A) 数字支付系统的下降
   B) 传统银行的历史
   C) 数字货币如何改变全球金融
   D) 增加交易成本的方法

2. 多少国家正在探索或试点CBDC?
   A) 超过100个
   B) 50个
   C) 75个
   D) 200个

3. 这些国家占全球GDP的多少比例?
   A) 50%
   B) 75%
   C) 95%
   D) 100%

4. 全球多少无银行账户成年人可以从数字货币中受益?
   A) 5亿
   B) 30亿
   C) 10亿
   D) 17亿

5. 数字货币采用伴随什么风险?
   A) 隐私问题、网络安全威胁、金融稳定性风险和能源消耗
   B) 仅高成本
   C) 没有风险
   D) 仅用户抵制

## 文章 95. Ocean Exploration Reveals New Species

The deep ocean remains Earth's last great frontier, with over 80% of the seafloor unmapped and unexplored, harboring ecosystems and species unknown to science. Recent expeditions using advanced submersibles（潜水器）, remotely operated vehicles, and environmental DNA sampling have discovered hundreds of new species in abyssal（深海的）plains, hydrothermal vents（热液喷口）, and deep-sea mountains, revealing biodiversity far richer than previously imagined. These discoveries challenge assumptions about the limits of life, as organisms thrive in conditions once considered inhospitable—crushing pressures, near-freezing temperatures, and complete darkness.

Hydrothermal vent ecosystems, first discovered in 1977, exemplify life's adaptability. These underwater geysers（间歇泉）spew superheated water rich in minerals, supporting communities of giant tube worms, blind shrimp, and specialized bacteria that derive energy from chemical reactions rather than sunlight through chemosynthesis（化能合成）. Recent expeditions have identified vents at greater depths than previously known, each hosting unique assemblages（组合）of organisms adapted to specific temperature and chemical gradients. Some species produce compounds with pharmaceutical potential, including antibiotics and anti-cancer agents, highlighting the practical value of biodiversity exploration.

Deep-sea mining threatens these fragile ecosystems before they are fully understood. Polymetallic（多金属的）nodules（结核）on the abyssal plain contain manganese（锰）, nickel, cobalt（钴）, and copper essential for batteries and renewable energy technology. Mining operations would destroy habitat that takes millions of years to form, generating sediment plumes（沉积物羽流）that could smother surrounding ecosystems. The International Seabed Authority is developing regulations to balance resource extraction with conservation, but scientists warn that irreversible damage could occur before adequate protections are established.

The research emphasizes that deep-sea exploration is not merely academic curiosity but essential for understanding Earth's systems and securing future resources responsibly. Marine protected areas can safeguard vulnerable ecosystems while allowing sustainable use of surrounding waters. International cooperation ensures that benefits from deep-sea genetic resources are shared equitably（公平地）. Technology advances including autonomous underwater vehicles and artificial intelligence accelerate discovery rates, enabling comprehensive mapping of ocean biodiversity. The ocean's final frontier holds secrets that could transform medicine, biotechnology, and our understanding of life's possibilities—knowledge worth preserving through careful stewardship（管理）rather than reckless exploitation.

**Questions:**

1. What is the passage mainly about?
   A) Methods for deep-sea mining
   B) The decline of ocean biodiversity
   C) How ocean exploration reveals new species and ecosystems
   D) The history of submarine development

2. What percentage of the seafloor remains unmapped and unexplored?
   A) 80%
   B) 20%
   C) 50%
   D) 95%

3. How do hydrothermal vent organisms derive energy?
   A) From chemical reactions through chemosynthesis
   B) They cannot derive energy
   C) From sunlight through photosynthesis
   D) From eating other organisms only

4. What threat do deep-sea ecosystems currently face?
   A) Deep-sea mining that would destroy habitat
   B) No threats exist
   C) Only pollution
   D) Only overfishing

5. What approach can safeguard vulnerable deep-sea ecosystems?
   A) Marine protected areas and international cooperation
   B) Only academic research
   C) Removing all protections
   D) Only deep-sea mining

**Answers:**
1. C - 文章主要讲述海洋探索如何发现新物种和生态系统
2. A - 80%
3. A - 通过化能合成从化学反应中获取能量
4. A - 深海采矿将破坏栖息地
5. A - 海洋保护区和国际合作

---

### 翻译

**文章 95. 海洋探索发现新物种**

深海仍然是地球上最后的伟大前沿,超过80%的海底未绘制地图和未探索,孕育着科学未知的生态系统和物种。最近使用先进潜水器、遥控飞行器与环境DNA采样的探险在深海平原、热液喷口和深海山脉中发现了数百个新物种,揭示了比之前想象的更丰富的生物多样性。这些发现挑战了关于生命极限的假设,因为生物在曾经被认为不宜居的条件下茁壮成长——极端压力、接近冰点的温度和完全黑暗。

1977年首次发现的热液喷口生态系统展示了生命的适应性。这些水下间歇泉喷出富含矿物质的过热海水,支持巨型管虫、盲虾和专门细菌的群落,这些细菌通过化能合成而非阳光从化学反应中获取能量。最近的探险在比以前已知的更深处发现了喷口,每个都栖息着适应特定温度和化学梯度的独特生物组合。一些物种产生具有制药潜力的化合物,包括抗生素和抗癌剂,突出了生物多样性探索的实际价值。

深海采矿在这些脆弱生态系统被完全理解之前威胁着它们。深海平原上的多金属结核含有电池和可再生能源技术必需的锰、镍、钴和铜。采矿作业将破坏需要数百万年形成的栖息地,产生可能窒息周围生态系统的沉积物羽流。国际海底管理局正在制定法规以平衡资源开采与保护,但科学家警告说,在建立充分保护之前可能发生不可逆转的损害。

研究强调,深海探索不仅仅是学术好奇心,而是对理解地球系统和负责任地保障未来资源至关重要。海洋保护区可以在允许可持续使用周围水域的同时保护脆弱生态系统。国际合作确保深海遗传资源的好处得到公平分享。包括自主水下航行器和人工智能在内的技术进步加速了发现速率,使海洋生物多样性的全面测绘成为可能。海洋的最后前沿持有可以改变医学、生物技术和我们对生命可能性理解的秘密——这些知识值得通过谨慎的管理而非鲁莽的开发来保护。

**题目翻译:**

1. 文章主要讲什么?
   A) 深海采矿的方法
   B) 海洋生物多样性的下降
   C) 海洋探索如何发现新物种和生态系统
   D) 潜艇发展的历史

2. 海底有多少比例未绘制地图和未探索?
   A) 80%
   B) 20%
   C) 50%
   D) 95%

3. 热液喷口生物如何获取能量?
   A) 通过化能合成从化学反应中获取
   B) 它们不能获取能量
   C) 通过光合作用的阳光
   D) 仅通过吃其他生物

4. 深海生态系统目前面临什么威胁?
   A) 将破坏栖息地的深海采矿
   B) 没有威胁
   C) 仅污染
   D) 仅过度捕捞

5. 什么方法可以保护脆弱的深海生态系统?
   A) 海洋保护区和国际合作
   B) 仅学术研究
   C) 移除所有保护
   D) 仅深海采矿

## 文章 96. Renewable Energy Storage Breakthrough

Energy storage represents the critical missing piece in the renewable energy transition, as solar and wind power generate electricity intermittently（间歇地）—when the sun shines and wind blows, not necessarily when demand peaks. Recent breakthroughs in battery technology, pumped hydro storage（抽水蓄能）, and thermal systems are addressing this challenge, enabling grids（电网）to store excess renewable energy and dispatch（调度）it when needed, transforming variable sources into reliable power supplies.

Lithium-ion batteries dominate current storage applications, powering everything from smartphones to grid-scale installations. However, their limitations—cost, energy density, charging speed, and reliance on scarce materials like cobalt—drive research into alternatives. Solid-state batteries（固态电池）replace liquid electrolytes（电解质）with solid materials, offering higher energy density, faster charging, and improved safety. Sodium-ion batteries use abundant, inexpensive sodium instead of lithium, potentially reducing costs by 30-50%. Flow batteries（液流电池）store energy in liquid electrolytes contained in external tanks, enabling easy scaling for long-duration storage. These innovations could reduce storage costs by another 50-70% over the next decade.

Pumped hydro storage, the most mature technology, accounts for 90% of global storage capacity. It works by pumping water uphill when electricity is abundant and releasing it through turbines（涡轮机）when demand peaks. However, suitable geography limits expansion. Compressed air energy storage uses excess electricity to compress air in underground caverns（洞穴）, releasing it to generate power when needed. Thermal storage systems heat or cool materials like molten salt（熔盐）or ice, storing energy for later use in heating, cooling, or electricity generation.

The research indicates that no single storage technology will dominate; rather, a portfolio approach matching different technologies to different applications will optimize grid performance. Short-duration storage (4-8 hours) handles daily cycling, while long-duration storage (days to weeks) addresses seasonal variations. Vehicle-to-grid technology allows electric car batteries to supply power back to the grid during peak demand, creating a distributed storage network. Policy support including investment tax credits, performance standards, and grid modernization funding accelerates deployment（部署）. The transition to 100% renewable electricity is technically and economically feasible with current and emerging storage technologies, requiring only sustained investment and supportive regulatory frameworks to unlock their full potential.

**Questions:**

1. What does the passage mainly discuss?
   A) The history of battery development
   B) Breakthroughs in renewable energy storage technology
   C) Methods for increasing fossil fuel use
   D) The decline of renewable energy

2. What percentage of global storage capacity does pumped hydro represent?
   A) 70%
   B) 95%
   C) 50%
   D) 90%

3. What advantage do sodium-ion batteries offer over lithium-ion?
   A) Higher energy density
   B) Lower costs using abundant sodium
   C) Faster charging only
   D) No advantages

4. What approach will optimize grid storage performance?
   A) Relying on a single technology
   B) A portfolio approach matching different technologies to applications
   C) Only using lithium-ion batteries
   D) Removing all storage systems

5. What is technically and economically feasible with current storage technologies?
   A) 50% renewable electricity
   B) 100% renewable electricity
   C) 75% renewable electricity
   D) No renewable electricity

**Answers:**
1. B - 文章主要讨论可再生能源存储技术的突破
2. D - 90%
3. B - 使用丰富的钠降低成本
4. B - 将不同技术与应用匹配的组合法
5. B - 100%可再生电力

---

### 翻译

**文章 96. 可再生能源存储突破**

能源存储代表可再生能源转型中关键的缺失部分,因为太阳能和风能间歇性地发电——在阳光照射和风吹时,而不一定是在需求高峰时。电池技术、抽水蓄能和热能系统的最近突破正在应对这一挑战,使电网能够存储多余的可再生能源并在需要时调度它,将可变来源转变为可靠的电力供应。

锂离子电池主导当前存储应用,为从智能手机到电网规模安装的一切提供动力。然而,它们的局限性——成本、能量密度、充电速度和对钴等稀缺材料的依赖——推动了对替代品的研究。固态电池用固体材料替代液体电解质,提供更高的能量密度、更快的充电和更高的安全性。钠离子电池使用丰富、廉价的钠代替锂,可能降低成本30-50%。液流电池将能量存储在外部容器中的液体电解质中,便于长期存储的扩展。这些创新在未来十年可能再降低存储成本50-70%。

抽水蓄能是最成熟的技术,占全球存储容量的90%。它通过在电力充足时上坡抽水并在需求高峰时通过涡轮机释放来工作。然而,合适的地理条件限制了扩展。压缩空气储能使用多余电力压缩空气在地下洞穴中,在需要时释放以发电。热能系统加热或冷却熔盐或冰等材料,将能量存储供以后用于加热、冷却或发电。

研究表明,没有单一存储技术将主导;相反,将不同技术与不同应用匹配的组合方法将优化电网性能。短期存储(4-8小时)处理每日循环,而长期存储(数天到数周)解决季节性变化。车辆到电网技术允许电动汽车电池在高峰需求期间向电网供电,创造分布式存储网络。包括投资税收抵免、绩效标准和电网现代化资金在内的政策支持加速部署。使用当前和新兴的存储技术,过渡到100%可再生电力在技术和经济上都是可行的,只需要持续投资和支持性监管框架来释放其全部潜力。

**题目翻译:**

1. 文章主要讨论什么?
   A) 电池发展的历史
   B) 可再生能源存储技术的突破
   C) 增加化石燃料使用的方法
   D) 可再生能源的下降

2. 抽水蓄能占全球存储容量的多少比例?
   A) 70%
   B) 95%
   C) 50%
   D) 90%

3. 钠离子电池比锂离子电池有什么优势?
   A) 更高的能量密度
   B) 使用丰富的钠降低成本
   C) 仅更快的充电
   D) 没有优势

4. 什么方法将优化电网存储性能?
   A) 依赖单一技术
   B) 将不同技术与应用匹配的组合方法
   C) 仅使用锂离子电池
   D) 移除所有存储系统

5. 使用当前存储技术什么是技术和经济上可行的?
   A) 50%可再生电力
   B) 100%可再生电力
   C) 75%可再生电力
   D) 没有可再生电力

## 文章 97. Urban Transportation Revolution

Cities worldwide are reimagining transportation systems to address congestion, pollution, and inequity, shifting from car-centric planning to multimodal networks that prioritize people over vehicles. The transformation includes expanded public transit, protected bicycle infrastructure, pedestrian zones, ride-sharing services, and emerging technologies like electric scooters and autonomous vehicles. This revolution responds to the reality that transportation accounts for nearly 30% of urban greenhouse gas emissions, while car dependency creates sprawl, isolates communities, and claims 1.3 million lives annually in traffic accidents.

Public transit forms the backbone of sustainable urban mobility. Bus rapid transit systems, light rail, and subway networks move large numbers of people efficiently while occupying far less space than private vehicles. Cities like Bogotá, Singapore, and Copenhagen demonstrate that high-quality transit reduces car dependence, improves air quality, and enhances quality of life. Integrated fare systems and real-time information make multimodal journeys seamless, encouraging ridership. The research shows that every dollar invested in public transit generates approximately four dollars in economic returns through reduced congestion, lower healthcare costs, and increased property values.

Active transportation—walking and cycling—offers additional benefits. Protected bike lanes separated from motor traffic encourage ridership across age groups and skill levels. Paris plans to become a "15-minute city" where all essential services are within a 15-minute walk or bike ride. Amsterdam and Copenhagen already derive over 30% of trips from cycling, with corresponding health benefits including reduced obesity, heart disease, and mental health conditions. Pedestrianized streets and car-free zones create vibrant public spaces that support local businesses and community interaction.

Emerging technologies present both opportunities and challenges. Electric vehicles eliminate tailpipe（尾气）emissions but do not address congestion or space consumption. Autonomous vehicles could improve safety and mobility for elderly and disabled populations, but may increase vehicle miles traveled if not integrated with transit. Ride-hailing services reduce drunk driving but may compete with public transit. The research suggests that successful urban transportation futures require prioritizing investments in public transit and active transportation over private vehicle infrastructure, using technology to enhance rather than replace sustainable modes. Cities that embrace this approach will become more livable（宜居的）, equitable（公平的）, and environmentally sustainable.

**Questions:**

1. What is the passage mainly about?
   A) The benefits of car dependency
   B) The history of automobile manufacturing
   C) The revolution in urban transportation systems
   D) Methods for increasing traffic congestion

2. What percentage of urban greenhouse gas emissions does transportation account for?
   A) Nearly 30%
   B) 50%
   C) 10%
   D) 20%

3. How much economic return does every dollar invested in public transit generate?
   A) Five dollars
   B) Three dollars
   C) Four dollars
   D) Two dollars

4. What percentage of trips do Amsterdam and Copenhagen derive from cycling?
   A) 10%
   B) Over 30%
   C) 50%
   D) 20%

5. What approach creates more livable cities?
   A) Removing all transit systems
   B) Prioritizing private vehicles
   C) Only building more roads
   D) Prioritizing public transit and active transportation

**Answers:**
1. C - 文章主要讲述城市交通系统的革命
2. A - 近30%
3. C - 四美元
4. B - 超过30%
5. D - 优先考虑公共交通和主动交通

---

### 翻译

**文章 97. 城市交通革命**

世界各地的城市正在重新构想交通系统,以解决拥堵、污染和不公平,从以汽车为中心的计划转向优先考虑人而非车辆的多模式网络。转型包括扩展公共交通、受保护的自行车基础设施、步行区、共享乘车服务以及电动滑板车和自动驾驶车辆等新兴技术。这场革命回应了一个现实:交通占城市温室气体排放的近30%,而对汽车的依赖造成蔓延、孤立社区,并每年在交通事故中夺走130万人的生命。

公共交通构成可持续城市出行的骨干。快速公交系统、轻轨和地铁网络高效地运送大量人员,同时占用比私家车少得多的空间。波哥大、新加坡和哥本哈根等城市展示了高质量的公共交通如何减少对汽车的依赖、改善空气质量并提高生活质量。综合票价系统和实时信息使多模式旅程无缝,鼓励乘坐。研究表明,每投资1美元于公共交通通过减少拥堵、降低医疗成本和增加财产价值产生约4美元的经济回报。

主动交通——步行和骑自行车——提供额外好处。与机动车交通隔离的受保护自行车道鼓励各年龄组和技能水平的人骑行。巴黎计划成为一个'15分钟城市',所有基本服务都在15分钟步行或自行车骑行范围内。阿姆斯特丹和哥本哈根已经超过30%的出行来自骑自行车,相应的健康好处包括减少肥胖、心脏病和心理健康状况。步行化街道和无车区创造充满活力的公共空间,支持当地企业和社区互动。

新兴技术既带来机遇也带来挑战。电动汽车消除尾气排放,但不解决拥堵或空间消耗。自动驾驶车辆可以改善老年人和残疾人的安全和流动性,但如果不与公共交通整合,可能增加车辆行驶里程。网约车服务减少酒后驾车,但可能与公共交通竞争。研究表明,成功的城市交通未来需要优先考虑公共交通和主动交通的投资,而不是私家车基础设施,使用技术增强而非替代可持续模式。拥抱这种方法的城市将变得更加宜居、公平和环境可持续。

**题目翻译:**

1. 文章主要讲什么?
   A) 汽车依赖的好处
   B) 汽车制造的历史
   C) 城市交通系统的革命
   D) 增加交通拥堵的方法

2. 交通占城市温室气体排放的多少比例?
   A) 近30%
   B) 50%
   C) 10%
   D) 20%

3. 每投资1美元于公共交通产生多少经济回报?
   A) 五美元
   B) 三美元
   C) 四美元
   D) 两美元

4. 阿姆斯特丹和哥本哈根超过多少比例的出行来自骑自行车?
   A) 10%
   B) 超过30%
   C) 50%
   D) 20%

5. 什么方法创造更宜居的城市?
   A) 移除所有交通系统
   B) 优先考虑私家车
   C) 仅建造更多道路
   D) 优先考虑公共交通和主动交通

## 文章 98. Soil Degradation Threatens Agriculture

Global soil resources are deteriorating（恶化）at an alarming rate, with approximately 33% of the world's soils moderately to highly degraded due to erosion, salinization（盐碱化）, compaction（压实）, acidification, and chemical contamination. This degradation threatens the ability to feed a growing population projected to reach 10 billion by 2050, as healthy soil is the foundation of agricultural productivity. Unlike other natural resources, soil forms extremely slowly—just one centimeter of topsoil（表土）requires 500 to 1,000 years to develop—making it effectively non-renewable on human timescales.

The causes are primarily agricultural mismanagement. Intensive tillage（耕作）breaks up soil structure, exposing it to erosion by wind and water. Monoculture farming depletes（耗尽）specific nutrients while promoting pest and disease cycles. Overgrazing（过度放牧）removes vegetation that protects soil surfaces. Excessive use of chemical fertilizers and pesticides kills beneficial soil organisms and disrupts nutrient cycling. Deforestation removes root systems that hold soil in place. Climate change exacerbates（加剧）these pressures through more intense rainfall events, stronger winds, and higher temperatures that accelerate decomposition and organic matter loss.

The consequences extend far beyond reduced crop yields. Degraded soils store less carbon, contributing to climate change. They absorb less water, increasing flood risk and reducing groundwater recharge（地下水补给）. Nutrient runoff from fertilized fields creates dead zones in coastal waters where aquatic life cannot survive. Dust storms from eroded fields affect air quality and human health across continents. The economic costs are staggering—soil degradation reduces global agricultural productivity by approximately 10%, costing over $40 billion annually in lost production and requiring increased fertilizer inputs that further degrade soils in a vicious（恶性的）cycle.

Regenerative agriculture offers pathways to restore soil health. No-till farming（免耕农业）minimizes soil disturbance, preserving structure and organic matter. Cover crops（覆盖作物）protect soil during fallow（休耕）periods while adding organic matter when incorporated. Crop rotation breaks pest cycles and diversifies nutrient demands. Agroforestry（农林复合）integrates trees with crops to stabilize soil and provide multiple benefits. Composting and reduced chemical inputs restore biological activity. The research demonstrates that regenerative practices can rebuild soil organic matter at rates of 1-3% annually, simultaneously improving productivity, water retention, carbon storage, and resilience to climate extremes. Transitioning to regenerative agriculture requires policy support, farmer education, and market incentives, but represents one of the most powerful solutions available for addressing multiple environmental challenges while securing food supplies for future generations.

**Questions:**

1. What does the passage mainly discuss?
   A) How soil degradation threatens global agriculture
   B) The history of chemical fertilizers
   C) The benefits of soil degradation
   D) Methods for increasing soil erosion

2. What percentage of the world's soils are moderately to highly degraded?
   A) 33%
   B) 20%
   C) 10%
   D) 50%

3. How long does it take to form one centimeter of topsoil?
   A) 500-1,000 years
   B) 10,000 years
   C) 10-50 years
   D) 100-200 years

4. What percentage of global agricultural productivity does soil degradation reduce?
   A) 10%
   B) 5%
   C) 30%
   D) 20%

5. What approach can restore soil health?
   A) Only removing all vegetation
   B) Intensive tillage
   C) Regenerative agriculture including no-till, cover crops, and crop rotation
   D) Only increasing chemical fertilizers

**Answers:**
1. A - 文章主要讨论土壤退化如何威胁全球农业
2. A - 33%
3. A - 500到1000年
4. A - 10%
5. C - 包括免耕、覆盖作物和轮作的再生农业

---

### 翻译

**文章 98. 土壤退化威胁农业**

全球土壤资源正以惊人的速度恶化,约33%的世界土壤中度至高度退化,原因是侵蚀、盐碱化、压实、酸化和化学污染。这种退化威胁着养活预计到2050年将达到100亿不断增长的人口的能力,因为健康土壤是农业生产力基础。与其他自然资源不同,土壤形成极其缓慢——仅一厘米表土需要500到1000年才能发育——使其在人类时间尺度上实际上是不可再生的。

原因主要是农业管理不善。集约化耕作破坏土壤结构,使其暴露于风蚀和水蚀。单一栽培耗尽特定营养物质,同时促进害虫和疾病循环。过度放牧移除保护土壤表面的植被。过度使用化肥和农药杀死有益的土壤生物并扰乱营养循环。森林砍伐移除固定土壤的根系。气候变化通过更强烈的降雨事件、更强的风和加速分解和有机质损失的更高温度加剧这些压力。

后果远远超出作物产量减少。退化的土壤储存更少的碳,促进气候变化。它们吸收更少的水,增加洪水风险并减少地下水补给。来自施肥田的营养物质径流在沿海水域创造水生生物无法生存的死亡区。来自侵蚀田地的沙尘暴影响跨大陆的空气质量。经济损失是惊人的——土壤退化使全球农业生产力降低约10%,每年造成超过400亿美元的生产损失,并需要进一步退化土壤的化肥投入增加,形成恶性循环。

再生农业提供了恢复土壤健康的途径。最小化土壤干扰的免耕农业保护结构和有机质。覆盖作物在休耕期保护土壤,同时在并入时增加有机质。轮作打破害虫循环并多样化营养需求。农林复合将树木与作物结合以稳定土壤并提供多种好处。堆肥和减少化学投入恢复生物活性。研究表明,再生实践可以以每年1-3%的速率重建土壤有机质,同时提高生产力、保水性、碳储存和对气候极端事件的恢复力。过渡到再生农业需要政策支持、农民教育和市场激励,但代表了解决多重环境挑战同时保障未来几代人食物供应的最有力解决方案之一。

**题目翻译:**

1. 文章主要讨论什么?
   A) 土壤退化如何威胁全球农业
   B) 化肥的历史
   C) 土壤退化的好处
   D) 增加土壤侵蚀的方法

2. 世界土壤有多少比例中度至高度退化?
   A) 33%
   B) 20%
   C) 10%
   D) 50%

3. 形成一厘米表土需要多长时间?
   A) 500到1000年
   B) 10000年
   C) 10到50年
   D) 100到200年

4. 土壤退化使全球农业生产力降低多少?
   A) 10%
   B) 5%
   C) 30%
   D) 20%

5. 什么方法可以恢复土壤健康?
   A) 仅移除所有植被
   B) 集约化耕作
   C) 包括免耕、覆盖作物和轮作的再生农业
   D) 仅增加化肥
