## 文章 99. Space Debris Threatens Orbital Operations

Space debris—defunct（报废的）satellites, spent rocket stages, and fragments from collisions—poses an escalating（不断升级的）threat to active spacecraft and the sustainability of orbital operations. Over 30,000 objects larger than 10 centimeters orbit Earth at velocities（速度）exceeding 28,000 kilometers per hour, where even a paint fleck（碎片）can damage a satellite. Millions of smaller particles, too tiny to track but traveling at hypersonic（高超音速的）speeds, create a hazardous（危险的）environment that endangers the International Space Station, communication satellites, weather monitors, and emerging commercial space ventures.

The problem stems from decades of unregulated space activity. Since Sputnik's launch in 1957, over 6,000 rocket launches have placed satellites in orbit, with many upper stages and payloads（有效载荷）remaining in space after mission completion. Collisions generate thousands of fragments—the 2009 collision between Iridium 33 and Cosmos 2251 created over 2,000 pieces of trackable debris. Anti-satellite weapon tests by several nations have deliberately（故意地）destroyed satellites, adding to the debris field. The Kessler Syndrome（凯斯勒综合征）, a theoretical cascade（级联）of collisions where each impact generates debris that causes further collisions, could render certain orbits unusable for generations.

Consequences extend beyond satellite loss. The International Space Station regularly performs avoidance maneuvers（规避机动）when debris approaches, consuming fuel that limits its operational lifetime. Communication, navigation, and weather satellites face constant risk, with potential failures affecting everything from GPS to disaster warning systems. Insurance costs for space operations have risen dramatically, making launches more expensive. The research indicates that without intervention（干预）, debris density in low Earth orbit will increase by 30-50% over the next decade, making certain altitude bands（高度带）too hazardous for reliable operations.

Solutions require international cooperation and technological innovation. Designing satellites for controlled deorbit（受控离轨）at mission end prevents accumulation（积累）. Active debris removal using nets, harpoons（鱼叉）, robotic arms, or laser systems could capture and deorbit large objects. Passivation（钝化处理）—venting remaining fuel and batteries from spent rocket stages—reduces explosion risk. Space traffic management systems track debris and coordinate maneuvers to prevent collisions. International guidelines established by the Inter-Agency Space Debris Coordination Committee provide frameworks, but enforcement（执行）remains voluntary. The research underscores that space debris is a global commons（全球公域）problem requiring binding international agreements and sustained investment to preserve orbital environments for future generations.

**Questions:**

1. What is the passage mainly about?
   A) The history of space stations
   B) Methods for increasing satellite launches
   C) How space debris threatens orbital operations
   D) The benefits of space exploration

2. How many objects larger than 10 centimeters orbit Earth?
   A) 10,000
   B) 100,000
   C) 1,000
   D) Over 30,000

3. What velocity do space debris objects travel at?
   A) 100,000 km/h
   B) 10,000 km/h
   C) 1,000 km/h
   D) Over 28,000 km/h

4. What could the Kessler Syndrome cause?
   A) Reduced debris
   B) Only minor damage
   C) A cascade of collisions rendering orbits unusable
   D) Improved satellite operations

5. What solution can prevent debris accumulation?
   A) Removing all satellites
   B) Designing satellites for controlled deorbit and active debris removal
   C) Only tracking debris
   D) Only increasing launches

**Answers:**
1. C - 文章主要讲述太空碎片如何威胁轨道运行
2. D - 超过30000个
3. D - 超过28000公里/小时
4. C - 导致轨道无法使用的碰撞级联
5. B - 设计卫星受控离轨和主动碎片清除

---

### 翻译

**文章 99. 太空碎片威胁轨道运行**

太空碎片——报废卫星、用完的火箭级和碰撞碎片——对活跃航天器和轨道运行的可持续性构成不断升级的威胁。超过30000个大于10厘米的物体以超过28000公里/小时的速度环绕地球,在这个速度下,即使一小片油漆碎片也能损坏卫星。数百万个太小而无法追踪但以高超音速行进的粒子创造了一个危险环境,危及国际空间站、通信卫星、天气监测器和新兴的商业太空事业。

这个问题源于数十年来不受监管的太空活动。自1957年斯普特尼克发射以来,超过6000次火箭发射将卫星送入轨道,许多上级和有效载荷在任务完成后留在太空中。碰撞产生数千个碎片——2009年铱星33号和宇宙2251号之间的碰撞创造了超过2000块可追踪碎片。几个国家的反卫星武器测试故意摧毁卫星,增加了碎片场。凯斯勒综合征,一种理论上的碰撞级联,每次撞击产生的碎片导致进一步碰撞,可能使某些轨道几代人都无法使用。

后果超越卫星损失。国际空间站在碎片接近时定期执行规避机动,消耗限制其运行寿命的燃料。通信、导航和天气卫星面临持续风险,潜在故障影响从GPS到灾害预警系统的一切。太空运行的保险成本急剧上升,使发射更加昂贵。研究表明,如果不干预,未来十年低地球轨道的碎片密度将增加30-50%,使某些高度带对可靠运行来说太危险。

解决方案需要国际合作和技术创新。设计卫星在任务结束时受控离轨可以防止积累。使用网、鱼叉、机械臂或激光系统的主动碎片清除可以捕获并使大物体离轨。钝化处理——排出用完火箭级的剩余燃料和电池——减少爆炸风险。太空交通管理系统追踪碎片并协调机动以防止碰撞。机构间太空碎片协调委员会建立的国际指南提供框架,但执行仍然是自愿的。研究强调,太空碎片是一个全球公域问题,需要有约束力的国际协议和持续投资来为子孙后代保护轨道环境。

**题目翻译:**

1. 文章主要讲什么?
   A) 空间站的历史
   B) 增加卫星发射的方法
   C) 太空碎片如何威胁轨道运行
   D) 太空探索的好处

2. 多少大于10厘米的物体环绕地球?
   A) 10000个
   B) 100000个
   C) 1000个
   D) 超过30000个

3. 太空碎片以什么速度行进?
   A) 100000公里/小时
   B) 10000公里/小时
   C) 1000公里/小时
   D) 超过28000公里/小时

4. 凯斯勒综合征可能导致什么?
   A) 减少碎片
   B) 仅轻微损害
   C) 导致轨道无法使用的碰撞级联
   D) 改善卫星运行

5. 什么解决方案可以防止碎片积累?
   A) 移除所有卫星
   B) 设计卫星受控离轨和主动碎片清除
   C) 仅追踪碎片
   D) 仅增加发射

## 文章 100. Biodiversity Conservation Success Stories

While biodiversity（生物多样性）loss continues globally, targeted（有针对性的）conservation efforts have achieved remarkable（显著的）successes, demonstrating that with sufficient commitment（投入）and resources, species recovery and ecosystem（生态系统）restoration（恢复）are possible. The recovery of the bald eagle（秃鹰）in North America, the giant panda in China, and the humpback whale（座头鲸）worldwide illustrate（说明）how coordinated（协调的）action combining legal protection, habitat restoration, and international cooperation can reverse（扭转）decline. These victories（胜利）provide hope and valuable lessons for addressing the broader biodiversity crisis（危机）.

The bald eagle, once reduced to just 417 nesting pairs in the lower 48 United States due to pesticide（杀虫剂）exposure（暴露）and habitat loss, now boasts（拥有）over 300,000 individuals following DDT bans, habitat protection, and reintroduction（重新引入）programs. The giant panda, removed from the endangered list in 2016, has seen its population increase to over 1,800 individuals through expanded nature reserves and bamboo corridor（走廊）restoration. Humpback whales, hunted to near extinction with populations down to 5,000 globally, have recovered to over 80,000 following international moratoriums（禁令）on commercial whaling（捕鲸）. Each success required decades of sustained（持续的）effort and adaptation（适应）to emerging（新兴的）challenges.

Key factors distinguish（区分）successful conservation（保护）programs. Clear, science-based recovery targets provide measurable goals. Strong legal frameworks（框架）with enforcement（执行）mechanisms（机制）protect species and habitats. Adequate funding ensures long-term commitment beyond political cycles. Community engagement（参与）creates local support and reduces conflict between conservation and livelihoods（生计）. International cooperation addresses migratory（迁徙的）species and transboundary（跨边界的）ecosystems. Adaptive management allows programs to respond to new information and changing conditions.

The research indicates that these successes, while encouraging, remain exceptions rather than the norm. Thousands of species continue declining without comparable intervention（干预）. Conservation must scale up dramatically to address the full scope of the biodiversity crisis. However, proven strategies exist—expanding protected areas, restoring degraded（退化的）habitats, reducing pollution, controlling invasive（入侵的）species, and addressing climate change. The challenge is not technical but political and economic, requiring societies to value biodiversity as essential infrastructure（基础设施）rather than optional luxury. The bald eagle, panda, and humpback whale demonstrate that recovery is possible when we choose to invest in life's preservation（保护）.

**Questions:**

1. What does the passage mainly discuss?
   A) The decline of all species
   B) Methods for increasing habitat destruction
   C) The history of hunting
   D) Success stories in biodiversity conservation

2. How many bald eagle nesting pairs existed in the lower 48 United States at their lowest point?
   A) 417
   B) 100
   C) 1,000
   D) 5,000

3. What is the current global humpback whale population?
   A) 100,000
   B) 5,000
   C) Over 80,000
   D) 20,000

4. What factors distinguish successful conservation programs?
   A) Only hunting
   B) Clear targets, legal frameworks, funding, community engagement, and international cooperation
   C) Only habitat destruction
   D) No planning or funding

5. What challenge remains for conservation?
   A) Scaling up to address the full scope of the biodiversity crisis
   B) Only removing all protections
   C) No challenges exist
   D) Only reducing funding

**Answers:**
1. D - 文章主要讨论生物多样性保护的成功案例
2. A - 417对
3. C - 超过80000头
4. B - 明确目标、法律框架、资金、社区参与和国际合作
5. A - 扩大规模以应对生物多样性危机的全部范围

---

### 翻译

**文章 100. 生物多样性保护成功案例**

虽然生物多样性丧失在全球继续,有针对性的保护努力取得了显著的成功,表明只要有足够的投入和资源,物种恢复和生态系统恢复是可能的。北美秃鹰的恢复、中国的大熊猫和全球的座头鲸说明了结合法律保护、栖息地恢复和国际合作的协调行动如何扭转下降。这些胜利为解决更广泛的生物多样性危机提供了希望和宝贵经验。

秃鹰曾因农药暴露和栖息地丧失在美国本土48州减少到仅417对筑巢配对,在DDT禁令、栖息地保护和重新引入计划后,现在拥有超过300000只个体。2016年从濒危名单中移除的大熊猫,通过扩大自然保护区和竹子走廊恢复,种群已增加到超过1800只。座头鲸被猎杀到濒临灭绝,全球种群降至5000只,在国际禁止商业捕鲸后已恢复到超过80000只。每次成功都需要数十年的持续努力和适应新出现的挑战。

关键因素区分成功的保护项目。明确的、基于科学的恢复目标提供可衡量的目标。具有执行机制的强有力法律框架保护物种和栖息地。充足的资金确保超越政治周期的长期承诺。社区参与创造当地支持并减少保护与生计之间的冲突。国际合作解决迁徙物种和跨边界生态系统。适应性管理允许项目响应新信息和变化的条件。

研究表明,这些成功虽然令人鼓舞,但仍然是例外而非规范。数千种物种继续下降,没有可比的干预。保护必须大幅扩大规模以应对生物多样性危机的全部范围。然而,经过验证的策略存在——扩大保护区、恢复退化栖息地、减少污染、控制入侵物种和解决气候变化。挑战不是技术性的,而是政治和经济性的,需要社会将生物多样性视为必要基础设施而非可选奢侈品。秃鹰、熊猫和座头鲸表明,当我们选择投资于生命保护时,恢复是可能的。

**题目翻译:**

1. 文章主要讨论什么?
   A) 所有物种的下降
   B) 增加栖息地破坏的方法
   C) 狩猎的历史
   D) 生物多样性保护的成功案例

2. 在美国本土48州最低点时有多少秃鹰筑巢配对?
   A) 417对
   B) 100对
   C) 1000对
   D) 5000对

3. 当前全球座头鲸种群是多少?
   A) 100000头
   B) 5000头
   C) 超过80000头
   D) 20000头

4. 什么因素区分成功的保护项目?
   A) 仅狩猎
   B) 明确目标、法律框架、资金、社区参与和国际合作
   C) 仅栖息地破坏
   D) 没有规划或资金

5. 保护面临什么挑战?
   A) 扩大规模以应对生物多样性危机的全部范围
   B) 仅移除所有保护
   C) 没有挑战
   D) 仅减少资金
